# DOG.ai (Dynamic Output Generator) — Build Plan

- **Next:** **M22 step 1: Core and storage** for joined clips (Fable 5.2, high; no screen). **M22 is planned** (2026-11-18, with the owner; section "M22: Joined clips, and the helper's animals" below): four steps: 1 Core and storage, 2 Make and Kept videos, 3 the helper's animals (a clip trial), 4 Finish. **Decided by the owner:** a joined clip is a new clip beside the others, never in place; at most three clips, 15 seconds; Make one like this on a joined clip puts back its last part and says so; Save as a file writes the joined file only; the helper's "anthropomorphic" fix is a step of its own. Ten defaults in that section stand unless the owner changes one (joining is a button on the Make tab and only for a continuation made in this run; the repeated frame is left out; same size only; one new column on kept clips; the projector for the helper's trial is asked for by name and size at step 3). Nothing was built, run or downloaded in the planning session. The four video files are kept in `D:\dogai-b\models\video` (owner's yes, 2026-11-16).
- **M21 step 6, Finish (2026-11-18). Done. M21 is finished.**
  - *What is there now.* (1) **DOG.ai's own memory while clips are made is explained and lower** (below). (2) **The status bar, the model button and the helpers say what the chat model made room for**: "set aside for a video, back with your next message" after a clip, "for pictures" after a picture (they said "pictures" for both). (3) **A story picture's Animate… works in a real story**: a passage written by the chat model, illustrated, the picture's large view, Animate…: the Video page opened with "From a story picture · Wide · 960 × 640" and the clip was made from it in 1 min 42 s (the estimate said 1 min 48 s). (4) **The fast decoder was fetched once through the app from GitHub** (the owner's yes): with the other three files present, Get models › Video offered "Download the missing file", fetched `taew2_2.safetensors` (22,848,048 bytes) and marked the model installed; its SHA-256 is the pinned one and the trial file's. (5) Five things the review found are fixed, and one the looks pass found (below).
  - *The memory rise, measured* (Windows' count of DOG.ai's own program, ten plain Quick clips in a row, logged every two seconds; a new check-only switch, `DOGAI_TEST_MEMORY_LOG`, writes the numbers). **Before:** from 0.21 GB to **1.53 GB** after the tenth clip (0.63 GB on average). Split at the end: one full collection took it to 1.12 GB (0.42 GB was copies nobody used any more, not yet collected); asking the .NET runtime to give memory back took it to 0.39 GB (**0.72 GB was memory it had already freed and was keeping**); 0.30 GB was in use, most of it a pool of buffers that keeps what each thread hands back (the way M20 step 6 read the engine's answers). **Changed:** the engine's answers are read into one buffer per clip, which goes when the clip is done; and DOG.ai asks the runtime to give its unused memory back before and after every clip, and again three seconds after the page has shown the finished clip (showing a clip passes it to the page twice, which alone left half a gigabyte behind until the next clip). **After the first two changes**, the same ten clips: 0.31 GB on average, 0.28 GB between clips, 0.95 GB at the end until asked. **On the final build** (with the third change): five clips in a row, three of them at Better (the run for the unchecked items, below): DOG.ai stayed between 0.23 and 0.29 GB throughout (0.26 GB on average), touched 0.46 GB for a moment as the last clip arrived, and stood at 0.28 GB at the end. A third run of ten plain clips was not made on the final build. Each give-back stops DOG.ai for a fraction of a second (8 to 26 milliseconds in the eleven that were logged), only between clips. Clips took the same time (118 to 126 s each, before and after).
  - *Leak check with clips, plain and locked* (one run each: a made-up word per thing; pieces of every clip, frame, still and preview searched as bytes and as base64): a Faster clip not kept; a clip continued from its last frame, not kept; a second last frame held and let go; the helper's words and both its sentences (from words and from a file's frame); the JPEG the helper was sent (taken from the recording run: the same file gave the same frame byte for byte); a clip kept; a clip cancelled at step 13 of 20; **and, new, a 3 MB document indexed and a sentence dictated with the key that works in every program while the first clip was made** (both on the processor: the Video page said so, and the card did not change). **Plain:** the database holds the kept clip (its sentence, video, still and first frame) and the document, and nothing else holds anything: not the logs, the browser folder, the models folder (no new file), the engine folder (86 files, none changed) or TEMP. **Locked:** nothing anywhere. The dictated sentence was typed where it belonged, exactly, in the checks without a document (10 s after the key was let go); with a document being indexed at the same time it came later than the script waited the first time, and in the locked run it was typed while the script's window was closing. In the locked run the first clip's file was not saved by the script, so that clip was searched by its still and last frame only (the second, also Faster and not kept, by its file).
  - *Every request recorded once* (stand-ins for both engines that pass everything through and write it down; not in the repo): **a plain clip's request is byte for byte M20's** (681 bytes, compared with the text written out) and its program was started with M20's switches; `--sage-attn`, `cache_mode` "dbcache" and `cache_option` "Fn=8,Bn=0,threshold=0.08,warmup=8" were sent **only** for the two Faster clips; **no request holds `end_image`**, an address, a path or a `data:`; the continued clip's first frame is exactly the last frame the page held; the picture drawn in Images sent no input picture. The helper's request from words has no picture; from a frame it has **one** part, `data:image/jpeg;base64,` (a 133 KB JPEG), no thinking, 800 tokens at most.
  - *Upgrade from M20's build, in the app.* M20's build (`88ff82c`) made and kept two clips on a fresh plain folder (one from a file's first frame). The folder was then locked before this build's first start, so the lock screen encrypted it and the upgrade ran on the locked database: both clips listed and played; **Make one like this on the clip from a first frame sent the very 250,531 bytes M20's build had sent**, with the same start switches, and made the clip in 2 min 0 s; Continue the clip on the other kept clip held its last frame. Backed up (a locked backup, 10 MB), one kept clip removed, the backup restored with its password: both clips back, both play, Make one like this puts the settings back. The script lost the app at "Restart and restore" (the app had restored and was waiting at its lock screen, as its log says); the rest was checked in a fresh start on the same folder.
  - *The published portable build* (a copy on D:, its own models folder of hard links): a Faster clip in 1 min 30 s, kept, Continue the clip, Kept videos plays it.
  - *Review (`/code-review high` over steps 2 to 5), five things fixed:* a request to the document search model that found its program gone could stop a program another request had just started again on the processor; leaving the card for a clip could be kept waiting by batch after batch of indexing; a listener at the door that failed left the door closed and the card counted as busy for the rest of the run; Continue the clip did not look again after reading the last frame, so a Draw pressed in that moment had its frame and settings changed under it; reading a last frame could wait for ever on a clip of one frame. Three were left as they are and said so (the "can the model see" rule exists three times; a refusal wording only test doubles can reach; the door closes when the clip is asked for, not when it has its turn).
  - *Looked at:* every screen M21 changed (Make with a try and its buttons; a continued frame held; Write it for me from that frame with its sentence; the page while a clip is made, with the line about the documents; the Documents screen saying the search model is on the processor; the page afterwards with the status bar; Kept videos and a kept clip's view; Get models › Video) in all three looks, dark and light, at 1,400 px (54 screens) and at 700 px with DOG.ai told its card has 12 GB (60 screens, with the side panel's drawer open on the smaller card's note). **One thing was wrong at 700 px and is fixed:** while a clip was made, the smaller card's longer status line did not wrap and pushed the sentence box and Cancel past the window's edge; the line wraps now and the box stays inside (seen again in three looks, measured by script). Everything else fits.

A General reply afterwards came 28 s after it was sent (the chat model loads again first). The clips were not read frame by frame in this step; step 3's and the trial's sheets stand for how continuations look.
  - *Found and not changed (for the owner):* **the video helper overdoes "anthropomorphic".** Counted in the app with Huihui (five answers per idea, twice): from words, a fox, a horse, a dog and an eagle came back as "an anthropomorphic grey …" in 19 of 40 answers (the form's own example, colour and all), and the horse was given a rider, a saddle or a bridle in 7 of 10; "a cat girl with white hair" became "an anthropomorphic grey girl" in 10 of 10; from a frame of a clip of an ordinary wolf, 12 of 12. Places and people were never touched, and the wolf in a jacket was called anthropomorphic 8 of 10, as meant. Step 4's trial had no plain animal among its six ideas. The forms were chosen by a clip trial and accepted, so they are as they were; a fix is one rule reworded and a short trial with plain animals in it. Also seen: a clip started straight after another while documents are being indexed took 1 min 46 s where the estimate said 1 min 19 s (twice): the search model had gone back onto the card and had to leave again.
  - *Tests:* 1,583 pass (3 new: the answer buffer; no shared pool in the engine's reader and the give-back around every clip; the words for a video and for pictures, and no screen wording them by hand; and the door test now has a listener that fails). The real search model's test (`CardGuestSmokeTests`, the installed CUDA engine) passes on the changed leaving code.

The guests while a clip is made *by a plan* with the real card filled (in the looks pass DOG.ai was told the card has 12 GB and documents were indexed on the processor meanwhile: the clip took 1 min 48 s, as said). - **M21 step 5, the card: guests and smaller cards (2026-11-17). Done.**
  - *What is there now.* (1) **While a clip is made, nothing else of DOG.ai's is on the graphics card.** The document search model and the speech model ask at a door before they use the card; the door is closed from the moment a clip is asked for until it has ended. Meanwhile each runs on the processor, at once: documents go on being indexed and searched, and what is dictated is written down. A guest that is in the middle of something when the clip is asked for (a batch of passages, a sentence) finishes that, leaves the card and carries on on the processor. After the clip each goes back onto the card by itself the next time it is used. The owner is told in words on the Video page, the one screen that is open while a clip is made (leaving it stops the clip): "Documents are being indexed on the processor while the clip is made, which is slower. Indexing goes back to the graphics card by itself when the clip is done." and "A video is being made, so what you say is written down on the processor meanwhile: that takes about as long as you spoke." The Documents screen and the chat box say the same if they are seen in the seconds a stopped clip takes to end. Pictures, chat models and Unload everything treat a guest as before. (2) **Cards from 8 GB make Quick clips.** A card under 24 GB never has room for the whole model, so the engine is given a plan and keeps the model itself in the PC's memory. A Quick clip is made when 7 GB of the card is free (within a quarter of a gigabyte) and 12 GB of RAM is free; with less of either it is refused in words before the engine is started. Better is as before: 16 GB free. Get models › Video offers the model from 8 GB, and on cards up to 16 GB the offer and the Video page say that it is Quick only, how it was measured, that about 12 GB of free RAM is needed, and that DOG.ai was not tried on a real card of that size. **A 24 GB card with nothing else on it is started and judged exactly as in M20** (the same need, no plan, previews, no RAM check; the tests that pin M20's notes and start switches pass unchanged).
  - *Guests, measured first (default 6: wait for the clip, or the processor).* The speech model (large-v3 turbo): a 7-second sentence takes 0.3 s on the card and **5.0 s on the processor**, the same while a clip is made, with nothing of the card taken. The search model (Qwen3 Embedding 0.6B): 100 passages a second on the card; on the processor 14 a second on an idle PC and **4 to 9 a second while a clip is made**; a question takes under a tenth of a second either way. Waiting would mean 2 to 7½ minutes. **Both run on the processor.** Two things found on the way: started with no layers on the card the search model's program still took 454 MiB of it (128 MiB with the Vulkan engine), so it is also started with `--device none` (then none, on both engines); and **a search model that was indexing on the card when the clip started, and stayed there as M20 had it, made the clip take 3 min 40 s where the estimate said 2 min 26 s**, with free RAM down to 1.9 GB. Stepping off instead: 2 min 18 s.
  - *Guests in the app* (Debug build, a throwaway folder of hard links, the card sampled once a second throughout). **A 13 MB document added and Draw pressed 1.5 s later:** the search model started on the processor and held 0 MiB of the card for the whole clip (6 passages a second), the Video page said so from the first second, and 3 s after the clip it was on the card again (2.0 GB, 108 passages a second). The clip took 2 min 31 s (estimate 2 min 26 s). **The key that works in every program held for 8.5 s during a clip:** the Video page showed the line, DOG.ai's own share of the card stayed at 27 MiB (on the card the speech model holds 0.6 GB), and the exact sentence was typed 10 s after the key was let go (0.6 s without a clip); the clip took 2 min 28 s. **The search model indexing on the card when Draw was pressed:** it left the card, the clip got the whole card (22.7 GB free, no plan) and took 2 min 18 s, indexing went on at 8 a second and was back on the card at 105 a second afterwards.
  - *Smaller cards, the engine alone* (DOG.ai's own `VideoServer` in a scratch program; another program, `llama-server` with a chat model, started anew for every clip and sized to leave the amount free; the same Quick clip, 960 × 640, same seed, no previews; the card, each program's own and shared memory, and free RAM sampled once a second):

    | Free on the card | Plan | Whole clip | The engine's own | The card's peak | The other program | The engine's RAM | Free RAM at its lowest |
    |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
    | 22.7 GB (nothing else) | none | 139 s | 17.7 GB | 18.6 GB | none | 1.6 GB | 16.2 GB |
    | 14.9 GB | 10.0 | 143 s | 12.9 GB | 21.6 GB | 7.8 GB, untouched | 11.0 GB | 5.7 GB |
    | 11.1 GB | 8.1 | 144 s | 10.8 GB | 23.2 GB | 11.6 GB, untouched | 10.9 GB | 6.2 GB |
    | 9.1 GB | 6.1 | 145 s | 8.9 GB | 23.4 GB | 13.5 GB, untouched | 11.0 GB | 5.5 GB |
    | 7.2 GB | 4.1 | 146 s | 6.8 GB | 23.2 GB | 15.5 GB, untouched | 11.0 GB | 5.5 GB |
    | 6.8 GB | 3.8 | 142 s | 6.5 GB | 23.2 GB | 15.8 GB, untouched | 11.1 GB | 6.3 GB |
    | 6.2 GB | 3.2 | **171 s** | 5.9 GB | 23.3 GB | 16.5 GB, untouched | 11.0 GB | 5.8 GB |
    | 5.1 GB | 2.0 | **nothing made** | | | | | |

    With Faster: 107 s at 7.2 GB free and 106 s at 6.8 GB; at 6.1 GB (a plan of 3.0) nothing was made. The engine takes its plan and 2.7 to 2.8 GB at every size. The other program's own memory never fell and its shared memory never rose, so nothing overflowed (the engine's "shared" 9.5 GB is the model it keeps in RAM). At 6.2 GB every step ran short of its working memory, by the engine's own warning.
  - *The RAM check, measured (the trial's correction).* With a plan the engine keeps the model itself (9.5 GB) in RAM: **11.0 GB of its own, and free RAM falls by 11 GB.** It decides by the free RAM it finds, less 2 GB. With a second program pinning RAM so that **12.5 GB was free, the clip took its usual 144 s and 1.7 GB was left; with 9.8 GB free it took 401 s** (the engine then kept the text encoder in RAM and put the model on a card that had no room for it). With 17 GB or more free it keeps the text encoder in RAM as well: 16.6 GB of its own, at normal speed, with about 1 GB of RAM left. That is what step 1 saw. So the check is **12 GB of free RAM for any clip made by a plan**, also on a 24 GB card that another program holds part of. A Better clip by a plan (17 GB free, a plan of 13): 7 min 16 s, 9.6 GB of RAM.
  - *Smaller cards in the app* (the same holder; DOG.ai's own window is on the card too):

    | Free on the card | Plan | Clip | The estimate said |
    |---|---|---|---|
    | 14.9 GB | 10.0 | 2 min 26 s | 2 min 26 s |
    | 11.1 GB | 8.0 | 2 min 26 s | 2 min 26 s |
    | 9.1 GB | 6.1 | 2 min 28 s | 2 min 26 s |
    | 7.0 GB, DOG.ai told the card is 8 GB | 3.9 | 2 min 28 s | 2 min 26 s |
    | 7.0 GB, the same, with Faster | 3.9 | 1 min 49 s | 1 min 48 s |

    The card peaked at 21.5 to 23.4 GB, the other program untouched, the engine's RAM 10.9 to 11.1 GB, free RAM never under 4.8 GB. **Refused in words:** with 6.4 GB free, "A video needs about 7.0 GB of graphics memory and only 6.4 GB is free after DOG.ai made room. Close other programs that use the graphics card, then try again."; with 10.0 GB of RAM free, "With part of the graphics card in use the video model is kept in the PC's own memory while a clip is made. That takes about 12.0 GB of free memory (RAM), and only 10.0 GB is free. Close other programs, then try again." Nothing was started either time. **The whole card, on the final build:** 22.6 GB free, no plan, previews, 1 min 38 s with Faster (the estimate said 1 min 48 s).
  - *Looked at:* Get models › Video with the smaller card's offer, the Video page with the note in the Quality fold, the Video page while a clip is made with the line about the documents, and the Documents screen saying the search model is on the processor, in all three looks, dark and light, at 1,400 px; the first two also at 700 px (the note inside the drawer). 36 screens. Nothing is past the window's edge (measured by script).
  - *Tests:* 1,580 pass (35 new, in `CardGuestsAndSizesTests.cs`: the door; no guest onto the card while a clip is made and each back afterwards; a guest on its way in let in first; a guest in use steps off, is never cut off, and the wait can be cancelled; the door open again after a failed, cancelled or refused clip; pictures, chat models and Unload everything as before; **every card size from 8 to 24 GB** through the rule and through the coordinator (the plan each gets, Quick made, Better refused before anything moves on cards up to 16 GB, the offer's words); a 24 GB card exactly as in M20; under 8 GB not offered; too little of the card and too little RAM refused in words; the search model's two start lists pinned). Four older tests that pinned the 16 GB limit now pin the new one. **Real engines** (`CardGuestSmokeTests`, behind `DOGAI_TEST_VOICE_DIR` and `DOGAI_TEST_SEARCH_MODEL`): the speech model writes the sentence down on the processor with nothing more of the card taken, and is back on the card with the door open; the search model starts on the processor holding nothing of the card, gives the same vectors as on the card, steps off in the middle of indexing without losing a batch, on the installed CUDA engine and the winget Vulkan one.
  - *Decided here (say so to change one).* **A guest in use steps off the card for a clip** (M20 let it stay and planned the clip around it; measured above). The smallest card is 8 GB, and an 8 GB card on which Windows and other programs hold more than about 1.2 GB is refused at the clip, in words: the engine's limit is where it is. The RAM check also applies to a 24 GB card with another program on it. Cards of 16 GB get the same words as smaller ones (Quick only, by a plan, not tried for real). For checks, `DOGAI_TEST_CARD_MIB` makes DOG.ai take its card for a smaller one (as `DOGAI_TEST_MIC` replaces the microphone).

The first dictation check typed into Notepad's restored unsaved note ("Hello!"); it was put back to what it held, and the check now uses a text box of its own. `tools/walkthrough.ps1`'s `globalkey` step, which used Notepad too, now does the same and prints what was typed.

A PC with less than 32 GB of RAM (the low-RAM runs pinned RAM with a second program). Better on cards of 17 to 23 GB (unchanged since M20; one clip by hand). The chat box's line while dictating (it can only show in the two or three seconds a stopped clip takes to end). The guests while a clip is made *by a plan* in the app (the engine alone: 157 s for 144). The estimate is not changed for a planned clip. From earlier steps, still open: Faster at Better's tall and square sizes, a plain continuation at Better, a third continuation in the app, the upgraded folder locked, backed up and restored in the app, the helper with chat models other than Huihui and at Better, the helper's request recorded (step 6).
- **M21 step 4, Write it for me for video (2026-11-17). Done.**
  - *What is there now.* A button **Write it for me…** beside Draw on the Video section's Make tab (off while a clip is made). It opens a dialog: **From words** (a few words of the owner's) or **From the first frame** (the frame held; the box then says what should happen, optional). It starts on the first frame whenever one is held. The chat model in memory writes one to three English sentences: what is seen, what moves, how the camera moves. If none is in memory, the General default is loaded, never swapped for another. The sentence is shown with the three parts it was written from (Seen · Moves · Camera), and **Use this sentence** replaces the sentence box (there is one box) and closes the dialog ("The sentence is in the box: change it there if you like, then press Draw."). There is **Stop**, and closing the dialog stops it too. A model that cannot see says so before anything is sent (the model in memory by the engine's word, else the General default by its file); from words it writes all the same. The words, the choice and the sentence are held in memory only, and a sentence written from one frame is let go when another frame is held. Each sentence takes 1.5 to 3 s once the model is in memory.
  - *How the wording was chosen* (Huihui Qwen3.5 9B Q8 with its picture input, downloaded again with the owner's yes, 918,166,016 bytes, checksum as published, deleted at the end; the installed CUDA engine). Six ideas: a lighthouse in a storm, a woman in a red coat on a snowy street, an anthro fox playing a lute in a tavern (from words), and a mountain lake, a girl in a wheat field, an anthro wolf at a campfire (from a first frame drawn in Images with novaFurryXL at 960 × 640, with the owner's words for the motion). Every clip was **Quick with Faster** (both halves of each pair the same, Wide, the same seed), made through DOG.ai's own `VideoServer` by a scratch program, 65 to 97 s each, and read as sheets of six frames. The helper's **first** answer was taken each time, never the best of several. The criteria were written before the first look: the asked motion happens, the subject stays itself, the picture is clean, the camera is sensible; a pass needs 0 worse and 4 of 6 better.
    - **Round 1:** 3 better, 1 same, **2 worse**. The fox's sentence dropped the lute and said "dimly lit… amber light", so the clip was all red with no instrument. "Under a dim streetlamp" with "her feet step forward" made a dark clip with a tiny, still figure.
    - **Round 2** (keep everything the words name, keep their verbs, a person fills the frame, never "dim" or "dark"): 4 better, 1 same, **1 worse**. "The camera pushes in from the front" swung the view round to the wolf's face, and he became another wolf. The model also invented a lighthouse keeper, who walked in mid-clip, and a coffee cup.
    - **Rounds 3 to 5** (no person or thing the words don't name; directions by what is there, never left or right; no "from the front"; buildings and rocks stay still): sentences only, until no answer invented anything. The model still wrote "a slow push in from the front" now and then, so the check takes "from the front/left/right/side" off a push-in in code.
    - **Round 5 clips:** 3 better, 2 same, **1 worse**: a push-in "toward his face" from the first frame zoomed onto the wolf's face, which blurred and changed.
    - **Round 6** (from a first frame the camera stays still unless asked): 2 better, 4 same, 0 worse.
    - **Held out** (new seeds, all twelve clips made again, the wording untouched): 2 to 3 better, 3 to 4 same, **0 worse**. A real bug showed there: the model wrote five sentences and the check kept the first three, which held only what is seen. The check now drops sentences from the middle, never the first or the last.
    - **Asked, the owner chose one more round. Round 7** (frame form only: a person's own motion small and slow, and the rest of the picture alive: clouds, wheat, flames and sparks), on both seed sets: **3 better, 3 same, 0 worse on each.**

The two faster caches the engine also has (easycache, spectrum: 37 to 48% faster) are visibly softer and are not used. **The owner can still say no before step 2 builds it** (it is at the bar's edge at Better). (2) **An end frame and loops: not built.** The engine accepts an end picture for the installed model without a word, at no cost in time or memory, but in all twelve clips (three seeds of a first and an end picture, three seeds of a loop, a place, a person, both decoders) **the last three frames are a washed-out grey version of the picture**, the end picture arrives as a cut in the last second and not as motion, and a loop stands nearly still for five seconds and then flashes grey. As settled: no other model, no download; **the owner is asked again** (a model that takes an end frame is one of the 14B ones). (3) **Continue the clip: built as planned.** A continuation starts exactly on the last frame of the clip before and takes the time of any clip. It holds for one continuation, is usable for two, and **by the third the picture is clearly softer (a third less fine detail) and the character has become another animal in the same jacket**; the page will say so in a line. (4) **Joining two clips into one file is possible without an encoder** (every frame of the engine's files stands by itself): a script of 60 lines made a 10-second file of 241 frames that plays and seeks in a page. Reported only; built only on the owner's yes, and not in M21's steps.

Seven pictures drawn in DOG.ai's Images section first on a throwaway data folder (novaFurryXL, Small: a mountain lake, a girl in a wheat field, an anthro wolf at a campfire, a second picture of each scene with the same seed, and a tall one). Clips were looked at as sheets of frames made by a headless browser page that reads a clip the way `video.js` does, and compared by numbers from the same page (how far a frame is from a picture, how sharp it is). The scripts are not in the repo.

- **M20 step 6, Finish (2026-11-17). Done, and with it M20.**
  - *What changed for the owner.* (1) **A clip made while another program uses the graphics card no longer crawls.** The picture engine does not see what other programs hold on the card: with a program holding 6.8 GB it believed 23.0 GB were free, put the whole model on the card, filled it to 23.9 GB, borrowed 2.8 GB of Windows' shared memory, and a short clip that takes 58 seconds took 222. Now, whenever less than 22 GB is free after DOG.ai cleared the card, DOG.ai tells the engine how much it may plan with (10 GB for Quick, 13 GB for Better) and asks for no previews; the page then says "Part of the graphics card is in use, so nothing of the clip shows until it is done, and it may take longer than was said." In the app beside such a program: **Quick 2 min 23 s (the estimate said 2 min 26 s); Better 7 min 33 s (7 min 19 s).** With the whole card free everything is as before (the engine is started exactly as in steps 2 to 5). With too little free the clip is refused in words, as before ("…only 13.7 GB is free after DOG.ai made room. Close other programs that use the graphics card, then try again."). (2) **Keep pressed in the first moment after a clip appears** marks the clip kept (it could stay "Keep", and a second press wrote the same clip again). (3) **After Cancel, or a clip the card refused, the clip shown before plays on** (it stood still on one frame). (4) **Playhouse's top bar keeps Search and Settings in view between 760 and about 1,050 px**: with Video as its seventh place the bar pushed them off the edge there; it now shows icons only below 1,100 px, as it already did below 760 px. (5) A clip straight after a clip can no longer be refused, or made the slow way, because the card had not yet given back the last clip's memory (the card is looked at a few more times first).
  - *The review* (`/code-review high` over `370df60..HEAD`, all of M20's code): nine findings. **Fixed:** a try is marked kept, or given its still, by its number (the cause of (2): Keep held the record from before the still arrived); the card is read again before a low reading is believed ((5)); the player is not refilled while it is being let go (a clip's memory could stay held until the Video page was left); (3); a clip's still is made once, not twice; the preview the engine repeats in every answer is not decoded again; the video folder's own four files are compared as full paths (a folder written with "/" or ".." listed them a second time under Other files); the "crowded" mark is read safely across threads. **Left as it is:** DOG.ai's own guests on the card (the document search model, the speech model) load without taking a turn, so one could come onto the card while a clip is at its peak; rare (the owner is on the Video page), and a turn for guests is a change to M15's design.
  - *A clip beside another program, in detail* ("another program" was a chat model in `llama-server` started by hand; each row a clip of the real length). The engine's own reserve (`--max-vram -3`, or `-5`): 222 s and 377 s for 8 steps (58 s on an empty card), the card at 23.8 to 23.9 GB. **What decides it is how large a plan the engine is given:** with 11 GB or less it keeps the model itself in RAM and brings it to the card piece by piece (it then takes the plan and about 2.8 GB: 13.8 GB at a plan of 11, 8.6 GB at 5.8) and turns a Quick clip into frames in a second or two; with 12.5 GB or more it keeps the model on the card and takes 17 GB while a Quick clip is turned into frames, which beside 6.8 GB took 13 s in one run and **had not ended after 2½ minutes in the app** (plan 13.1; found by the app check, after the engine alone had passed). Previews at full size took most of a minute each on a crowded card (4 min 26 s for a Quick clip). The model's own decoder (Better) is the other way round: with a plan of 12.5 or 13.1 GB it worked (13.6 GB, its usual 3¼ minutes of decoding); with 10 GB **its decoding had not ended after nine minutes and free RAM fell to 1.8 GB**. Hence 10 GB for Quick and 13 GB for Better. With a plan of 5.8 GB the engine made a Quick clip in 154 s with only 8.9 GB free, so the 15 GB a clip is said to need is more than the engine needs once it is told; left as it is (it costs 9.5 GB of RAM that way, and no small card was tried).
  - *Every request body* (a stand-in program in the picture engine's place recorded one run of the app: 6 clips started, 1 picture, 1 cancel, 1,343 progress questions). A clip's request holds the sentence, the fixed negative prompt, width, height, seed, 121 frames, 24 a second, Euler, 20 steps, guidance 5, flow shift 5, "webm", file-size setting 70, the preview kind and interval, and the first frame as plain base64 when there is one; **no address, path or `data:` in any request**. The first frame sent for the picture handed over by Animate… is byte for byte the WebP Images drew; the one cut from a file is the JPEG the page made; **Make one like this sent the kept clip's request byte for byte** (and got the same frames back). Images' own request has no input picture. The engine's answers do repeat the sentence (`generation_info`, in all 6): DOG.ai never reads that part. **Every clip made in that run (5) passes DOG.ai's own file check** (one VP8 track, 121 frames, the writer's name, nothing else) **and holds no word of its sentence**; the four kept rows hold exactly the bytes the engine sent (SHA-256 compared).

  - *Leak check with clips* (Debug build, the installed engine, the same steps on a plain data folder and on one locked from before its first start): a picture drawn in Images and handed over by Animate…, a clip from it **kept**; a first frame cut from a file, a clip from it **not kept**; a clip from a sentence **kept and then removed** in Kept videos; a first frame from a second file **held and let go**; a clip **cancelled** while it was drawn. Each clip was saved with Save as a file… into a folder outside the ones searched. Searched for (bytes and base64, UTF-8 and UTF-16): five made-up words, the three clips, the three first frames, four previews (one per clip, taken off the screen), two stills, the Images picture and the two source files, in the data folder (database, logs, the browser control's folder: 182 files), the models folder, the installed engine's folder (86 files) and TEMP. **Plain: the database holds the kept clip (its video, still, first frame and sentence) and nothing else holds anything. Locked: nothing anywhere.** Not searched: the still of the removed clip (a flat picture, under the tool's smallest size).
  - *In the app, on the real engine* (Debug build, throwaway folders of hard links, the installed CUDA engines). **Sizes not made before:** Quick Square from a file cut to square 2 min 23 s (estimate 2 min 21 s), again by Make one like this 1 min 56 s (1 min 52 s); **Better Tall (832 × 1216) from a picture drawn in DOG.ai 7 min 35 s (7 min 20 s)**; Better Wide from a sentence 7 min 6 s (6 min 50 s). **A chat model in memory when Draw is pressed:** set aside ("the chat model (8.7 GB) was set aside. 22.8 GB is free."), the clip in 2 min 29 s (2 min 26 s), and the note under the clip says so. **Straight after that clip:** a chat reply 14 s after it was sent (the model loads again), a picture 19 s after Draw (beside the chat model), no video program left. **Ten Quick clips in a row:** 2 min 1 s to 2 min 11 s each; the card at 723 MiB before and 705 to 745 MiB after every clip; free RAM 20.1 GB before and 18.0 to 19.8 GB after; four clips held at the end, as designed; the page's own memory (the browser control) between 0.5 and 0.7 GB. **DOG.ai's own memory rose from 0.2 GB to 1.9 GB** over the ten (it had fallen back to 0.3 GB once in between). The engine's answers were being copied whole once a second (each repeats the newest preview, up to 1.9 MB of text); they are now read in place. That did not change the figure by itself (five clips: 0.8 GB with it, as without), so it is mostly memory the .NET runtime has freed and not yet given back, not something held: the engine code run alone for eight clips, with everything unused collected after each, held 6 to 9 MB throughout. Not followed further.

  - *What the owner can expect.* (1) **Video has two tabs, Make and Kept videos** (as Images has Make and Kept pictures; "Kept videos" is in Ctrl+K too). A still opens the clip large and **it plays** (looping, no sound), with its facts ("640 × 960 · 5 s · Quick · seed … · from a picture · 4.4 MB · kept …"), its sentence and the picture it started from; ‹ › step through the clips shown. **Make one like this** puts the sentence, the first frame, Quick or Better, the shape and the seed back on the Make tab, so Draw makes the same clip (when a Better clip comes back on a card that cannot make Better, the note says Draw makes it at Quick). **Save as a file…** writes the very WebM; **Remove** asks first. Going from tab to tab keeps a clip being made (Kept videos says so, with a link back). (2) **Animate…** is a new button beside a picture in five places: a try in Images, a kept picture's large view, the portrait in the character editor and in the persona editor, and a story or chat picture's large view. It opens Video with that picture as the first frame, shown large with the line "The clip will start from a kept picture. Say below what moves, then press Draw." A picture DOG.ai drew goes as it is; any other picture (a PNG portrait, a picture of another shape, one enlarged past what the engine takes) is cut from its middle to the nearest shape, as a file is, and the line says so ("the portrait, cut to wide"). The picture itself is not changed. Without the video model or the picture engine the button is not there, so those screens are as before M20.

- **M20 step 4, Video: Make (2026-11-17). Done.**
  - *What the owner can expect.* (1) **A Video section** (a sixth place in the navigation of every look, a sixth tile on Playhouse's home screen, "Video" in Ctrl+K). Type a sentence, press **Draw**: the line beside it says how long it will take ("Takes about 2½ minutes · Quick · 960 × 640"), a bar fills while the clip is made (steps are counted while it is drawn; turning it into frames and writing the file run on the clock), and with Quick the clip's frames show every few steps. **Cancel** stops it in a second or two. (2) **Start from a picture…** opens a picture file; DOG.ai cuts it from the middle to the nearest of the three shapes (tall, square, wide), shows it large until the clip is made, and the clip begins with it. One line says a clip follows its first frame only loosely. The shape follows the picture while it is held. (3) **Quick or Better, shape, seed** in the side panel; remembered, never the sentence. (4) The finished clip **plays in the page** (looping, no sound), with up to four clips held beside it as stills; **Keep** puts one inside DOG.ai, **Save as a file…** writes the very WebM, **Use this seed** makes the next clip from its seed. Nothing is kept unless Keep is pressed. (5) Where the video model or the picture engine is missing, the page says what is missing with a link to Models.
  - *What was built.* **Core** (`Core/Video/VideoSession.cs`): `VideoSession` (memory only: sentence, first frame, at most four tries, the clip in progress, the bar that never goes back), `VideoTry` (the clip as made, its still, `ToKeep()`), `VideoFrame` (a held first frame, checked for both qualities), `VideoFrames.Cut` (nearest shape and middle part, written at Better's size), `VideoBar` (the progress rule), `VideoChoices` (document `video`: quality, shape, seed), `VideoPanels` (fold ids and every line of wording). `VideoEstimate.IsWarm`. **The card rule** `VideoMemory.CardCanMake` / `CannotMake` / `RoomEnough` (below), used by the page and `CardCoordinator`. **UI:** `Pages/VideoMake.razor`, `js/video.js` (blob: player, the still), `attach.js` `openFrame` / `openFrameBytes` / `cutFrame` / `dropFrame`, a "video" icon and colour, the Modes entry. **App:** `VideoSession` and `VideoChoices` registered, the choices loaded at start.
  - *Decided while building* (say so to change one). (1) **Better on a 16 GB card: not offered.** A real 16 GB card reports about 16,376 MiB. The coordinator's size check was the smaller problem: after clearing, Windows keeps about 1 GB, so such a card has about 15 GB free and Better's 16 GB need would be refused only after the chat model had been set aside. Now one rule, `VideoMemory.CardCanMake`: the card's total less 1 GB (Windows) plus 512 MiB (the slack Models already allows) must reach the need. A 16 GB card gets Quick (the room after clearing is judged with the same slack: 15.0 GB free makes a 15 GB clip) and Better is off with the words "Better needs about 16.0 GB of free graphics memory, more than this card (16.0 GB) has once Windows has its part. Quick works on it."; the coordinator refuses Better there before anything is moved. Tested, not tried on a real 16 GB card. **The room check is looser on every card, not only 16 GB ones:** a clip is now made with 0.5 GB less free than its need (14.5 GB for Quick, 15.5 GB for Better). On this 24 GB card that matters only when another program holds about 7.5 GB or more while a clip is made, which is the case step 2 left open for step 6 (the card peaks at 23 of 24 GB while a clip is decoded). (2) **A first frame from a file is cut at Better's size of its shape** (1216 × 832, 1024 × 1024, 832 × 1216), whichever quality is chosen: the file itself is not kept, so a frame cut at Quick's size would be enlarged for a Better clip; at Quick the engine trims a sliver (under 3%) from two edges. (3) **At most four clips are held** (3 to 20 MB each); the one shown is never let go. (4) **Use this seed** was added beside Keep and Save (the seed row's Keep needs a seed to keep; this sets it from the clip shown). (5) Leaving the page cancels a clip, as leaving Images cancels a Draw.
  - *Checked in tests* (`dotnet build`; `dotnet test` 1,455 pass, 23 new in `VideoMakeTests`). The session through a fake engine: previews asked for, the seed the engine used kept, nothing of a finished clip left in progress, the kept row's owner, sentence, seed, files and video, a first frame carried to the row; four held, the one being looked at kept; Cancel, two at once refused, a request with a problem sends nothing, a refusal leaves no try; Keep, still and Remove marking; no constructor arguments; the remembered document has exactly quality, shape and seed, and damaged values are dropped. The bar at every stage (waiting and making room 0, start on the clock and capped, steps counted, decoding passed when never reported, never over 99%) and never going back. The cut for eight sizes (each fits inside the picture, has the frame's shape, and passes for both qualities); frames refused in words (nothing, not a picture, another shape, too small) and a DOG.ai WebP taken as it is. The card rule at 16,376 and 24,564 MiB, unmeasured and 12 GB; on a pretend 16 GB card Quick is made and Better refused before the chat model moves. Ctrl+K, the back button's name, fold ids clash with no chat or Images fold; the words.
  - *Checked in the app* (Debug build, throwaway data folder `D:\dogai-b-data\d4` and models folder of hard links, the installed picture engine read only; no chat model loaded in these runs). **Real clips:** from a sentence, Quick Wide: **2 min 20 s, the estimate said 2 min 26 s**; from a picture (a Windows wallpaper, 3840 × 2400, cut to wide), Quick: **2 min 4 s against 2 min 26 s**, and its first frame, read from the player, is the cut picture; Quick **Tall** (640 × 960): 2 min 3 s against 2 min 26 s, and the next one, warm, 2 min 3 s against 1 min 57 s; **Better Square** (1024 × 1024, the model's own decoder): **7 min 23 s against 7 min 35 s**, the bar running on the clock through 3½ minutes of decoding. Every clip played in the page from a blob: address (5.0 s, its own size), got its still in the strip; **Keep**: the two kept rows in the database hold exactly the bytes **Save as a file** wrote (SHA-256 compared), the frame clip's row its first frame (the 117 KB JPEG); the `video` settings document holds `{"Quality":0,"Shape":2,"Seed":null}` and nothing else. **Cancel** while a Better clip was drawn: the page said "Cancelled." 1.5 s later and no sd-server was left. The app log's card note said each time that the video needs the whole card (15.0 GB, 16.0 GB for Better), that nothing else was on it, and that 22.6 GB was free. **Looks:** the empty page and the page with a first frame in **all three looks, dark and light, at 1,400 px and at 700 px** (with the side panel opened as a drawer at 700), and Playhouse's home with six tiles, read on contact sheets; nothing wider than the window. A clip being made and a finished clip in Workbench dark, Fireside light and Workbench light at 1,400 px, and a finished clip in Playhouse dark at a short window (886 × 630: the page scrolls to the buttons, as Images does). **A finished clip in all six looks at 700 px** (one more Quick clip, made at 700 px in 2 min 9 s) and in Fireside dark, Playhouse dark and Playhouse light at 1,419 px: each time the page was left for Settings and opened again, and the clip played again from a new blob: address with its still in the strip. **Without the model** (an empty models folder) and **without the picture engine** as well: the page shows only the missing lines with their links, in Workbench dark, Fireside light and Playhouse dark at 1,400 px (model) and 700 px (both). **Found in the looks and fixed:** the bar sat over the picture's bottom edge (it is under the picture now); an empty stage with a held first frame now shows the frame. Without the model the side-panel opener is no longer shown (there is no panel; as On this PC › Voice).

The page now says that at Better nothing shows until the clip is done. Why (the preview-only switch, the size, memory) is not known: for step 6.   - *What the owner can expect.* (1) **Models › Get models › Video** offers the video model, Wan 2.2 TI2V-5B, as one download of four files (16.3 GB as the app counts, the plan's 17.5 GB in thousands): the model, its text encoder, its decoder and the fast decoder, three from Hugging Face and one from GitHub, **each checked against its published checksum before it is kept**. - **M20 step 2, Video: Core, storage and engine (2026-11-16). Done. No screen yet.**
  - *What the owner can expect.* Nothing new to click: this step is everything under the Video section. (1) **DOG.ai can make a clip**: the code that asks the picture engine for one, from a sentence or from a sentence and a first frame, at Quick (960 × 640 and its tall and square sizes, fast decoder) or Better (1216 × 832, the model's own decoder), with progress, previews at full size and Cancel. On this PC a real Quick clip took **149 seconds with the chat model set aside first; the estimate said 146** ("about 2½ minutes"; about 2 minutes when a clip was made just before). (2) **A clip takes the whole card and gives it back**: the chat model is set aside, the picture model and the document search model are let go, the clip is made, and afterwards the card was at 1.4 GB (Windows' own) every time; the chat model was back and had answered 13.5 seconds after the next message. (3) **Cancel takes about 2 seconds**, also while the model's own decoder runs (the program is ended). (4) **Kept clips have their place in the database** (not used by any screen yet), covered by the lock, backups, restore and a moved portable folder like everything else. (5) **Every clip is checked before DOG.ai shows or keeps it**: a file with tags, an attached file, a title, a date, sound or any part DOG.ai does not know is refused, so a clip with the prompt written inside can never be kept, whatever the engine does.
  - *What was built.* **Core** (`DogAI.Core/Video`): `VideoRequest` beside `ImageRequest`, sharing nothing (sentence, the fixed English negative prompt, size, seed, decoder, first frame as bytes; 121 frames, 24 a second, 20 steps, Euler, guidance 5, flow shift 5, file-size setting 70; `For(sentence, quality, shape, …)` with `PictureLevels`' own sizes); `VideoStartFrame.Problem` (a WebP without EXIF or XMP or a JPEG without profile or extras, of the clip's shape); `VideoEstimate` (the trial's table, part by part, and in words); `VideoMemory.Need` (15 GB Quick, 16 GB Better); `WebmInfo` (the WebM check by structure) and `VideoClips.Problem`; `IVideoEngine`; `Clip`, `ClipOwners`, `IClipStore`; `VideoFolders`, `VideoFiles`, `AppSettings.VideoFolder()`. **Engine:** `SdApi` has the video route (`VideoBody`, `MakeVideoAsync`, `ParseVideoJob`: `generation_info` is never read, and nothing made of a reply has a place for it); `VideoServer` (Engine/Video) starts the picture engine's program with the model's four files, `--taesd` (with `--taesd-preview-only` for the model's own decoder), `--max-vram -3` and `--disable-image-metadata`, tells its own `PictureLogFilter` the sentence before the request leaves, checks the clip, and ends the program in every case. **Card:** `CardCoordinator.MakeAsync` (it is `IVideoEngine` for the app, as it is `IImageEngine`). **Storage:** the `Clips` table (migration `Clips`: 26 columns, `Still`, `StartFrame`, `Video` last in that order; three triggers that remove a deleted message's, character's or persona's clips) and `SqliteClipStore`. **App:** the services are registered and the video engine's filtered lines go to the log; no page uses them.
  - *Decided while building* (say so to change one). (1) **The video program runs for one clip only**: started for it, ended when the clip is collected, cancelled or failed. It follows the plan's "ends the video program when the clip is collected" to the letter and makes three things simple: the decoder is always the one asked for (the engine decides it at start), a cancel always takes, and nothing can stay on the card. It costs about 3 seconds a clip. (2) **The first frame must have the clip's shape within 5%**, not its exact size: every DOG.ai picture of one shape (640 × 960, 832 × 1216, 960 × 1472, 1280 × 1856) then goes with every tall clip as it is, and the engine takes a sliver off two edges; a tall picture for a wide clip is refused, for the page to cut (step 4). (3) **The fixed negative prompt** is written here (the trial's exact English wording was not kept): "overexposed, static, motionless, blurred details, subtitles, text, watermark, worst quality, low quality, jpeg artifacts, ugly, deformed, disfigured, extra fingers, fused fingers, poorly drawn hands, poorly drawn face, malformed limbs, extra limbs, cluttered background, walking backwards". It leaves out the model's own "style, artwork, painting", which would push a drawn first frame towards a photograph. (4) **A clip of up to 96 MB** may be kept (five seconds were 2.7 MB at Quick here). (5) A card that cannot be measured is cleared and the clip is made (a picture is refused the benefit of the doubt the other way; a clip has nothing left to move).
  - *Checked with the real engine* (the installed `sd-server.exe`, read only, the four files in `D:\dogai-b\models\video`; 13 clips made, 1 cancelled; `VideoSmokeTests`). **Short clips** (33 frames, 832 × 480, 8 steps): from a sentence 13 to 15 s with 4 previews (PNG, 0.5 MB each); the same seed again gives the same frames (the file differs in 7 bytes: the muxer's random track number); **from a first frame** (a real JPEG, 1024 × 640 into 768 × 480) 13 s, and **looked at as a sheet of frames in a browser: the clip starts as the picture** (a green disc and the word WOOF) and moves on from it, while the same request without the frame is something else; the model's own decoder 22 s. **The file:** the header says "webm", then an index, the facts (time unit, length, "stable-diffusion.cpp" twice as the writer), one VP8 track with its size, one frame a cluster, cues; **no tags, no date, no title, nothing else**, and the sentence's words, the model's file name and the first frame's bytes are not in it (searched as plain and as two-byte text). **Cancel** while a 30-step clip with the model's own decoder was drawn: back after 2.2 s, the program gone. **What it printed** (479 lines): no word of a sentence; of the 295 lines that would be logged none shares a word with one. **Its folder** (86 files, sizes and dates) unchanged; DOG.ai's own folder for the run held only its blank page. **A full Quick clip beside a real chat model** (Huihui Qwen3.5 9B Q8, the installed CUDA engine, twice): chat model loaded (8.7 GB) and answering; the clip (960 × 640, 121 frames, 20 steps, previews) made in 149 s both times against an estimate of 146 s; the note in the log read "a video needs the whole card (about 15.0 GB); the chat model (8.7 GB) was set aside. 22.5 GB is free."; afterwards 1.4 GB used; the next message was answered 13.5 s later with the model back at its 8,192 tokens.
  - *Seen, not changed: how full the card gets.* With a program started fresh for each clip, **the card peaks at 23.0 to 23.1 of 24.0 GB while the clip is decoded** (measured stage by stage on three clips: with the fast decoder 19.2 GB while drawing and 23.0 GB for the second it decodes; with previews the same peak already while drawing; the model's own decoder 23.1 GB). The engine takes what the cleared card has, up to the card's size less the 3 GB of `--max-vram -3`, and Windows itself holds 1.4 GB of that. **Nothing overflowed: shared memory stayed at 0.1 GB and no clip slowed down.** The trial's 13.8 GB was the second clip of a program that stayed running. What this means: on this PC with nothing else on the card it is fine; **if another program holds several gigabytes of graphics memory while a clip is made** (a game, another AI program), the clip could overflow into shared memory and crawl, and DOG.ai's rule (refuse under 15 GB free) would not catch a card with, say, 18 GB free. Not tried. Two ways to close it, for step 6's memory check or the owner's word: a larger reserve when other programs hold memory (the switch's number worked out from the measured card), or keeping the program between clips.
  - *Checked in tests* (`dotnet build`; `dotnet test` 1,414 pass, 62 new). The request and its defaults for all six sizes; the first frame (both kinds, every DOG.ai size of a shape, other shapes, EXIF, a comment, a profile, a PNG, a path as text); the estimate against the trial's four totals and its 12- and 30-step runs; the need; **the WebM check** (the engine's own layout; tags, an attached file, chapters, a title, a date, a file name, a track name, private data, sound, a second track, VP9, "matroska", bytes after the end, padding with words in it, a writer's name that is a sentence, an unknown part, frames of another track, a file cut at every seventh byte, 3,000 files with bytes changed: never an exception); the request body (every setting; no `batch_count`, `embed_image_metadata`, LoRA, path or address; the first frame as the very bytes); stages and steps reported once each, previews once each; `generation_info` with the prompt in it leaves no trace; refusals in words; a cancel the engine does not act on is given up after the wait; **both programs' start switches pinned whole** (a picture's as before M20); the log filter told before the request leaves; a clip with tags or of another size never handed on; nothing started for a bad request, a `.ckpt` or a missing file. **The card** (fake engines): chat model, picture model and search model leave in one go and the clip is made; a reply being written is waited for; a message, a picture and a model load sent meanwhile wait and then succeed; Unload everything says "A video is being made…"; too little room after clearing is refused in words (naming a search model that is indexing) and nothing is made; a card too small, a bad request or a missing file is refused before anything moves; a cancelled or failed clip gives the card back; an unmeasurable card; a page's own model load is set aside. **Storage** (the real database): a clip comes back byte for byte with its still and first frame and makes the same request again; lists carry the still and never the video or first frame; 19 ways a clip is refused; the column order; **upgrade from the build before** (old chat and kept picture intact, a clip can be kept) and back again (no trigger left behind: deleting a chat still works); a removed clip's video, still, first frame and sentence are gone from the data folder's bytes and another clip stays; a replaced still is gone; clips go with their message, character or persona, whichever code deleted it; **locked**: nothing in the clear, lock on and off byte for byte; **backup and restore** in all four cases; **portable**: nothing to rewrite (the table's text columns are listed, and no file name may be a full path).

Two things must be kept as they are for that: the start switch `--disable-image-metadata` (without it the prompt, the negative prompt and the model file names are written inside every WebM, WebP and AVI), and the reply's `generation_info` (it repeats the prompt) must never be logged or kept.

  - *What changed for the owner.* (1) **Write mine writes a real message.** Right after a greeting and in group chats it used to give back its own instruction, the character's card or a line in brackets almost every time (0 to 3 real messages in 20 tries); it now wrote a real message in every try: 280 of 280 by hand on seven chats and two models, 39 of 39 in the app. (2) **A draft no longer makes the engine read the chat again.** Until now every Write mine re-read the whole chat, and the reply after it re-read it once more; a draft takes about a second. (3) **In a group chat the draft does not start with your own name** ("Alex: …" or "[Alex]"), not even while it is being written. (4) Drafts are a few sentences with an action, somewhat longer than before, and never think first whatever the thinking level (in practice they never did). **Nothing else changes: "[Start a new chat]" stays, so no character's reply is any different** (*owner's decision, 2026-11-16*, asked in the session with the trial's numbers: "Fix Write mine only").
  - *What the trial found* (numbers under "M19 › Trial results"). **The opening line is not the cause.** With today's request no wording passes: right after a greeting the best plain line gave 5 of 20 on a short card and 3 of 20 in a group (today's line 0 and 0), on both models. What the model gives back is **Write mine's own instruction** (the bracketed line DOG.ai put into the system message: 14 to 19 tries of 20 on the short card), then the card's headings, then the opening line. With that instruction gone the open turn was still unreliable in groups and on the second model. **Asking for the draft as an ordinary request** (the chat, then one turn that asks for the owner's next message) passed on every chat, with the old line or a plain one alike. And "Hello." as the line changed replies: a short-card character greeted with "Hi" remarked on the word in 4 of 5 replies (0 of 5 with today's line).

The planner for Illustrate was sent no picture. (A first recording, before the Write mine and Continue fixes: 122 requests, 28 picture parts, 0 problems.)
  - *Graphics memory after twenty pictures in one chat* (the engine's own figure, sampled every two seconds beside the app; Huihui Q8 with its picture input, 8,192 tokens; pictures made 2,048 px and reduced by the page to 1,024 × 1,024, the most a picture costs: 1,026 tokens each; five messages of four pictures, then six of one, 26 in all, every word on them read right on both engines):

    | | Installed (CUDA, build 12359) | winget (Vulkan, build 12314) |
    |---|---|---|
    | At load | 9,854 MiB | 9,516 MiB |
    | After the first pictures | 9,938 MiB | 9,791 MiB |
    | After twenty pictures | 9,938 MiB (+0.08 GB) | 9,791 MiB (+0.27 GB) |
    | After twenty-six | 9,944 MiB (+0.09 GB) | **11,876 MiB (+2.3 GB)** |

    **Twenty pictures leave both engines flat, as the 1,024 size and the cap were meant to.** The Vulkan engine's step came later, at the 26th picture, so three more runs were made on it. A chat of 24 messages with one picture each: flat until the 9th message, then **one step of 2.2 GB** (9,791 → 11,979 MiB; the whole card's figure rose with it), then flat for the fifteen messages after. **A chat with no picture at all** (sixteen long messages): 9,516 at load, 9,757 after the first reply, **one step of 0.7 GB** at the 8th message (10,460 MiB), then flat. In all three runs the step came once the chat had outgrown the model's room, the oldest messages were left out, and the engine read the prompt again from its changed start. So it is that engine build's own behaviour in any long chat, larger when the prompt holds pictures, and it is one step, not a leak that goes on; picture size has nothing to do with it (none was over 1,026 tokens). The engine DOG.ai installs does not do it. Unloading the model, or its next load, gives the memory back. Nothing was built for it (see "For the owner to decide").

- **M17 step 2, Core, storage and engine (2026-11-16). Done. No screen yet: nothing looks different, but everything under the screens of steps 3 to 5 is there.**
  - *What it does.* (1) **A chat message can carry up to four pictures**, kept in the database in a table of their own (so under the lock, in backups, moved with a portable folder), deleted with their message or chat, and gone from the disk the way removed data is. (2) **Only a plain JPEG is ever kept or sent**: DOG.ai reads the file's structure itself and refuses anything that is not a whole JPEG of at most 1,024 pixels with nothing in it but the picture (no camera information, no comment, nothing after the picture's end; the browser's colour profile is taken out). The same check runs again right before a picture goes to the model. (3) **A model that has its projector file beside it is loaded with it and sees**; one without is started exactly as before. The projector is found by rule each time (a file with "mmproj" in its name, in the model's folder, whose header matches the model; F16 first, never F32); a projector that does not belong is never passed. (4) **The model gets the stored picture, byte for byte, in front of its message**, in General, character chats and group chats; regenerate keeps it, an edited question keeps its pictures (or only the ones chosen), a reply is carried on with its pictures in place. (5) **A model that cannot see gets a short note where the picture is**, and the reply remembers that, for the line step 4 will show. (6) **A picture costs what the engine itself counts** (asked only of a model in memory, so counting never wakes one), else the rule from the trial; it is left out only together with its message; the prompt inspector shows "[picture 1,024 × 640, 642 tokens]" where it is. (7) **Too much for the context is said in words** with both numbers, in place of the engine's own sentence. (8) **Summaries, recalled messages, the markdown export and a story made from a chat get "[picture]"** where a picture was. (9) **Fit counts the projector** (its file size + 0.25 GB): the load decision, the fit badge and "Fits beside pictures" on On this PC › Chat models, the benchmark; memory measured with the projector is kept apart from memory measured without. (10) **If the engine stops when given the projector** (an engine too old for the switches), the model is loaded again without it and the log says so. (11) **LoRA file words are sent cleaned** (the owner's decision of 2026-11-15): the picture maker and the test picture now send an unsaved LoRA's file words the way they are shown; a card the owner saved is sent as it is.
  - *Confirmed.* The three points under "M17 › For the owner to confirm" were built as recommended (1,024 pixels and JPEG at quality 90; the projector loaded whenever the model has one; the F16 file first); the owner did not say otherwise at the start of the session.
  - *Decisions taken here (say so to change one).* (a) **A message's pictures come first in it, before its text**; found document passages come before both. In a group chat, where two messages of the owner's in a row reach the model as one turn, each picture stays in front of its own message. (b) **A reply is carried on (Continue, Write mine) with the pictures really in the prompt**, through the engine's raw route (tried on both engines), not with notes in their place. (c) **A message may be a picture with no words**; a chat that starts so is named "A picture". (d) **Stories refuse pictures** outright. (e) **A picture's file may be at most 2 MB** (noise at 1,024 × 1,024 and quality 90 is 0.9 MB). (f) The note a model that cannot see is given: "[The user attached a picture here. You cannot see pictures, so say so if it matters.]".
  - *Checked with a real model, on both chat engines* (the installed CUDA one, build 12359, and the winget Vulkan one, build 12314; Huihui Qwen3.5 9B Q8 by hard link with its projector, downloaded again with the owner's yes, checksum as listed, removed afterwards): the projector is found by the rule and the model sees; **the engine's count of a picture equals the rule** (42, 642 and 1,026 tokens for 256 × 160, 1024 × 640 and 1024 × 1024); **a streamed reply about a picture** ("red"; the word on a sign with text on both sides of the picture); **thinking with a budget and a picture**; **Answer now with a picture in the prompt**, straight at the engine and through the chat service; the prompt's size as DOG.ai works it out is the engine's own figure (within 6 tokens); a second turn in words still knows the picture, a second picture joins it; regenerate; **a reply carried on with the picture in place**; **nine pictures in one message are refused in words** ("What was sent needs 9,… tokens, and the model was loaded with room for 8,192 …") and the engine goes on answering; without its projector the model loads as before, is given the note, and answers that it cannot see; **a projector that does not belong, forced on a small model, stops the engine and the model is loaded again without it** (3 to 6 s); what the engine prints holds no picture and no question. Left unchecked by the trial and checked here: streaming, and Answer now with a picture.

Then a picture was put on the old chat's first question through the store (a small scratch program with DOG.ai's own code and the folder's password; there is no button yet) and the app was asked about it: **"The word written in the picture is WOOF, and the shape beside it is a green circle."** No piece of the picture, as bytes or as base64, is readable anywhere in the data folder (database, its log file, the app log, the browser control's folder).

- **M17 step 1, the trial (2026-11-16). Done; no app code.** Run by hand with hard links to the owner's models in a throwaway folder, on both chat engines: the winget one (build 12314, which turned out to be the Vulkan build) and the one DOG.ai installed (build 12359, CUDA). The numbers and what they change are under "M17 › Trial results"; in short:
  - *The model.* **No new model is needed: the owner's own Huihui Qwen3.5 9B sees pictures** once its projector file is beside it, and its repository has that file. So do the other three Qwen models here (Defiant Fable 9B, the 27B and the 40B), each from its own repository; the two Gemma writers cannot. **One download, with the owner's yes:** `Huihui-Qwen3.5-9B-abliterated.mmproj-f16.gguf`, 918,166,016 bytes, checksum as Hugging Face lists it; removed again afterwards with the test folders. Three more were offered and not wanted (the same projector as Q8, the original Qwen3.5 9B's, the 27B's), so those were not tried.
  - *It works on both engines.* A picture is answered about correctly; four pictures in one message, pictures over several turns, a character's system message, a group chat turn, thinking with a budget, and no thinking with a fixed answer form (what the Images helper needs) all work. **A later turn does not read the pictures again** (they are kept with the rest of the prompt; a new question took 0.1 s of reading).
  - *The numbers.* **The projector adds 1.1 GB of graphics memory** (9.75 GB for Huihui Q8 in place of 8.65). **A picture costs (width ÷ 32) × (height ÷ 32) + 2 tokens**: 642 for 1024 × 640, 1,026 for 1024 × 1024, 2,306 for 1536 × 1536, never more than 4,098. **Reading one takes 0.2 to 0.4 s.** Text replies are as fast as before, and a model loaded without a projector is byte for byte as before (the engine never picks one up by itself).
  - *What it changes.* (1) **The chat engine cannot read WebP**, the format DOG.ai keeps pictures in: a kept picture is turned into a JPEG in the page before the model sees it. PNG, JPEG, BMP and GIF are read; WebP, TIFF and SVG are refused. (2) **Pictures are reduced to 1,024 pixels on the long side** (recommended; the plan said 1,024 to 1,536): four of them then cost at most 4,104 tokens of the 8,192, and the sentences on a full-HD screenshot are still read right (at 768 they are invented). (3) **The engine fetches a web address when one is given in place of a picture**, and tries to start a program called `ffprobe` on bytes it cannot read: DOG.ai sends only JPEGs it made itself, as bytes, never an address. (4) **The engine can count a picture's tokens itself**, exactly and at once, so the context meter needs no guess. (5) **A projector that does not belong makes the engine load the whole model and then stop** (23 to 40 s lost): DOG.ai compares the two files' headers first. (6) With the projector loaded, **Huihui Q8 still fits beside a Small picture but no longer beside a Normal one** (the chat model is set aside for it, as for Fine today).
  - *How well it sees.* A photo-like landscape: time of day, shores, "no people", "a computer rendering", all right. A screenshot of DOG.ai's Settings: the three thinking settings, the selected entry, a quoted line and the status bar, all right at 1,024 pixels. Three pictures drawn here turned into tags with the helper's own form: the girl in the sunflower field and the forest waterfall came back with nearly all their prompt's tags and the right number of people; **the anthro wolf knight came back as a person with wolf ears** (and as "1girl" in two of four runs): step 5 has to tell the form about such characters.

- **M18 step 4, Thinking budget (2026-11-15). Done.**
  - *What changed for the owner.* (1) **Thinking levels in place of the three on/off switches** (Settings › Chats and stories, "Thinking"): Off, Short (up to 512 tokens), Normal (1,536), Long (4,096), No limit, one list each for General, character and group chats, and stories. They start at General No limit, the other two Short. What was set before is read as decided: General on is No limit, General off is Off; character chats, group chats and stories are Short whatever their old switch said. A backup from before this step restores the same way. (2) **With a limit the thinking is cut there and the model goes on to its answer.** A character reply at Short took 9 s where it took 34 to 47 s with no limit. Nothing is added at the cut: the thinking stays the model's own words. (3) **Answer now**, beside Stop, shows while a reply is thinking; it ends the thinking and the answer starts about 0.15 s later. Stop still stops everything. It is also in the story rewrite dialog, and it works while an adventure's choices are being worked out. (4) **The count while it thinks**: the reply's heading reads "Thinking… 1,200 / 1,536" (without the second number when there is no limit). (5) **A small line under the thinking** says when it was cut: "The thinking was cut at its limit of 512 tokens." or "The thinking was ended with Answer now after 216 tokens." (6) **A chat engine too old for this** thinks past the limit, or does nothing when Answer now is pressed; the line under the thinking then says so and points to Models › Engines. Nothing else is built for it. (7) A reply with no answer after a cut says "the model wrote nothing after its thinking was cut short". (8) Summaries and DOG.ai's own jobs (the character draft, picture planning) still never think.
  - *How it is built.* `ThinkingLevel` and `ThinkingLevels` (Core/Chat/ThinkingLevel.cs): the budgets, the names, how old settings are read (`AppSettings` keeps three nullable levels; with none, General follows its old switch and the other two are Short; nothing is rewritten, which is why old backups read the same), and `Apply(preset, level)`. `SamplerPreset.ThinkingBudget` and `ThinkingControl` (never saved with a style) become `thinking_budget_tokens` and `reasoning_control` in `LlamaApi.ChatRequestBody`. The stream's first piece names the reply (`ChatDelta.ReplyId`); `ActiveReply` keeps it and counts thinking pieces; `ChatService.AnswerNowAsync` → `IChatEngine.EndThinkingAsync` → `LlamaApi.EndThinkingAsync` (`POST /v1/chat/completions/control`, `reasoning_end`). What a reply's thinking was is kept with it as numbers (`ThinkingFacts`: tokens, budget, Answer now, refused; `thinking` in the message's details beside a picture or choices); `ThinkingCut` turns them into the line (within 100 of the budget = cut at the limit; more than 100 past it, or Answer now refused = engine too old). UI: `AnswerNowButton`, `ThinkingCutNote`, the three lists in `Settings.razor`. Details are in `CLAUDE.md` › "Thinking budget (M18 step 4)".
  - *Confirmed by the owner (2026-11-15, at the start of this step).* No words are added at a thinking cut. An engine too old for the thinking fields gets only the note to update it; DOG.ai's own stop-and-ask-again route is not built.
  - *Decision taken here (say so to change it).* **A story job that has a size limit of its own thinks no longer than its answer may be, and the thinking comes on top of the limit**: the choices step (2,400 tokens), a rewrite of a selection (as little as 50), the story plan draft (3,000). Without this, Short would have taken up to 512 tokens out of a rewrite's room, and Long or No limit could have used all of it and left no answer. So choices at Short may take 2,400 + 512 tokens; a 52-token rewrite thinks for at most 52.
  - *Checked.* `dotnet build` (the one old warning in a voice test); `dotnet test` 1,232 pass (new in `ThinkingBudgetTests`: the budgets and their order; each level on a request; a job with a size limit keeps its room; the request body with and without a limit, Off, and DOG.ai's own jobs carrying neither field, and never a budget message; the budget never saved with a style; the control route's answers read; old settings read as decided, a number that is no level, and through the real SQLite store a document written before the levels, then a choice kept; the count's wording; the trial's counts at a cut reading as "cut at the limit", thinking well under the limit and with no limit having no note, Answer now winning, an engine past the limit or refusing; the facts kept beside a picture; no answer after a cut; and through the real chat service with a fake engine that thinks until told to answer: each kind of chat sending its own level, story requests, Answer now in General, the count and the button's state in a character chat, a group round going on after Answer now, an adventure at No limit ended with Answer now and still getting its choices, a cut told from the count, an engine that ignores the budget, an engine too old for Answer now, a kept picture keeping the facts). **In the app** (Debug build, throwaway data folder `D:\dogai-b-data`, a hard link to Huihui Qwen3.5 9B Q8 in `D:\dogai-b`, 8,192 tokens of context), **with both engines**, the winget one (build 12314) and the one DOG.ai installed (build 12359, read only; the running program's path was checked):

- **M18 step 2, Picture sizes (2026-11-15). Done.**
  - *Decided by the owner (2026-11-15).* The two trial choices that bear on this step are confirmed: **Small is 640 × 960** (960 × 640 wide, 768 × 768 square), and **Make larger is ×1.5, or ×1.25 when only that fits beside the loaded chat model**. Done; no app code.** Run by hand with hard links to the owner's models in a throwaway folder (Huihui Qwen3.5 9B Q8 and the `illustrious_novaFurryXL_ilV180A` checkpoint), the winget chat engine (build 12314), the chat engine DOG.ai installed (build 12359) and the owner's picture engine. The numbers and what they change are under "M18 › Trial results"; in short:
  - *Pictures.* **Small is 640 × 960** (wide 960 × 640, square 768 × 768): 4.0 s against 6.6 s for today's size, and 1.4 GB less graphics memory at the peak. 576 × 864 is too small (broken faces and hands on full-body pictures). The picture engine rounds every side up to a multiple of 64, so sizes must be multiples of 64. **Make larger at ×1.5** (960 × 1472, 8.7 s) keeps the layout and looks as good as or better than a picture drawn at today's size; ×1.25 lands exactly on today's size but is softer. With Huihui Q8 loaded both Small and today's size fit beside it (the card peaked at 19.5 and 21.1 GB of 24); Small has 2.2 GB to spare by DOG.ai's rule where today's size has 0.65 GB.
  - *Place.* The cause is not the wording. When the story's World box names the place, today's planner already draws it. It fails **when the place was set only in earlier passages** (a talk-only passage got a tavern, a bedroom and a garden bench; given the passage before it, or the last picture's place, it got the forest campfire 3 times of 3), and **in chats when the card's description is long**: the Scenario is cut off before the planner sees it. With no place words at all the checkpoint draws a room. Step 3 is reordered around these two causes.
  - *Thinking.* **Both engines take the budget per request** (`thinking_budget_tokens`), so nothing is reloaded and DOG.ai's own stop-and-ask-again route is not needed (it was tried and works). **Both also have their own "end thinking now"** (`reasoning_control` and `POST /v1/chat/completions/control`): the answer began 0.05 s after it was asked. A character-chat reply took 34 to 47 s with no limit, 20 s at 1,536, 8 s at 512 and 2 to 3 s with thinking off; a story passage with no limit thought for 6,846 tokens and wrote nothing, and took 52 s at 4,096, 22 s at 1,536, 10 s at 512. The answers after a cut were as good as the unlimited ones.
