The picture engine's "Install again". Use this and Delete on On this PC › Voice. The Hugging Face token saved or removed in the new Connection panel (the code moved unchanged). A failed download ("Failed" on the button). The Connection panel is in the side panel, so below 1050 px the token cannot be reached, as before M11. Left from M10: Benchmark, Show in folder and Delete in the ⋯ menu; "(not found)" defaults; the ⋯ menu on the last card; Settings search in a narrow window; the chat model picker's own context size choice.
- **M10, Defaults and Settings (2026-11-14). M10 is done.**
  - *What changed for the owner.* (1) **Models → Library** starts with **Default models**: one dropdown each for General, Characters, Story, Document search and Pictures, and the **Context size** models load with (moved here from the side panel, owner's decision). Chat modes offer "No default"; Document search and Pictures offer "Pick automatically" (owner's decision for Pictures, 2026-11-14: with nothing chosen DOG.ai draws with the first checkpoint it finds, as before, so "No default" would have been untrue). Pictures is the same choice as Settings → Pictures and Models → Images "Draw with". A default whose file is gone shows as "(not found)". The per-model "Default for…" menu is gone; each model keeps its "Default for …" chip. The side panel's "How fit is judged" now only says which context size the fit badges use and links to Library. (2) **Model cards** in Library have one button, **Load**, and a **⋯** menu with the file path, **Benchmark**, **Show in folder** (new) and **Delete…** (still asks first). A document search model has only the menu. (3) **Settings in categories**: a side list (Appearance, Chats and stories, Voice, Pictures, Privacy, Windows, Backup, About) with a **Find a setting** box above it; a result opens its category and flashes the setting. Settings opens on the category shown last (Appearance the first time); `settings/<category>` opens one directly. In a window narrower than 760 px, where the side list is hidden, the categories are tabs at the top (search is not available there). (4) **Shorter help text** in Settings: one line under a control, the rest behind a small ⓘ button. Warnings stay visible (losing the password and recovery key, what a backup leaves out, what the Ctrl+Alt+Space key watches). (5) **Default models** and **Folders** in Library fold away with a click on their heading and stay as left; folded, Default models says what is set.
  - *Shared pieces for M11 to M13* (`DogAI.UI/Components`): `Fold` (a collapsible section remembered by id), `MoreMenu` (the ⋯ menu), `InfoButton`, `PageTabs`. What is remembered (folded sections, the last Settings category) is `InterfaceMemory` (Core/Settings), one JSON document `interface` in the settings store, apart from `AppSettings` so it never races a screen saving settings. Default-model logic moved to `ModelDefaults` (Core/Models); the Settings categories and search entries are `SettingsCatalog` (Core/Settings).
  - *Checked.* `dotnet build`; `dotnet test` 922 pass (19 new: remembering and forgetting sections, damaged documents, the last category; setting, clearing and deleting defaults and the document search re-index signal; settings search; every search entry's element id exists in the Settings files). **In the app** (throwaway data folders, `D:\DOG.ai\Models` only read as the models folder; nothing there was deleted, downloaded or benchmarked): every default set and cleared (chips, messages, the side panel's context line, Settings → Pictures following the Pictures row, choices kept after leaving the page), the context size changed, folds closed and still closed after leaving the page, Settings remembering its category, search ("micro" opened Voice at Microphone), the ⋯ menu opened and closed. Every element id and name that Settings had before M10 is still on the page (compared by script; 17 new). **Looked at in all three looks, dark and light:** Library with the defaults block and an open ⋯ menu, Library with Default models folded, Settings → Chats and stories with an ⓘ text open. **Narrow window (700 px), all six:** Settings with the category tabs and Privacy's ⓘ text. Seen in Workbench dark only: Settings → Appearance, Pictures with the rating ⓘ open, the search results, Voice after a search.
  - *Links into Settings.* Plain `settings` opens the category shown last, so the two texts that sent the owner to Settings now name the place: Models → Voice links to **Settings › Voice**, and the lock screen's "turn the lock on again" says **Settings › Privacy**. Seen: the Voice link opens Voice. `#rating-tags-note` is on the page with an empty models folder while another category is open.
  - *Not checked.* Benchmark, Show in folder and Delete from the ⋯ menu were not clicked (they act on the owner's real models; the code behind Benchmark and Delete is unchanged). The search model's "(not found)" case and a Pictures default whose checkpoint is gone were not seen. The ⋯ menu on the last card opens downwards and makes the page scroll rather than opening upwards. Settings search at narrow widths. The model picker in chats still has its own context size choice (left as it was); both write the same setting.
- **Pictures in character chats and group chats (2026-11-14, after M9).** A reply in a character chat or a group chat can now be illustrated, the same way a story passage can.
  - *What changed for the owner.* Every reply has **Illustrate** beside Copy and Edit (hidden while a reply is being written). It opens the picture maker; **Use this picture** keeps the picture above the reply. Clicking it opens it large, with **Redraw** and **Remove**. A reply keeps one picture. Regenerate and the swipe arrows make a new version with no picture; the earlier version keeps its own. Deleting the chat deletes its pictures. General chats have no button.
  - *What the picture is planned from.* The reply under its writer's name, with up to six messages before it as background (cut to fit; the newest are kept). Who may be in it: the character (or the group's members), then your persona, each with their own Looks; at most two people. Default shape Wide.
  - *Not carried over.* Turning a chat into a story copies no pictures. Chat export (Markdown) has none.
  - *Under the hood.* No new table and no migration: it is the story-illustration storage (`Illustrations.KeepAsync` / `RemoveAsync`). New: `ChatPictures` (Core/Chat). The reading of kept pictures, the large view and the maker hook moved out of `StoryThread` into a shared `MessagePictures` component, which `ChatThread` now uses too.
  - *Checked.* 903 tests pass (8 new). In the running app with a real chat model and a real checkpoint: a picture drawn, kept and shown in a character chat; the large view; Regenerate (new version has none, the first shows its own again); a two-person picture in a group chat; a story made from the chat has no pictures; a story passage still keeps and removes a picture through the shared component. Looked at: Workbench dark and light, Fireside dark and light, Playhouse dark (group chat) and light (character chat).
  - *Published portable build (checked the same day).* A copy of `publish\win-x64` (launcher, `portable.txt`, `app\`) on a temporary folder, with its own `models\`: a picture drawn and kept in a character chat, removed through the large view (the button went back to "Illustrate"), drawn and kept again, and a two-person picture kept in a group chat. Afterwards its `data\` held no picture files outside the database and `data\temp` was empty. Workbench dark only.
  - *Not checked.* No separate leak check (the storage path is the one story pictures use, checked in M9 step 9). Not tried with the lock on. A persona with Looks in a picture was covered by tests only.
- **M9 step 9, finish (2026-11-14). M9 is done.** The leak check, the published portable build, the leftovers, a review of all of M9, and the fixes that came out of them.
  - *What changed for the owner.* (1) A picture engine that cannot start now says why at once (see next point), and its box on Models → Images has **Install again** beside "Up to date", for a damaged copy. (2) In the picture maker the strip of tries is no longer squeezed to a thin line when the maker is taller than the window. (3) A rating word shared by two levels no longer goes into the prompt and the negative prompt at once. (4) "On: 1 extra tag" instead of "1 extra tags". Nothing else looks different.
  - *The picture engine dying at start (the leftover from step 7).* Reproduced with the trial's engine copy that lacks NVIDIA's files. Cause: Windows showed its own box ("sd-server.exe - System Error: … cudart64_12.dll was not found") on the desktop, and the program stayed alive behind it, so DOG.ai waited its whole minute and then said "took too long to start" (measured: 60.0 s). Now the program is started so that Windows shows no such box; it ends at once and DOG.ai says: "The picture engine could not start: Windows could not find a file it needs (one of the libraries that come with it). Install it again in Models › Images (at the end of the page)." Measured in the app: **1.8 s** (was 60.0 s). The same for a library of the wrong version or a damaged one. A program that really hangs for a minute now gets "was still not answering 60 seconds after it started, so DOG.ai stopped it. Try the picture again" (this wording was not seen: nothing here hangs). **Not changed:** llama-server and the document search model are started the old way, so a chat engine with a missing library would still sit behind such a box until its own timeout; the same one-line change would fix it, with its own check.
  - *Leak check (two long walkthroughs of the built app; throwaway data and models folders; the samples hard-linked in and the links removed afterwards; the 27B read where it is; graphics memory free and nothing running before each run).* Each run drew **16 pictures to the end and cancelled 3**, each group with its own marker word in the prompt: on Models → Images, one thrown away by drawing the next, one kept, one with the Maid_marian LoRA kept and then removed, one thrown away by leaving the page, one cancelled at step 3; a **character portrait** through the picture maker (Draw planned by the 27B, Try again and a redraw thrown away, a redraw cancelled a few steps in, a redraw used, cropped and saved with the character); a **persona portrait** (one thrown away, one used and saved, then **Remove** and Save); **story illustrations** on an adventure opened with the card's greeting (Draw and Try again thrown away, a redraw cancelled a few steps in, a redraw kept with Use; the maker opened again on the kept picture, one more drawn and the maker closed without Use; a second passage written by the 27B, a picture drawn and thrown away, one kept and then removed in the large view). Every picture's bytes were saved from the page (and a preview of two of the cancelled ones, and the card's and the persona's PNG pictures). Afterwards every file was searched for the 10 marker words, the made-up extra tag and the password (any case, UTF-8 and both kinds of UTF-16) and, per picture, for three 32-byte pieces, the same pieces as base64 text however they line up, and two pieces of the page's own address text: 500 patterns.
    - **Plain data folder** (201 files, 13 MB: database, its log files, logs, WebView2 folder): the kept test picture, the kept portrait (its row and the card's own PNG), the kept story picture, their marker words and the Looks marker were found **only in the database file**, which is the control that the search finds things. The three removed pictures, the ten thrown-away ones, the cancelled ones' previews, the removed persona picture's PNG and all their marker words: **nowhere**.
    - **Locked data folder** (202 files; `lock.json` written before its first start, unlocked with the password, Windows Hello declined): **nothing at all**, not a marker, not a picture including the kept ones, not the password.
    - **Both runs:** nothing in the picture engine's folder (27 files, 1.2 GB), nothing in the system temp folder (20 and 5 files changed during the runs; all could be opened this time), no new crash dumps, nothing changed in llama-server's folder. In the models folder the made-up tag was found only in the tag file itself. The app logs (453 and 474 "picture engine" lines) hold no prompt word; in the plain run five lines say how many engine lines were held back (6 to 18 each); the Extra tags lines say only "on, 1 extra tag" or "off". No warnings.
    - **Said plainly:** the search left out `%TEMP%\claude`, the Claude desktop app's own working folder, where the scripts, the saved pictures and the throwaway data folders themselves were (the data folders were searched on their own). Files in the temp folder older than the run were not opened. The sample files are unchanged (size, date, one name each again). Nothing under `D:\DOG.ai` was written (24 files there carry dates later than the run: the owner's picture engine files, made at 10:25 with the dates from their zip). The plain run was done in two parts, with the review's fixes built in between, because my script unticked "Open with her first message" the first time; two empty stories were left in that folder.
  - *Published portable build.* `dotnet publish … -p:PublishProfile=win-x64`; `DOG.ai.exe`, `portable.txt` and `app\` copied to a temporary folder (never the owner's). **Fresh:** the image folder is `<folder>\models\image`, the engine box says "Not installed", and the picture maker says "The picture engine is not installed. Install it in Models › Images" (this notice had never been seen). **Engine: located, not downloaded.** The copy that step 2's real install made (master-1094, with its marker file) was put into `<folder>\data\engine\images`; the app showed it as "DOG.ai's copy". The install by download was **not** run in the portable build (0.9 GB; it was run for real in step 2). llama-server came from the installed one through the test override, since portable mode looks nowhere else. **In use:** the 27B through Library → Add a folder, made the default; a made-up tag file through **Choose a file** ("On: 1 extra tag"); Settings → Pictures set to Draft and Sensitive, and the maker then started at Draft and Sensitive; a test picture (10.9 s, the first) kept and one thrown away; a portrait (Draw 51.7 s with the 27B loading, Try again 4.8 s, Use, crop, saved); a **written story** (authored, which had never been illustrated in the app): the 27B wrote the first passage (41 s), Illustrate, Draw (20.6 s), Use, and the picture sat above the passage. **Moved:** the whole folder was moved into another folder with a space in its name. At the next start the log said "DOG.ai moved from … to …; 1 saved paths updated"; the models folder was the new one, the added folder and the defaults were still there, Settings → Pictures still said Draft, Sensitive and the checkpoint, Extra tags was still on, the engine was found in the new place; the kept test picture **drawn again from its saved settings was identical**; the portrait was shown, the maker opened on "The picture in use" and redrew it at Standard from the same seed (9.5 s); the story's picture was above its passage. A search of the moved folder found the kept pictures only in its database and the two thrown-away ones nowhere, and **its own temp folder (`data\temp`), which the engines use in portable mode, was empty** (the trial's last "not tried"). Looked at in Workbench dark only: the maker's "not installed" notice, the moved story with its picture, the moved maker with the redrawn portrait. *Seen in passing:* `publish\win-x64` in the repository folder still holds loose program files from the old flat layout (12 November) and a `data` folder from an earlier run; they are not part of a publish and were not copied or touched.
  - *Found and fixed: a rating word on both sides.* When two rating levels share a word, the lower level put it into the prompt and, as a word of the level above, into the negative prompt too. A word the chosen level uses itself now stays out of the negative prompt. A test pins it (the old test had the mistake written into it).
  - *Try again after a rating change (step 8's leftover), seen in the app:* a story's kept picture was drawn under one rating; after the rating was changed in Settings, **Try again** drew with the new level's words.
  - *Code review of all of M9 (`/code-review high` over the commits since M8, about 14,000 lines).* Nine findings. **Fixed (7):** two page renders could read a story's pictures at the same time, each picture twice, and the slower one could put back an older set (now one load at a time); every try's picture was turned into text again on every screen update, about 10 MB a second of waste while drawing with a full strip (now once per picture); a database failure at **Use this picture** on a passage would have shown as a page error instead of the maker's own message; the extra tag list followed the old models folder after the folder was changed, until Models → Images was opened (the maker and the Looks box now look first); the progress wording existed twice; a model's paths were built in two places; a chosen tag file was checked and then read a second time to copy it (the copy itself is now what is checked). **Changed afterwards on the owner's word (1):** Looks on an imported card were used exactly as typed, unchecked, like the owner's own; see "Looks on imported cards" below. **Changed afterwards on the owner's word too:** a model the owner asks for no longer waits behind a page's own load; see "A wanted model does not wait" below.
  - *Checked.* `dotnet build` (the one old warning in a voice test); `dotnet test` (893 pass; 8 new cases: the exit codes Windows gives a program it cannot start, the picture engine's message for them, other codes left to the program's own lines; the rating test now pins a shared word on one side only). **Real engine, one class at a time, graphics memory free before each:** `ImageSmokeTests` (2 pass; new: the program copied without its libraries says so after 2.2 s); `CardSharingSmokeTests` with the 27B (pass: set aside, two pictures, back in 32 s, answered). That test failed twice first, on an empty answer and not on the sharing: the 27B spent its 40 tokens thinking. The test now asks without thinking. `EngineSmokeTests` was not run (the chat engine's code did not change). **Looked at, in all three looks, dark and light:** the "could not start" message under Draw a test picture, and the picture engine box with "Up to date" and **Install again** (a throwaway data folder holding the program without its libraries; DOG.ai went online only to ask GitHub which release is newest; nothing was downloaded). **The strip of tries after the fix:** looked at in Workbench dark, Fireside light and Playhouse dark; measured in all six (108 px high, pictures 96 px, the maker inside the window, its body scrolling).
  - *Not checked.* **Install again** was not clicked (a 0.9 GB download). The picture engine's install by download in the portable build. The "still not answering" wording. From earlier steps, still open: the **Remove** confirmation looked at in no look; Rewrite and Edit of an illustrated passage; the saved web page and EPUB opened in a browser or reader; backup and restore of pictures, and export of a character with a generated portrait, in the running app; a changed LoRA strength in Edit and redraw; three people in one picture; NoobAI, Pony and sentence checkpoints; a real Hugging Face download into the image folder; the final "Waiting for the chat model to finish loading…" wording. As before: what sits in memory was not examined, and Windows' page file is outside what DOG.ai controls.
  - *Looks on imported cards (the owner's decision, 2026-11-14, after the review).* When a card is imported, its Looks are now checked against the list of tags DOG.ai leaves out of its own tag list: a piece that is such a tag (in any spelling, with filler words, written as one word) or contains one is taken out; everything else stays as written, known tag or not. A note in the Characters list says how many were taken out and why, never which ("2 of this card's Looks tags were left out, because DOG.ai leaves them out of its own tag list. Cards keep such tags once the full tag list is added in Models › Images."). With the full tag list added, cards come in whole and nothing is said, as for the planner's own tags. What the owner types in a Looks box is still never checked, and a card's Looks are checked only once, at import. **Not covered:** rating words in a card's Looks are not tags on that list and stay; cards imported before today are not looked at again. *Measured with the real lists (counts only):* all 3,682 tag names in the source file that are on the left-out list are caught as written and with spaces; written as one word, 198 of the longer ones are not. None of DOG.ai's own 29,451 tags is taken for one; of its 22,907 tags of several words, 11 would be if a card wrote them as one word. *Checked:* 3 new tests with made-up tags (896 pass). In the app, with a card built by script from the source file so that no such word was typed or printed: without the full list the two tags were gone from the Looks box and the note showed; with the real full list on, all five pieces came in and no note showed; neither tag is in the app log. The note was looked at in all three looks, dark and light (three of them with a longer first wording).
  - *A wanted model does not wait for a page's own load (the owner's decision, 2026-11-14, after the review).* Since step 3 every chat model load waits for its turn on the graphics card, and a page's own load of its default keeps the turn until it ends. So a model the owner asked for in that time waited for the whole of that load first. Now such a load is stopped (the default was not asked anything yet) and the wanted model loads at once; and a page's load that had to wait its turn no longer replaces a model that came in meanwhile. **A correction to what the review said:** it named the assistant's "Load it" button as the way to run into this. That button, like every Load button, is off while a model is loading, so through the screens the wait could only happen in the first moment after a page opens, before the screen says "Loading" (the page's load already has the turn then, while it measures what fits). The fix covers that moment as well as a load already under way. *Checked with the real llama-server and the 27B* (a new test, `CardSharingSmokeTests`, needing only `DOGAI_TEST_MODEL`): asked two seconds into the page's load, the wanted model was ready 31.6 to 41 s later, fully on the graphics card (16.6 GB), and the page's load never finished; asked in the first moment, ready 23.8 s later, one load only; a page's load arriving late left the loaded model alone. **With the fix switched off the same test failed**: the page's load ran to the end and the wanted model was ready 111.6 s after it was asked for. Afterwards, one class at a time: `EngineSmokeTests` (3 pass), `CardSharingSmokeTests` (2 pass); `dotnet test` 897 pass. In the app: opening a character still loaded the Characters default by itself. *Not checked:* the first-moment case by hand in the app (it is a fraction of a second); the 27B's load time varied a lot today (24 to 74 s) for reasons outside this change, most likely Windows' file cache.
  - *Also changed.* `tools/walkthrough.ps1` can write a step's value to a file (`@{ eval = …; save = '<file>' }`), which is how every picture was collected. `CLAUDE.md` has a short map of how pictures work and how to repeat the leak check.
- **Extra tags and the download token (2026-11-14, after M9 step 8; the owner's decisions).**
  - *The bundled tag list* holds 29,452 tags. Some tags are left out of it by the owner's choice; the repo keeps them only as SHA-256 fingerprints (`Images/Tags/excluded-tags.txt`), never as words, and the tag check drops a written tag that is one of them, in any spelling, or contains one, instead of correcting it into a tag nearby. `tools/make-taglist.ps1` builds the list with DOG.ai's own rules (`TagCsv`); `tools/tag-fingerprints.ps1` adds fingerprints from a word list kept outside the repo and prints only counts. Measured on 204,538 made-up written forms of tags that stay: 140 that the tag check used to resolve are now dropped (0.07%).
  - *Extra tags (Models → Images).* Off by default. **Add the full tag list…** offers two ways: **Download** (it shows first: `danbooru_e621_merged.csv`, 6 MB, from GitHub, project DominikDoom/a1111-sd-webui-tagcomplete at commit 40ad070a02, MIT licence; it goes through the existing download manager and the online gate, with no new host; the file is checked against its pinned SHA-256 before use) or **Choose a file…** (a Windows file picker). The file is kept as `<models>\image\tags\full-tag-list.csv` (a fixed name inside the image folder, so nothing is saved anywhere and a portable move needs nothing). It is read at start and when added, with the same rules as the build (general and species tags, 100+ posts, no artist, character, series or meta tags), and treated as untrusted: at most 64 MB, UTF-8, every line exactly "name, category, posts, other spellings", otherwise refused whole with a plain message and nothing copied. Its tags are merged into the bundled list in memory; the box then says **On: N extra tags** with **Remove**, which deletes the file and goes back to the bundled list. The log says only "Extra tags: on, N extra tags" or "off".
  - *Found by review and fixed: the Hugging Face token.* Every download sent the owner's Hugging Face token, if one is saved, to whatever server the file came from. Until now every download came from Hugging Face; the full tag list comes from GitHub, so the token would have gone there. The token now goes to Hugging Face only (`FileTransfer`), for one connection and for several; a test checks both.
  - *Checked.* Tests with made-up tags (the tag file rules, the fingerprints, the tag check, the extra tag file added, read at start, refused, reported when damaged, and removed; the token kept from GitHub). In the app, on throwaway folders: a file that is not a tag list refused with nothing copied, a made-up tag file chosen and removed, and the real download (6,024,803 bytes with the pinned SHA-256, "On: 3,606 extra tags", in use 1.6 s after the click). The box was looked at in all three looks, dark and light.
- **M9 step 8, story illustrations (2026-11-14).** A story passage can now have a picture above it.
  - *What the owner sees.* **Illustrate** in a passage's actions (beside Edit and Rewrite) opens the picture maker from step 7, set for stories: **Wide** to start with, the chat model picks one moment of the passage and plans it with **at most two people**, people whose card has Looks keep them exactly. **Use this picture** puts it above the passage at once. Clicking the picture opens it large, with **Redraw** (the maker again, showing "The picture in use": Try again, Edit and redraw, or New idea) and **Remove** (asks first). Afterwards the action reads **Redraw picture**. The **Web page** and **EPUB** copies under "Save a copy" include the pictures above their passages; **Markdown** does not.
  - *Who can be in a picture.* The story's linked cards (with their Looks, their character LoRA and a few words from the card about who they are), then the names in the bible's **Cast** box (no Looks; the rest of their line, such as "a grey wolf mercenary in a battered leather jacket", tells the model who they are). The story's premise and world go along as background, not to be drawn.
  - *Keeping (decided here).* Unlike a portrait, the picture is kept **at Use**, not on a later save: the passage already exists, and there is nothing else to save. It is a `Pictures` row owned by the passage's message; the passage records which row it shows. One per passage: a new one replaces the old row. Tries stay in memory until Use; a closed maker leaves nothing.
  - *Exports (decided here).* The web page carries the WebP pictures inside the file itself (nothing points outside it). The EPUB carries **JPEG copies at quality 92**, made in memory in the page: WebP only became a standard EPUB picture format in EPUB 3.3, and older e-readers do not show it. Pictures in the book are separate files (`images/picture-1.jpg`…), not pages. 75 KB as WebP became 147 KB as JPEG.
  - *Settled from earlier steps.* (1) **Try again after the rating changed: fixed.** Try again now uses the rating chosen in Settings at that moment: the rating words after the quality words are swapped and the negative prompt is made again from the checkpoint card. Edit and redraw is left alone, because its prompt is shown and the owner edits it. (2) **"Planning" while a set-aside model loads again: fixed.** The maker now says "The chat model was set aside to make room for pictures. Loading it again before it plans the picture (about half a minute for a large model)…"; seen for 32 s in the app before "planning" took over. (3) **The first picture after opening a page.** For **Draw** and **New idea** the wait is not waste: the chat model must be loaded to plan, and the page's own load is that load. For **Try again** or **Edit and redraw** of a kept picture right after opening the page it is waste: measured 48.7 s, of which 33 s waiting for the page's 27B load to finish, then the model was set aside at once. **Fixed afterwards, on the owner's go-ahead (same day):** when a picture needs the graphics card while a chat model is loading *only because a page opened* (nobody has asked it anything yet), DOG.ai stops that load and marks the model "set aside" at once, instead of waiting for it and setting it aside a moment later. The next message loads it, as after any picture. A load the owner starts in the model picker, a benchmark, and a model coming back for a message that is waiting are never stopped: the picture waits for those as before. Measured in the app: Try again right after opening the story **13.9 s** (was 48.4 s); the model button said "· set aside"; the next action brought the 27B back and its passage was written 41.9 s after the click (the load the page would have done anyway). The log says "a chat model was loading only because a page opened, so the load was stopped and the model set aside for the picture". Also: the maker said only "Starting…" during that wait; it now says "Waiting for the chat model to finish loading; then the picture makes room on the graphics card if it needs to…" (worded so it stays true when the load is not the page's own, and for a chat model small enough to stay beside the picture). An earlier wording ("opening this page started it…") was seen in the app for the whole 31 s wait (Playhouse light), in a second run that measured 48.4 s again; the final wording was not seen in the app.
  - *How the early set-aside works.* `ModelLauncher.LoadDefaultIfIdleAsync` (a page opening, or a DOG.ai job such as planning that loads the mode's default) marks its load as automatic; `EngineManager.SetAsideLoadAsync` stops only such a load and leaves the model `Resting` with its context size; the graphics-card coordinator calls it before it waits for its turn (choosing the checkpoint and drawing). A job that loaded the default for itself is safe too: a set-aside model is still "ready", and the job's first request loads it again. Checked: 2 new tests with pretend engines (a page's load is set aside and the picture does not wait; the owner's load is waited for), and one with the real llama-server and the 27B (set aside within seconds of starting to load, nothing left running, the next request loaded it with the same context size; the owner's own load was not stopped); `dotnet test` 847 pass. The real-engine tests for sharing the card were run again with the 27B, the scratch picture engine and the sample checkpoint read where it is: `EngineSmokeTests` (3) and `CardSharingSmokeTests` pass. Run together, `CardSharingSmokeTests` failed once on a memory figure (17.3 GB measured for the chat model coming back): `EngineSmokeTests` was loading a second copy of the 27B at the same time, since test classes run side by side; run alone it passed. Run the real-engine tests one class at a time. Found by review and fixed: a model stopped this way has no measured size, so when it comes back the picture model is now always let go first (before, a size left over from a smaller model set aside earlier could have kept the picture model loaded beside the 27B); a test pins it. Graphics memory before these runs: 1.0 to 1.8 GB in use by other programs, no model loaded, no llama-server, picture engine or Ollama running. *Not seen in the app:* **Draw** (with a plan) right after opening a page since this change (it waits for the page's load to plan, as before; nothing draws until the plan has ended). *A trade:* a small chat model that a page is loading, which would have fitted beside the picture, is now set aside too; it costs one reload of that small model with the next message. Not tried with a small model.
  - *Found while building and fixed.* Working out an adventure's choices read the passage before the model answered and wrote it back after, so a picture kept in between would have been lost; it now reads the passage again before saving. A test pins it.
  - *Timings (27B, Illustrious sample, Standard Wide 1216×832).* First illustration with the 27B loaded: 27.5 s from Draw (plan 10 s, setting aside and loading the checkpoint 6 s, drawing 10 s). New idea with the 27B set aside: 53.6 s (32 s loading it again). Try again: 6.5 s. Second passage with the 27B loaded: plan about 5 s, Draw to finished 21.6 s.
  - *Checked.* `dotnet build`; `dotnet test` (844 pass, 11 new: a passage with only a picture keeps it; who can be in a picture and what is said about them; the planner gets the passage, two people at most and the background; Try again under a new rating in both directions, Pony's missing level, sentence checkpoints, a prompt without quality words; the web page with its picture and Markdown without; the EPUB's picture files, manifest and pages; one row per passage, replaced, chosen again, removed; a picture for a passage that is gone leaves nothing; deleting a story takes its pictures; choices worked out while a picture is kept do not drop it). **In the app** (three walkthroughs on one throwaway data folder; throwaway models folder with the samples hard-linked in, links removed afterwards, samples unchanged; the 27B read where it is through Library → Add a folder and made the Story default; the picture engine unpacked fresh from the trial's two zips into a scratch folder; graphics memory free and no model loaded before each run): an adventure with Marian (Looks and the Maid_marian LoRA) and Garrick in the Cast box; the 27B wrote the opening; Illustrate, Draw, Use: the picture above the passage, clearly the LoRA's character; the large view; Redraw → New idea → Try again → Use replaced the picture (the database then held only the new row, and the passage pointed at it); an action, the next passage written below the illustrated one; the second passage illustrated and then removed (row gone, the first passage's picture kept); the web page (101 KB, one picture inside it, no web address), the EPUB (one JPEG, listed in the book, shown above its passage) and the Markdown (no picture) saved and their contents checked; the story deleted in the app, and its picture row went with it (1 → 0). No warnings in the app log; nothing left running.

Seen in Playhouse light only: the waiting-for-load message (earlier wording). Seen in Workbench dark only: a passage with **Illustrate** before it has a picture, the empty maker for a passage, the maker with a planned passage picture and "How it was drawn", two illustrated passages.

Opening the saved web page in a browser or the EPUB in an e-reader (their contents were read, not displayed). A passage streaming smoothly below several pictures was not measured (seen working with one picture above). Three people in a story picture (two is the limit, as asked). Rewrite and Edit of an illustrated passage in the app (by design Rewrite makes a new version with no picture and Edit keeps it; not tried). The higher rating levels drawn. **Try again after a rating change** in the app (unit tests only). The **Remove** confirmation (Yes, remove it / Keep) was clicked through but not looked at in any look. The EPUB's plain-paragraph fallback with a picture (no passage was found that triggers it; the code keeps the picture outside the markdown). The published portable build (step 9).
  - *Seen, not caused by pictures.* The 27B wrote a one-line second passage (14 tokens) once. Pictures never enter the story's prompt, so this is the model's own doing; not looked into.
- **M9 step 7, portraits (2026-11-14).** Characters and personas can now have a picture drawn for them, and Settings has a **Pictures** section.
  - *What the owner sees.* **Generate picture** beside Add picture in the character editor and on the personas page opens the **picture maker**. **Draw**: the chat model plans a portrait from the card (the moment, clothes, pose, place, light, framing), code builds the prompt with the person's Looks exactly as typed, and it is drawn at once, with a live preview, the step count, the waiting and making-room messages and **Cancel**. After that the button says **New idea** (a new plan). **Try again** draws the same plan with a new seed, side by side in a strip of tries; it needs no chat model, so a 27B that was set aside for the first picture stays aside. **Edit and redraw** keeps the seed and changes one thing: the prompt, the quality, the shape, or the LoRAs (tick boxes and strengths; untick the character LoRA to see the picture without it). **How it was drawn** shows the prompt, the negative prompt and what the tag check did. **Use this picture** hands it to the existing cropper. Up to 12 tries are held in memory; nothing is written anywhere until the owner saves the character or persona.
  - *Keeping (decided here).* The `Pictures` row is written when the character or persona is **saved**, not at Use: a new character has no id yet, and a cancelled edit must leave nothing. Each owner keeps **one** row, the picture in use; choosing another (drawn or from a file) or **Remove** deletes the old row on save (`Portraits.KeepAsync`: the new row is added before the old one goes, so a picture that cannot be kept leaves the old one; the editor then says so). Reopening the maker shows that row as "The picture in use", ready for Edit and redraw from its own seed, checkpoint, VAE and LoRAs.
  - *Settings → Pictures.* Checkpoint (the same choice as Draw with on Models → Images), the quality pictures start at, **Show me the prompt before drawing** (off by default; on, the maker shows the moment sentence, what the tag check corrected and dropped, and the prompt, which can be changed before **Draw this**), and the **rating** pictures aim for (four levels; General is the default). Kept in the `image-models` settings document, so backups and the lock cover them.
  - *Rating.* Each checkpoint card now has **Rating words** for the four levels. The chosen level's words go after the quality words; the words of every level above go into the negative prompt (no word twice); the chat model is told what the picture may show. Each family starts with its own words (plain SDXL has none); cards saved before today follow their family's words.
  - *Settled from earlier steps.* (1) **WebP and the cropper:** the picture is turned into PNG inside the page (a canvas, in memory), so DOG.ai does not need Windows' WebP support at all; this PC has Microsoft's WebP extension (1.2.47) but it is not used. Seen working: generated pictures reached the cropper and the character list. (2) **A character LoRA from a card**, checked by eye: Marian's card names Maid_marian; with it the picture is clearly the LoRA's character (lilac gown and hood, the face); redrawn on the same seed with the LoRA unticked and its trigger word taken out, a generic fox in a dark cloak in the same pose and place. (3) **Stop on a Looks draft** works (the draft stops, nothing is filled in, the box is usable again). (4) **The Looks box loading the default model by itself** did **not** work: opening Characters → New starts loading the Characters default, and Draft pressed during that load said "No model is loaded". Fixed: `ModelLauncher.EnsureReadyAsync` waits for a load under way, else loads the default; the Looks box and the picture maker use it. Seen working on both pages afterwards (the personas page, which has no model button, loaded the 27B by itself). (5) **The Square shape** drawn in the app: 1024×1024 at Draft, and a kept Square picture redrawn at Fine (1536×1536, same picture, sharper).
  - *Found in the app and fixed.* The maker closed DOG.ai at the first progress step of the first picture (it updated the screen from a background thread); and a late progress message could leave "Finishing the picture…" showing after a picture was done. Both fixed and seen right afterwards (four Try agains in a row kept "Drawn in … seconds"). Two layout flaws, fixed and looked at again: with the Edit and redraw box open the maker was taller than the window and its bottom (Redraw, the tries) could not be reached; it now stays inside the window and scrolls (measured in all six looks). The LoRA tick box sat above its name.
  - *Timings (27B loaded, Illustrious sample).* First picture 21 s (the 27B set aside); Try again 5.0 s at Draft, 6.3 s at Standard; Fine 15.6 s; persona plan plus picture 16.8 s after the review. When the maker is used just after opening a character page, the page has started loading the 27B (about 30 s), the picture waits for that load and then sets the model aside: 49 to 54 s. Wasteful, but correct; not changed here.
  - *Checked.* `dotnet build`; `dotnet test` (833 pass, 19 new: rating words per family, where the rating goes and what goes into the negative, no repeats, cards and settings from before step 7 still read, preferences saved; the planner told the rating; tries drawn as kept pictures, Try again with a new seed, redraw keeping the seed across Draft, Standard, Fine and Square, a LoRA left out or weakened, the cap of 12, a kept picture taken back in with its own quality, shape, VAE and LoRAs and redrawn the same way; one kept picture per owner, replaced, chosen again, removed, and a failed keep leaving the old one). Real engine test (`ImageSmokeTests`) passed. **In the app** (seven walkthroughs; throwaway data and models folders, the samples hard-linked in and the links removed afterwards, samples unchanged, nothing written under `D:\DOG.ai\Models`; the 27B read where it is through Library → Add a folder and made the Characters default; graphics memory free before each run): everything above, plus the database afterwards: one `Pictures` row per owner, the replaced one gone. No warnings in the app log after the fixes. `tools/walkthrough.ps1` can now fill in a Windows folder picker too.
  - *Looked at, each in all three looks, dark and light:* Settings → Pictures; the character editor with Generate picture; the empty maker; the maker with the picture in use and a second try in the strip; the show-prompt review (moment, tag notes, prompt, negative prompt); the Edit and redraw box, top half, and after the fixes its bottom half with the LoRA tick box and Redraw (seen in Workbench light and Playhouse dark; the other four measured, not looked at); the checkpoint card's rating words; the personas page with a generated portrait. Seen in Workbench dark only: the cropper with a generated picture, the cancelled state, the redrawn Fine picture.
  - *New idea after the model was set aside* (tried after the fixes): the 27B came back and planned again in 43 s; the status said "The chat model is planning the picture…" the whole time, with no word that the model is loading again first.
  - *Not checked.* The higher rating levels were not drawn, and whether the rating words change anything was not compared by eye. NoobAI, Pony and sentence checkpoints (none here). The maker with no picture engine or no checkpoint (the notice was not seen). A changed LoRA strength in Edit and redraw in the app (unticking was tried; strengths only in tests). Exporting or backing up a character with a generated portrait in the app. The published portable build (step 9).
  - *Known limits.* Try again repeats the plan's prompt as it was, so after the rating is changed it still carries the old rating's word until New idea. The tag check turned "russet fur" into "fur" in a Looks draft (the colour was lost); "white muzzle" was dropped. The picture engine copy left by the trial lacked NVIDIA's runtime files; it died at start and DOG.ai said only "The picture engine took too long to start" after a minute. A fresh copy from the trial's two zips (already on disk, nothing downloaded) worked; a clearer message when the engine dies at start is for step 9.
- **M9 step 6, prompts and quality (2026-11-14).** DOG.ai can now turn a card or a story passage into a picture prompt, and has its three quality levels. Pictures of cards and passages are still not drawn anywhere in the app (portraits are step 7, illustrations step 8). What the owner sees today: a **Looks** box on characters and personas, quality and shape choices and **style LoRAs** on Models → Images, and Euler as the Illustrious sampler.
  - *Looks.* The character editor and the personas page have a **Looks** box: the appearance words that go into every picture of that person ("1girl, grey hair, long hair, braid, green eyes, scar on cheek"), and optionally a **character LoRA** with a strength (empty means the LoRA's own). **Draft from the description** has the loaded chat model write the words from the description, for the owner to change; it says what it corrected and what it left out. If no model is loaded it loads the Characters default first. Looks are never sent to the chat model in chats. A character's looks are kept inside the card (`extensions.dogai.looks`), so export and import keep them and other apps pass them through; a persona's are a new column (an additive migration, `PersonaLooks`). Looks on an imported card are treated as untrusted: put on one line, cut to 1,000 characters, and a LoRA is accepted only as a file name inside the loras folder.
  - *The tag list (the owner's decisions, 2026-11-14).* DOG.ai ships a list of **33,057 real tags** and checks every tag the chat model writes against it; nothing is looked up online. Source: `danbooru_e621_merged.csv` from the GitHub project DominikDoom/a1111-sd-webui-tagcomplete (MIT licence; tags as of 2024-12-22), downloaded once with the owner's approval (6.0 MB). The owner chose the **merged Danbooru + e621 file** over Danbooru alone, for furry checkpoints. Kept: Danbooru's general tags, e621's general tags and e621's species tags, with at least 100 posts. Left out on purpose: character and series names (they would make the checkpoint draw an existing character over the card's own looks), artist names (they would change the style, and many look like ordinary words), meta and lore tags, and rare tags. What the owner types in a Looks box is never checked. The source, licence text and how to rebuild the list are in `src/DogAI.Core/Images/Tags`; `tools/make-taglist.ps1` rebuilds it.
  - *How a tag is checked.* A real tag is kept. Another spelling the sites list becomes the real tag ("silver hair" → "grey hair", which is where Danbooru files it; "pointed ears" → "pointy ears"). Near misses are put right: joined or plural forms ("pony tail" → "ponytail", "glove" → "gloves"), filler words ("wearing a red dress" → "red dress"), one slip of the keyboard ("candelight" → "candlelight"), two tags packed into one ("long silver hair" → "long hair, silver hair"), and an unknown describing word ("tattered cloak" → "cloak"). Everything else is dropped. A doubtful correction is refused rather than made: never across a colour, size or side word ("grey cloak" never becomes "green cloak"), never when a whole short word differs, never for a sentence, never past "no" or "without".
  - *The picture plan.* The chat model does not write a prompt. It first says in one sentence which **one moment** it will show, then fills a form: who is in it (at most two by default, three at the very most), and for each person what they are, their clothes, expression and pose; then place, light, how much is shown (one of six) and from where (one of five, or straight on). The form is enforced by the engine (structured output), the model is told not to think first, and the answer is read leniently and refused if it describes nothing. There is one job for a **portrait** (from a card or persona) and one for a **passage** (with the names of who may appear, and a few words about each from their card so their usual clothes are not invented).
  - *Putting the prompt together* is done by code: the checkpoint's quality words, LoRA trigger words, a count ("1girl, 1boy", "2girls", "solo", or "no humans, scenery" for a place), then for each person their looks **from their own card exactly as typed** (the model's idea of their looks is ignored), their clothes, expression and pose, then place, light and framing, and the checkpoint's negative prompt. Only someone with no looks of their own gets the model's. Tags are not repeated, each part has a limit, and three people get fewer tags each. The checkpoint's style LoRAs and each person's character LoRA are added (one used twice counts once, at the person's strength; a LoRA that is not installed is left out with its trigger words). For checkpoints that read sentences (plain SDXL) the same form is filled with short phrases and strung together, with no tag check.
  - *Quality levels and shapes.* **Draft** draws 70% of the checkpoint's steps (20 of 28), never fewer than 16. **Standard** is the checkpoint's own. **Fine** is Standard, then enlarged 1.5 times with Lanczos and refined with half the steps at strength 0.3. Shapes: **Tall** 832×1216, **Square** 1024×1024, **Wide** 1216×832. Changing the level or shape changes nothing else in a request, so the same seed can be drawn again at another level.
  - *Tuned by looking (107 pictures from the owner's checkpoint, five subjects: a portrait, a furry portrait, two people in a tavern, a landscape, a full figure in the rain; faces and hands compared enlarged).* (1) **Steps:** at 12 a braid appeared on the wrong person, an eye went wrong and clothes moved to the other person; 16 held together; 20 was good; 28 was cleanest. (2) **Sampler:** with Euler a, every step count gives a different picture from the same seed, so a Draft would not show what the Standard picture will be. With Euler, 20 and 28 steps give the same picture. Euler and Euler a looked equally good on three seeds (Euler a's lines a touch crisper), DPM++ 2M left colour specks on faces, so **Illustrious now starts with Euler**. A checkpoint card the owner has changed keeps its own sampler. (3) **Guidance:** 4 is flat, 7 is harsh and dark, 5.5 stays. (4) **Fine:** the engine's default way of enlarging ("Latent") redrew scars, hair and face shapes at every strength tried, and strength 0.45 closed an open eye. **Lanczos at 0.3** sharpened faces and hands and kept the picture as it was. 8, 14 and 20 second-pass steps looked the same. (5) **Quality words and negative prompt:** the pictures are good with or without them on this checkpoint, slightly more finished with them; they stay as they were.
  - *With the real chat model (the owner's 27B, read where it is).* Looks drafted in 1 to 2 seconds, a portrait or passage planned in 3 to 8. For a scout described in a paragraph it wrote "1girl, grey hair, long hair, braid, green eyes, scar on cheek, slim, mature female"; for a wolf mercenary "1boy, anthro, wolf, grey fur, yellow eyes, tall" (dropped: "broad", "notch in right ear"). Three plans of the same tavern passage each picked the same moment (she sits by the hearth, he holds out a mug). Six of its prompts were then drawn and looked at: **the scout is recognisably the same person in all four of her pictures** (grey braid, green eyes, scar, leather armor, green cloak), the wolf wears his jacket, the valley picture has no people. Seen going wrong, as the plan warned: in one of three tavern pictures the wolf's yellow eyes showed up on her, and both people smiled when only one was meant to.
  - *Found by reading what the model wrote, and fixed.* The first version of the near-miss rule turned "glowing aura" into "glowing fur" and "notch in ear" into "ear focus"; both are now dropped or reduced to a real tag ("aura"). The source data files "lean" (a build) under "leaning" (a pose); that one spelling is ignored. The model picked two framings that contradicted each other ("full body, close-up"), so framing is now one size and one view. It called a male wolf "other" rather than "boy" until the wording said "anyone male, human or not"; it still does in passages (the picture then says "1girl, 1other", which drew correctly).
  - *Style LoRAs.* A checkpoint's settings now have **Style LoRAs** tick boxes: ticked ones go into every picture drawn with that checkpoint, at the strength on the LoRA's own card. They are saved as ticked, apart from the checkpoint's card, so choosing one does not stop the card following its family's defaults.
  - *Checked.* `dotnet build`; `dotnet test` (814 pass, 65 new: levels and shapes; looks made safe, kept through card export and import in every format, other apps' card data untouched, a persona's looks saved and a database from before them upgraded; the tag check case by case on a small list and on the bundled one; the form's shape and lenient reading; the prompt's order, counts, limits, LoRAs and sentence form; style LoRAs kept without freezing a card; the model jobs with a pretend model). **Real engine:** a prompt assembled by the code drawn at Draft and Fine, the Fine one kept and drawn again from its saved settings: identical. **Real chat model:** the run above, three times (the first before the tag list was in). **In the app** (throwaway data and models folders, the samples hard-linked in and the links removed afterwards; samples unchanged; graphics memory free before each run): the 27B loaded, looks drafted for a character and a persona, a character LoRA and strength chosen, saved, and still there after leaving the page and after a restart; a style LoRA ticked and shown on the checkpoint; test pictures at Draft (10.6 s, the first, with loading), Standard (6.6 s) and Fine Wide (15.8 s, 1856×1280) with the style LoRA; with the 27B loaded, the chat model was set aside for the picture as in step 3. Graphics memory peaked at 17.9 GB in use during the Fine picture, which is what step 3's estimate expects. The Looks box on both pages, the quality and shape choices and the style LoRAs were looked at in all three looks, dark and light. One layout flaw found and fixed (a dropdown stretched beside a field with a note). No warnings in the app log; nothing left running.
  - *Not checked.* The NoobAI, Pony and plain SDXL defaults (no such checkpoints here; their numbers are still their makers'), and a sentence prompt drawn for real (one was written and read, not drawn). A picture with a **character LoRA from a card** (the LoRA is saved with the looks; nothing draws from a card until step 7). Three people in one picture. The Square shape in the app (drawn only in the tuning runs). The **Stop** button on a draft, and the Looks box loading the default model by itself (the model was loaded with the model button first). Exporting a card with looks from the running app (tests only). A smaller chat model for the plans (only the 27B was tried). Draft against Standard side by side in the app (compared in the tuning runs). The published portable build (step 9).
  - *Known limits.* The tag check cannot know what a tag means: "ridge" became "ridges" (a body feature on e621), and other odd spellings in the source data may be waiting. A dropped tag is lost without a word unless the prompt is shown (step 7's "Show me the prompt" can list what was corrected and dropped). Exact actions between people, who holds what, and features staying on the right person are as unreliable as the plan says. The **rating** (Settings → Pictures) is not in the prompt yet: step 7.
  - *For steps 7 and 8.* Plan with `PictureWriter.PortraitAsync` or `SceneAsync`, then `PicturePrompt.Assemble(scene, new PictureOrder(settings, people, styleLoras, shape, quality, seed))`, then draw the request through `IImageEngine` **after** the model job has ended. `PlannedPicture.Notes` says what the tag check did; `PictureScene.Moment` is the sentence to show the owner. Edit and redraw: `request.At(quality, shape)` and a kept seed. People come from `Looks.Of(character)` / `Looks.Of(persona)`; a person's `PersonLooks.Lora` is the LoRA's card from `ImageModelCatalog.LoraFor` when the file is installed, else null. Give `SceneAsync` each cast member's `CastEntry.About` (`PictureWriter.About(card)`), and keep to two people unless the owner asks for three.
- **Removed means gone from the database files (2026-11-14, after M9 step 5; the owner's decision).** What the owner deletes (a chat, a story, a character, a persona, a document, a kept picture) no longer stays readable in a plain database's files. With the lock on it was never readable; this is for a database without the lock.
  - *What was really wrong.* Step 5 reported that a removed picture could still be found, and suggested switching on SQLite's "secure delete". That was only partly right: **secure delete has been on since M8** (the SQLite build DOG.ai ships is compiled with it), so the database file itself already overwrote deleted content with zeros. The removed picture was in the **log file beside the database** (`DOG.ai.db-wal`), which keeps earlier versions of changed pages while DOG.ai runs and after a crash. Looking further found a second place: the **search index** kept the words of deleted messages (seen in the file: the words of a deleted reply, in order).
  - *What was done.* (1) Shortly after any delete (0.7 seconds after the last of a burst) DOG.ai folds the log file into the database and cuts it to nothing (`DatabaseTidy`, told by a watcher on every database command); it is also done once after each start. It never fails or slows the delete itself, and tries again a few times if the database is busy. (2) A migration (`SearchWipe`) turns on the search index's own secure delete, so a deleted message's words leave the index at once, and rebuilds the index from the messages that exist, which drops the words of chats deleted earlier. (3) A test pins that the SQLite build has secure delete on, so a package update cannot lose it silently.
  - *Checked.* `dotnet build`; `dotnet test` (749 pass, 10 new; the whole suite passed six times in a row, after two of the new tests' own file searches were made tolerant of SQLite removing its log files mid-search): with DOG.ai still open, a removed picture's bytes and prompt, a deleted chat's words (any case, UTF-8 and UTF-16, title, messages and search index), and a deleted character's text, chats and pictures are in no file of the data folder, while what was not deleted is still there and still found by search; the same for messages written before this change, and for a chat deleted before this change once the update has run; several deletes are tidied once and DOG.ai goes on working; a delete just before closing leaves nothing; a locked database's log file is emptied too; the download token taken out of a backup is not in the backup's bytes. **In the app:** the plain leak-check walkthrough run again (four pictures drawn, one kept, one kept and removed, one cancelled): the removed picture and its LoRA's trigger word are found nowhere, the log file is 0 bytes, and the kept picture and the marker are found in the database (the control).
  - *Not checked, and limits.* A deleted **chat** in the running app (tests only; the picture was removed in the app). The time the search index takes to rebuild at the first start on a large database (it reads every message once). Changing something (a card's text, a setting) is not a delete: the earlier version can stay in the log file until the next delete, the next start or a normal close. A crash in the fraction of a second between a delete and the tidying leaves it in the log file until the next start. On an SSD the emptied log file's space is freed, not overwritten, like any deleted file. Things deleted before M8's SQLite build (13 November) may still sit in unused space of a plain database until SQLite reuses it; turning the lock on, or restoring a backup, rewrites the file without it. Backups and exports made earlier hold whatever they held.
- **M9 step 5, keeping pictures (2026-11-14).** DOG.ai can now keep a picture safely: inside the database, with everything needed to draw it again. Nothing in the app keeps pictures yet (portraits are step 7, story illustrations step 8); the owner sees no change except that the test picture on Models → Images is now drawn the way pictures will be kept.
  - *What exists.* A `Pictures` table (an additive migration; nothing existing is rebuilt). Each kept picture holds the image as WebP, its size, and how it was drawn: checkpoint, separate VAE and v-prediction, prompt and negative prompt, size, seed, steps, sampler, scheduler, guidance, clip skip, LoRAs with strengths, hires fix, and when it was made. Files are named by their path inside their own folder (`illustrious/nova.safetensors`), never a full path; the store refuses a full path, a path that climbs out of its folder, and anything that is not a whole WebP file. `IPictureStore` (Core) has add, read (with or without the image), list by owner (newest first, without the images so a list stays light), delete one, delete all of an owner's; `SqlitePictureStore` (Data) is the real one. `Picture.Of` turns a finished drawing into a picture to keep, `ToRequest` and `ModelIn` give back the request and the model to draw it again.
  - *What a picture belongs to* is a kind plus an id: `character`, `persona`, `message` (a story passage is a message; chat replies can use the same later) and `test`. The kind is plain text, so steps 7 and 8 can add kinds with no migration. **The database itself removes pictures when their owner is deleted** (three triggers: a message, also when its whole chat or story is deleted; a character; a persona), so no code path can leave pictures behind. A new kind that has its own table needs its own trigger.
  - *WebP quality: 90.* Five pictures from the owner's checkpoint (portrait, night street with neon signs, close-up face on white, a Fine picture at 1280×1856, a painterly LoRA picture) were each drawn from one seed as PNG and as WebP at 70, 80, 85, 90, 95 and 100, then compared against the PNG by eye at four times enlargement, with a difference map beside each. At 70 thin neon strokes smear. At 80 the fine lines in an iris are gone and hair highlights soften. At 85 the iris lines are fainter. **At 90 I could see the difference only on the difference map, not in the picture.** 95 costs 55 to 80% more bytes for nothing I could see, and 100 is still not lossless (the slight colour softening of very thin saturated lines is the format's, at every quality). Sizes at 90: 92 to 113 KB at 832×1216 or 1024×1024, 170 KB for the busy night scene, 174 KB for the Fine picture, against 1.5 to 3.5 MB as PNG. 85 would save about a quarter, which is not worth it for pictures that get opened large and cropped into avatars. The engine makes the WebP itself, so DOG.ai needs no picture library; it writes nothing inside the file (checked: no EXIF or XMP part).
  - *The lock, backups, restore and portable mode needed nothing new*, as planned. Tests: the bytes of a picture and its prompt can be found in a plain database file and cannot once it is encrypted, nor in the log file beside it while the app is writing; a database locked from its first day never holds them in the clear; a backup and restore bring pictures back byte for byte in all four cases (plain or locked backup into plain or locked DOG.ai), with nothing readable waiting on disk whenever either side is locked; a locked backup's description says nothing about pictures; moving a portable folder rewrites only the settings row and the picture finds its files under the new models folder.
  - *The picture engine's lines now reach DOG.ai's log* (`picture engine: …`, about 15 to 25 lines a picture: model loading, timings, the seed, hires and LoRA steps, errors) through a filter with two separate rules once any prompt has been sent. (1) Only status lines pass: the ones the engine marks `[I]`, `[W]`, `[E]`, and graphics-library messages such as out of memory. Detail lines (`[D]`, where the engine would print prompts), progress bars and anything unrecognised are dropped. (2) A status line that shares a word with a prompt or negative prompt sent in the last ten minutes is held back, also when the word sits inside a longer one or is cut short; the engine forgets a finished picture after one minute. The log then says how many lines were held back, never what they said. The price: an ordinary line that happens to contain a prompt word is lost too (seen for real: the LoRA loading lines for `Maid_marian.safetensors` when `maid_marian` is in the prompt). The engine's raw output is still never logged.
  - *On screen.* The test picture is now drawn as WebP at 90 (was JPEG). **Keep this picture**, the kept pictures and **Remove** / **Draw again and compare** exist under it only when `DOGAI_TEST_PICTURE_WORDS` is set (it also adds those words to the test prompt): a switch for checking, not something the owner sees.
  - *Leak check (two walkthroughs of the built app, throwaway data and models folders, the samples hard-linked in and the links removed afterwards, graphics memory free, no chat model loaded).* Each run drew four pictures to the end with the marker word `quokkazanzibar` in the prompt: one thrown away by drawing the next, one kept, one with the Maid_marian LoRA kept and then removed, one thrown away by leaving the page; a fifth was cancelled at step 5 with previews on screen. The kept picture was then drawn again from its saved settings and came out identical. Afterwards every file was searched for the marker (UTF-8 and UTF-16, any case) and for three 32-byte pieces of each picture plus two pieces of its base64 text. **Plain data folder** (175 files: database, logs, WebView2 folder): the two thrown-away pictures were found nowhere; the kept picture and the marker were found only in the database's own log file, which is the control that the search finds things. **Locked data folder** (176 files; locked from before its first start): nothing found at all, not the marker, not any picture including the kept one, not the password. In both runs nothing was found in the models folder, the picture engine's folder or the system temp folder (files changed during the run), and the app's log held none of the prompt's words. One file in the system temp folder changed during the plain run and could not be opened: it belongs to the Claude desktop app, which holds it open (made three hours before the run).
  - *Found, and closed the same day (see the entry above this one).* In a **plain** database a picture that was kept and then removed could still be found in the database files (seen in the plain run).
  - *Checked.* `dotnet build` (a full rebuild; the one warning is an old one in a voice test); `dotnet test` (739 pass, 41 new: the WebP header reader with lossy, lossless and extended files and damaged ones; store round trip, lists, deletes, refusals; a drawing turned into a kept picture that sends the engine the same request again; pictures going with a deleted story, character or persona and no others; a database from before this step upgraded with a story, a passage and a character intact, then keeping pictures, with the delete rule working on the old tables; the lock, the four restore cases and the portable move above; the log filter with marker words in every shape tried, real line shapes copied from the engine, languages without spaces, and the ten-minute rule). **Real engine** (`ImageSmokeTests`, with the sample checkpoint and LoRAs read where they are): a picture drawn as WebP with a LoRA, kept in a real database, read back and drawn again from its saved settings gave the same bytes; 154 of the engine's 284 lines passed the filter and none held a prompt word. **In the app**: the two leak-check runs above; a third run across three starts of one data folder showed the kept picture still there after restarts and no Keep controls without the switch. The kept-picture row was looked at in all three looks, dark and light; the test picture as WebP in Workbench dark. No warnings in the app log; no engine left running; the samples unchanged.
  - *Not checked.* Backup and restore of pictures **in the running app** (tests only, through the code the app uses). The published portable build (step 9). A kept picture with a hires fix, a separate VAE or v-prediction drawn again for real (the columns are tested; only Standard 832×1216 pictures were kept and redrawn with the engine). Whether Windows can open WebP for the avatar cropper (step 7 will need it). The log filter with sentence prompts for plain SDXL against the real engine (more ordinary lines will be held back, since sentences share more words with the engine's lines). The engine's verbose level (never switched on; the detail-line rule was tried with made-up lines only). Thousands of pictures in one database (size and speed not measured; each is about 100 to 170 KB). Memory was not examined: a discarded picture lives in DOG.ai's and WebView2's memory until it is replaced, and Windows' page file is outside what DOG.ai controls, as for chat text.
  - *For later steps.* Draw anything that may be kept with `request.ForKeeping()`, hold it in memory, and call `IPictureStore.AddAsync(Picture.Of(...))` only on the owner's **Use** or **Keep**; that call is the only way a picture reaches the disk. Show pictures from memory (a `data:` address, as the check does: nothing of it was found in the WebView2 folder); do not write thumbnails or cache files. Portraits still end up as the card's own PNG through the cropper; the `Pictures` row is what makes **Edit and redraw** possible. A story passage's picture belongs to the message (`PictureOwners.Message`), and `StoryTurn` records which one is shown.
- **M9 step 4, Models → Images (2026-11-14).** The owner can now see their image models, set how each checkpoint draws, and draw a test picture from the tab. Pictures are still not drawn anywhere else in the app (steps 5 to 8).
  - *The folder.* `<models>\image` with `checkpoints`, `loras`, `vae` and `upscalers`, created only when needed (Open folder, a download, a picture). Only `.safetensors` is used; `.ckpt`, `.pt`, `.pth` and `.bin` files are listed as refused ("can carry program code that runs when it is opened") and are never opened.
  - *Recognition* reads only each file's header (1.4 MB for the 6.9 GB checkpoint; the weights are never read) and tells apart: SDXL checkpoint (with its family and v-prediction), SDXL LoRA (and LyCORIS), VAE, upscaler, and what DOG.ai cannot use, said plainly: SD 1.5 checkpoints and LoRAs, Flux and SD3 files, inpainting checkpoints, diffusers-layout parts, damaged files. A file in the wrong folder (a LoRA among checkpoints) is shown with "move it to the loras folder", because the engine reads LoRAs only from there. Every SDXL checkpoint has the same tensors, so the **family is guessed from names** (the file name and the model's own title in its metadata), word by word ("il" counts only as a whole word): NoobAI first, then Pony, then Illustrious, otherwise plain SDXL. The owner can change it on the card.
  - *LoRA trigger words* are read from the file's training tags: tags present in at least 95% of its training pictures, minus counting and figure tags (1girl, solo, furry…), or the maker's declared trigger phrase when there is one. On the samples: AddMicroDetails → `addmicrodetails`; Incase → `icjournals, blocky, painterly`; Maid_marian → `maid_marian`. The card says they are guessed and shows the ten most common other training tags. "Made for Illustrious" is shown when the LoRA's names say so.
  - *Settings cards.* Per checkpoint: family, prompt style (tags or sentences), quality words before and after, negative prompt, sampler, scheduler, steps, guidance, clip skip, normal or v-prediction, VAE (from the vae folder). Per LoRA: trigger words and default strength. **Starting numbers**: Illustrious as in the trial (Euler a, 28 steps, guidance 5.5, clip skip 2, the plan's quality and negative words); NoobAI and NoobAI V-Pred from Laxhar's model cards on Hugging Face (V-Pred: Euler, 30 steps, guidance 4.5; the cards' rating word and furry terms were left out of the negative: the rating comes with Settings → Pictures, the subject is the owner's); Pony from its author's page (via its Hugging Face mirror: the six score words, clip skip 2, Euler a, 25 steps; guidance 7 is the usual value, not from the page); plain SDXL is a usual starting point (sentences, DPM++ 2M Karras, 30 steps, guidance 7). All to be tuned in step 6. The **rating words per family** the plan puts on the card are **not** there yet: they come with Settings → Pictures (step 7).
  - *Where settings are kept, and why:* one JSON document (`image-models`) in the settings store, beside the assistants and prompts. So backups, restore and the lock cover it with no new table (the `Pictures` table is step 5). Files are named by their path inside their own folder, never by a full path, so moving the models folder or a portable install needs nothing from `PortableMove` (a test moves a portable install and checks). **A card is saved only once the owner changes it**: untouched checkpoints keep following their family's defaults, so step 6's better numbers reach them. **Back to the … defaults** forgets the owner's changes.
  - *Drawing.* **Draw with** picks the checkpoint pictures will use (the first one until the owner picks); a LoRA can be added to the test picture. **Draw a test picture** draws a fixed subject (tags or a sentence, matching the card) at 832×1216 through the graphics-card coordinator, with previews, the waiting and making-room stages, Cancel, and a line saying whether the chat model was set aside. The two test buttons on Models → Engine are gone. Opening the tab starts nothing.
  - *Recommended downloads* (checked on Hugging Face 2026-11-14: names, sizes, licences, none gated): Illustrious XL 2.0 (best pick), NoobAI XL 1.1, NoobAI XL V-Pred 1.0, Pony Diffusion V6 XL (its author's own repository; saved under a name that says Pony, as the original file is `v6.safetensors`), SDXL 1.0, the SDXL VAE fp16 fix, and Stability's small offset LoRA (47 MB). They go through the existing download manager with Hugging Face's checksums, and never become chat-model records (that was a real gap: every non-voice download used to). **Open folder** shows the image folder in File Explorer for Civitai files; Civitai stays off the allow-list.
  - *The picture engine moved* from Models → Engine to the end of Models → Images (status, check, install, update); Engine keeps one line and an **Open Images** button. Moved rather than shown in both places because each tab tracked its own install, and two install buttons could have raced on the same folder.
  - *Checked.* `dotnet build`; `dotnet test` (698 pass, 29 new: recognition of every kind from small made-up files, family words and v-prediction markers, trigger words from the three samples' tag lists, damaged headers, a `.ckpt` held locked by another handle still reported as refused (so it was never opened), folder scan with misplaced and refused files, family defaults and their limits, settings round-trip with reset, prompt assembly, the database document holding no full path and surviving a portable move, the recommended downloads' saved names recognised as the right family; plus the real sample files when `DOGAI_TEST_IMAGE_MODEL` and `DOGAI_TEST_IMAGE_LORAS` are set, run and passed). **In the app** (throwaway data and models folders, the samples hard-linked in and the links removed afterwards; samples unchanged; the picture engine copy installed in step 2's walkthrough through `DOGAI_SD_SERVER`; graphics memory free, no chat model loaded): the checkpoint shown as "SDXL checkpoint, Illustrious" with Illustrious defaults; the three LoRAs with the trigger words above; a LoRA linked into the checkpoints folder shown with "move it to the loras folder"; a `.ckpt` shown as refused; a test picture drawn from the tab in 11.1 s (first, with loading), then with Maid_marian in 9.5 s, both looked at; a changed step count saved and shown as "your settings" after leaving and reopening the tab. The tab was shown in all three looks, dark and light, but not every part in every look; what was looked at: Workbench dark: the top with the lists, the "other files" (misplaced and refused), and both drawn pictures; Workbench light: the checkpoint card, before and after the fix; Fireside dark: the top and the checkpoint card, before and after the fix; Fireside light: the downloads, folder and engine box; Playhouse dark: the top and the LoRA card with its training tags; Playhouse light: the checkpoint card and the downloads. One layout flaw was found and fixed (a dropdown stretched beside a field that has a note under it). Going online and checking for the engine from the tab worked; it said the newest release's Windows files were not up yet, and that was true: release master-1098 came out at 09:33:04 UTC, its CUDA file finished uploading at 09:33:45 and the check ran at about 09:33:55 (both files are there now, under names the updater matches). Nothing was installed. The Engine tab seen without the test buttons. No warnings in the app log; no engine left running.
  - *Not checked.* **No download was made** (none asked for): a real download into the image folder and its "Installed" state are untested in the app. Open folder was not clicked (it opens File Explorer). **Cancel** on the test picture was not clicked (the same cancel as step 3's, which was tried). The **waiting** and **making room** messages were never on screen, because no chat model was loaded. The Install button on the Images tab was not seen (the check happened in the minutes when the newest release had no Windows files). No v-prediction checkpoint, separate VAE, LyCORIS, SD 1.5 or Flux file was tried for real (only made-up headers). The family defaults for NoobAI, Pony and SDXL were not drawn with. The tab with a chat model loaded (the coordinator's swap from this tab) was not tried; the coordinator is the same as in step 3. The checkpoint picker's choice is not used by anything else yet.
- **M9 step 3, sharing the graphics card (2026-11-14).** The chat model and the picture model now take turns on the graphics card by themselves. There is still nothing to draw with in the app (steps 4 to 8); what the owner can see is the chat model marked "set aside" after a picture and coming back with the next message.
  - *What exists.* `CardCoordinator` (Core/Images) is the picture engine the whole app gets (`IImageEngine`); the real `ImageServer` sits behind it, so no picture can be drawn without it.
