Story settings live in their own table; choices, state, dice rolls and chapter numbers are kept with each message.
  - *Choices come from a second request, not from the passage itself.* The passage streams as ordinary prose (better writing, and you can read it as it arrives); then the same prompt plus the passage is sent again with a JSON schema for the choices and state. The engine reuses the prompt it has just processed, so this takes a second or two.
  - *The narrator is reminded of the rules next to your newest action* (length, do not ask what the player does, the dice result, the current state), because models follow what is nearest the end. A closing "What do you do?" that slips through anyway is removed from the saved passage, since the choices are right below it.
  - *Rewriting a selection is held to the selection's size:* the request states the length in words, the reply is capped in tokens, and the temperature is lowered. The first live try, without this, turned one sentence into a page.
  - *Rewriting a selection changes the passage in place* (after you accept it). Rewriting a whole passage keeps the old version beside it.
  - *Going back never deletes.* In a written story, writing on from an earlier passage makes the later text another version; the arrows on the direction line switch between them.
  - *A turn that got no passage* (a problem, or stopped at once) is replaced by the next one rather than followed by it, so two user turns never go to the model in a row.
  - *New stories start with the Creative style.* Dice are off and state tracking is on unless changed.
- **Tried live** with Mag-Mell 12B (ChatML template): an adventure from a one-line idea through bible draft, opening, choices, a typed action with dice, state updates, the branch map (going back and branching), other choices, editing the state by hand (it was still there after leaving and returning), continue, and hand-editing a passage; a written story through bible and six-chapter outline draft, three passages with a direction and a chapter change, rewriting a selection four ways, rewriting a passage, editing the outline afterwards, and all three exports through the real Save dialog. All story screens (landing, new story, adventure, branch map, written story with the selection bar, rewrite dialog, story bible page) were looked at in all three looks, dark and light.
- **What the live tries showed about the model, not fixed by code:** passages run about 350–450 words when asked for 250. The dice result was followed in two of three rolls after the wording was strengthened (a failed roll was once still narrated as a success). Rewrites of a selection sometimes drift into the sentence that follows. A stronger model should do better on all three.
- **Left out of M4, still owed:**
  - *Hierarchical summaries* (scene → chapter → story so far). There is one rolling "story so far" summary, the same mechanism as chat memory. It was not run live in a story (unit-tested).
  - *Lorebooks and document collections as world reference.* Standalone lorebooks are still owed from M3; documents are M5. The bible's World field is the place for world facts until then.
  - ~~*Narrator read-aloud* comes with voice in M6.~~ Done in M6.
  - *Scene beats as their own level.* The outline is chapters, each with a free-text plan; beats can be written into the plan.
  - A past action or direction cannot be edited; go back and do something else instead. The branch map is for adventures only.
  - Not tried: opening the EPUB in an e-reader (its structure and well-formedness are unit-tested), a strict-template model (Gemma, Mistral `[INST]`; role alternation is unit-tested), a reasoning model, and a story long enough to need the summary.
- **What M3 part 3 delivered (group chat):**
  - *Group chats* live in the Characters section, under "Group chats" in the list, with a **New group** button (shown once there are two characters). Pick two or more characters and a turn order, then start. A group keeps its members in its own table; deleting a character takes it out of groups but keeps the chats and what it said ("Removed character").
  - *Turn order*, changeable at any time in the side panel: **Natural** (whoever your message names answers, by full or first name, in the order named; if nobody is named, whoever has been quiet longest), **Everyone in turn** (every member who is not muted answers once, in list order; Stop ends the whole round), **You choose** (nobody answers until you pick). Under the message box a row of member buttons makes one of them speak now, and **Anyone** lets turn order pick (someone the last speaker named, else the quietest, never the one who just spoke). Members can be reordered, muted (they stay in the scene but only speak when picked), removed and added.
  - *Each speaker gets its own prompt:* its own card (and its own main instruction, if the card has one) with a group version of the default instruction ("…never write the words or actions of {{user}} or of Ann and Bo"), a short note on every other member (their description, cut to about 700 characters, each card's {{char}} filled with its own name), lore from every member's book, the chat's scene/instructions, author's note and memory.
  - *Other characters' replies are named in the history* ("Ann: …"). Replies from several characters in a row are joined into one assistant turn, and the prompt always ends with a short user turn "[Write Bo's next reply.]", so roles strictly alternate (strict templates such as Gemma and Mistral refuse anything else, and llama-server would treat a trailing assistant turn as text to continue). A leading "Bo:" the model copies is removed from the reply, and writing stops if a line starts with another member's or the user's name.
  - *Regenerate, swipe and continue keep the same speaker.* The opening message's alternatives are every member's greetings, each member's V3 `group_only_greetings` first, so the arrows choose who opens the scene and how. One-to-one chats never use group-only greetings. The card editor has a new **Group-only greetings** section; they are saved inside the card and exported.
  - *Memory and "Write mine"* work in groups: summaries label each line with its speaker; the draft is told not to speak for any member.
  - The side-panel settings shared by character and group chats (persona, style, instructions, author's note, memory) moved into one component, `RoleplayPanels`.
  - Fixed on the way: the summary request was built with `string.Format`, which turned `{{char}}` into `{char}`, so the names were never filled in (the model was asked about "the story so far between {char} and {user}"). It also said "write an summary".
  - Database: one additive migration (a `TurnOrder` column and a new `ChatMembers` table). A test upgrades a part 2 database.
- **How M3 part 3 behaves, decided while building it:**
  - *One opening slot, not one greeting per member.* Chaining a greeting from each member would hang the second greeting under one version of the first, so flipping the first would hide it. Others join once the user writes, or when picked.
  - *Muted members do not answer even when named*; pressing their button is the way to hear them.
  - *A group chat is one chat*, not a reusable "group" with several chats. **New chat** in the side panel starts a fresh chat with the same members, order, mutes and turn order.
  - *Lore budget in a group* is the largest budget any member's book sets, at most a quarter of the context.
- **Tried live** with Mag-Mell 12B (ChatML template) and three test characters: naming Pip made Pip answer; with "Everyone in turn" all three answered in list order, each in character, none writing another's lines; the copied "Name:" prefixes were removed; regenerating kept the same speaker. The prompt inspector showed the alternating prompt described above. Screens checked in all three looks, dark and light (group chat and the new-group form). **Not tried:** a model with a strict template (Gemma, Mistral `[INST]`; none on this PC, so alternation is guarded by unit tests only), the member up/down/mute/add buttons and the group-only greetings section of the card editor were seen on screen but not clicked through, and "Write mine" and memory were not run live in a group (unit-tested).
- **What M3 part 2 delivered:**
  - *Prompt in parts.* The system message is now built from parts: instruction, lore placed before the card, card and persona, lore placed after the card, example dialogue, the chat's own instructions, and the memory summary (`PromptLayout`, Core). Older turns are trimmed first. If even the newest message does not fit, example dialogue goes next, then memory, then lore; the instruction and card always stay. The card's final instruction and the author's note go *inside user turns*, never as an extra system message, because strict chat templates refuse a system message anywhere but first.
  - *Lore.* The card's embedded lorebook is used. Recent messages are searched for each entry's keywords (whole words; `/pattern/` keys are regular expressions with a 50 ms limit, since cards are untrusted). Entries can need a second keyword, be always on, match capitals exactly, or bring in other entries. The lore budget is the book's own, at most a quarter of the context. A **Lore** page (button on the character's page) edits entries; they are saved back into the card, so export gives them to other apps with unknown fields intact. **Import lore…** reads SillyTavern World Info files or another card's book. The prompt inspector lists the entries used.
  - *Author's note* per chat in the side panel, placed with the newest message or 1–8 messages back (default 4).
  - *"Write mine"* (impersonate) next to Send: the model drafts your next message into the box for you to edit. Nothing is saved.
  - *Memory.* When older messages stop fitting, they are summarised (in chunks if needed) and the summary is sent in their place. The summary runs a few messages ahead of what is trimmed so it is not rewritten after every reply. The side panel shows what it covers, lets you correct it, switch memory off, or forget it. The inspector shows when a summary was used.
  - *AI card builder.* On the new-character page, describe a character in a sentence and the loaded model fills in every field. The engine is asked for JSON matching a schema (structured output), so the answer always parses. The draft is not saved until you press Create.
  - *Avatar cropping.* After choosing a picture (characters and personas) a cropper shows the square used in lists and chats: drag it, move it with the arrow keys, size it with the slider, and see a round preview. Characters keep the full picture, so **Crop** can change it later; personas keep only the avatar, so they are cropped when the picture is chosen.
  - Database: one additive migration (new columns only). Chats from M3 part 1 get note depth 4 and memory on; a test upgrades a part 1 database.
- **How M3 part 2 behaves, decided while building it:**
  - *The summary belongs to one branch.* It records the last message it covers and is only used while that message is on the branch shown. Switching to another greeting or an earlier edit means a new summary is written when needed, replacing the old one (one summary per chat). Editing a reply's text in place does not mark the summary out of date.
  - *Bad summaries are refused.* Small models sometimes repeat the request back, loop ("User 2: User 2: …"), or run to the length limit. Such text is thrown away rather than sent with every later reply, and the next try waits a few messages. The request sits after the messages, where small models follow it best, with repetition penalties on.
  - *Impersonate writes inside an open user turn* (raw completion from where the user's turn starts in the model's own template), which makes the model speak as the user much more reliably than asking it to. If the chat already ends with an unanswered message of yours, it asks instead. The text the template puts after a user's message is used as a stop string (and the draft is cut there too), so on templates whose turn end is ordinary text, like Mistral's `[/INST]`, the draft cannot run on into the character's reply; that case is unit-tested only.
  - *Lore matches whole words* so "cat" does not fire on "category". A keyword containing a comma cannot be typed in the editor (commas separate keywords); imported ones are kept.
  - *Lore is searched in the last 4 messages* unless the book says otherwise (SillyTavern's default is 2).
- **Tried live** with two local role-play models: Self After Dark 8B (Llama 3 template) and Mag-Mell 12B (ChatML template). Lore fired and was listed, the note and final instruction landed in user turns, the card builder produced a full card, cropping saved the chosen square, and memory worked in a 4,096-token context (after a fix: the first run left 35 messages out but summarised only 31, because the summary's own size pushed out more turns; now the check is repeated with the summary in place, and the final run covered 41 with 37 left out). The 12B model wrote good summaries and good "Write mine" drafts. The 8B model did not: it echoed the request back or looped when summarising, and in a fresh chat its draft was a placeholder. That is what the refusal checks above guard against. **Not tried:** a model with a strict template (Mistral `[INST]`, Gemma), a reasoning model summarising, and a SillyTavern World Info file from the wild (the import is tested with a sample).
- **Left out of M3 part 2, still owed:** standalone lorebooks shared between characters or attached to a chat (lore lives in each card for now; World Info import copies entries into a card); the SillyTavern "character's note" (`extensions.depth_prompt`) is kept on export but not used.
- **What M3 part 1 delivered:**
  - *Characters section.* Import Character Card V1/V2/V3 from PNG or JSON; export as a PNG card (V2 and V3 inside, as other apps expect) or as V3 JSON. Fields DOG.ai does not edit (extensions, the embedded lorebook, V3-only fields) are kept and written back unchanged.
  - *Card editor* with every standard field, extra greetings, a picture, a style for new chats, and a live token count (exact when a model is loaded).
  - *Personas:* who you are in a chat (name, description, picture). One is the default; each chat can switch.
  - *Character chat:* greetings are alternatives you flip through with the arrows; the last reply always shows arrows, and the right arrow past the newest writes another version (swipe). The prompt inspector shows the full card text sent.
  - *Continue* (General and Characters) carries on the last reply from where it stopped. *Styles* can now be copied and edited ("Edit styles…" under Style); the four built-in ones stay fixed.
  - The model picker labels and lists first the default model for the mode you are in.
  - `tools/walkthrough.ps1` can now fill in Windows open/save dialogs, so import and export are tested in the real app.
- **How M3 part 1 behaves, decided while building it:**
  - *Continue is hidden on a reply that has only "thinking" text* (stopped before any answer): the raw continuation cannot keep thinking apart from the answer.
  - *Continue changes the reply in place* rather than adding a version beside it: it is the same reply made longer. The raw prompt (template up to the reply, then its text so far) goes to the engine's `/completion`, so the model carries on mid-sentence.
  - *Greeting first:* a character chat's prompt starts with a short user turn, "[Start a new chat]", before the greeting, because strict chat templates (Mistral `[INST]`, Gemma) refuse a conversation that opens with a reply. Tried with real models using the ChatML and Llama 3 templates, which accept it; no model with a strict template was on this PC to try. (Tried in M19 step 1 with a Gemma 2 model, whose template refuses a system message and anything but user and reply by turns: accepted, in one-to-one and group chats.)
  - *Names in greetings are filled in when the chat starts.* Switching persona later changes what the model is told, but greetings already shown keep the old name.
  - *The card's "final instruction"* (post-history instructions) went at the end of the system message in part 1; since part 2 it goes with the newest user message.
  - *Pictures* are converted to PNG on import (so any picture can carry a card on export) and a 256-pixel avatar is cut from the top-centre square. There is no manual crop yet.
  - *No database links* from chats to characters and personas: adding them would make SQLite rebuild the chats table when upgrading. The store deletes a character's chats and clears a deleted persona itself. Upgrading an M2 database was tested.
- **Left out of M3 part 1, still owed:** everything listed under Next. Not tried live: a card with a JPEG or WebP picture (the code converts them through Windows, but only PNG cards were imported in the app).
- **What M2 delivered:** a Models section with five parts. *Recommended* shows researched picks per use case with a fit badge and the best file for this PC pre-selected. *Browse* searches Hugging Face, shows each repository's files with fit badges and the model card. *Library* lists models from the models folder and any added folders, and can load, benchmark, delete, or make one the default per mode. *Downloads* queues downloads, pauses and resumes them, checks free space first and verifies every file's SHA-256 before using it. *Engine* installs and updates DOG.ai's own CUDA build of llama.cpp. The side panel shows hardware, graphics memory, the context size used for fit, and an optional Hugging Face token stored with Windows DPAPI. The status bar now shows tokens per second and graphics memory, and an unused model unloads itself after 30 minutes.
- **How M2 behaves, decided while building it:**
  - *Going online* is a button in the Models section, off at start. Leaving the section goes offline, unless a download or engine install is running; then it goes offline when that finishes. "Go offline" pauses running downloads.
  - *Fit* adds the model file, the memory for the chosen context (read from the model's header where possible), and working memory, then compares with free graphics memory. Memory held by the currently loaded model counts as free, since loading another model releases it. A partial fit gives a layer count, which loading now uses instead of "all layers".
  - *Too large* models are refused before anything is unloaded, with a message saying how much memory they need and how much is free. A benchmark's proven settings override the estimate for the same context size. If the header cannot be read, the model is loaded anyway and the engine decides.
  - *Benchmark* loads the model with the estimated layer count, steps down if loading fails, measures, and saves the layers and speed for later loads. It tunes GPU layers only; it does not change the context size.
  - *Stable engine releases*: llama.cpp now marks numbered builds as pre-releases and publishes a stable release that names one build (b12110 on 2026-11-12). DOG.ai installs that build, picking the newest CUDA version the driver supports (CUDA 13.4 here) plus its runtime.
  - *Curated picks* are in `src/DogAI.Core/Models/curated-models.json` (researched 2026-11-12). There is no "refresh" button yet: the only allowed hosts are Hugging Face and GitHub, and there is no online copy of the list. Publishing the JSON in a GitHub repo would make it refreshable. Uncensored role-play fine-tunes were left out of the defaults; they can be found through Browse. Voice picks come with M6.
- **Left out of M2, still owed:**
  - Per-mode defaults are saved and the model picker labels the current mode's default (General, Characters and, since M4, Story). Nothing loads a default automatically yet.
  - ~~Links in model cards do not open.~~ Done in M7: every web link asks first, then opens in the user's browser.
  - Not tried live: a gated model with a token, partial offload of a model bigger than the card, and the 30-minute idle unload. Unit tests cover the logic.
  - The download resume and checksum tests use an in-process fake server, not a real local web server as the Verification section suggests.
  - Model cards written in HTML have their tags removed (the text stays) so they read cleanly; images show as links, since remote images are blocked.
  - The real data folder still uses the winget engine until Install is clicked once in Models → Engine.
- **What M1 delivered:** the app starts and stops `llama-server` itself (found from winget for now), loads any GGUF from the models folder or picked by hand, and General mode has streaming chat with stop, regenerate, edit-and-branch, version switching, markdown with code copy buttons, collapsed "thinking", four style presets, per-chat instructions, a context meter with automatic trimming of old turns, and a prompt inspector. Chats are saved in SQLite as a message tree.
- **Left out of M1 on purpose, still owed by a later milestone:**
  - ~~"Continue" a stopped reply, and editing your own sampler presets.~~ Done in M3 part 1.
  - ~~Syntax colouring and LaTeX in replies.~~ Done in M7 (highlight.js and KaTeX bundled under `DogAI.UI/wwwroot/vendor`).
  - ~~Idle auto-unload, VRAM readout and GPU-layer tuning.~~ Done in M2.
  - ~~The winget `llama-server` is a Vulkan build.~~ M2's Engine tab installs the CUDA build.
- **Decided after this plan was first written:**
  - The app ships **three switchable looks** instead of one: Workbench (default), Fireside and Playhouse, each in light and dark, chosen in Settings. See `CLAUDE.md` for how they are built. The "Console" proposal was dropped.
  - The logo and Windows icon are the **D-Dog** mark (`src/DogAI.App/Assets/icon.svg`).
  - Every new screen is checked in all six look/theme combinations as part of its milestone.
  - Original mockups are kept for reference in `design/proposals.html` and `design/icons.html`. Model names in them are placeholders.

## Context

You want a private, almost fully offline Windows desktop app for running local LLMs, maintainable in VS 2022, with three distinct modes (General, Character chat, Story) plus one online section for browsing and downloading models with hardware-aware recommendations.

`C:\Claude\DOG-AI` is empty and not a git repo, so this is a greenfield build.

**Your machine (measured):** RTX 4090 (24 GB VRAM), Core Ultra 9 285K (24 cores), 32 GB RAM, VS 2022 Pro 17.14 with the .NET desktop workload, .NET SDK 9.0.320. `D:` is empty (931 GB free); `C:` has 184 GB free.

**Decisions you made:** WPF + Blazor Hybrid UI; app-managed `llama-server` engine; extras = chat with documents, voice in/out. Image understanding and image generation are out of scope. *(Changed 2026-11-13: image generation is now planned as M9.)*

**Decisions I made (say so if you disagree):**
- **Target `net9.0-windows`** (since M8: `net9.0-windows10.0.19041.0` for the app, the same .NET 9 with access to Windows Hello). It is the newest framework VS 2022 supports. .NET 10 needs VS 2026; the code will move over with a one-line change if you upgrade.
- **Models live in `D:\DOG-AI\Models`** by default (configurable). App data (database, cards, settings) in `%LOCALAPPDATA%\DOG.ai`.
- **Two online touchpoints, not one:** Hugging Face (models, voices) and GitHub releases of llama.cpp (engine updates). Both live in the same Models section and nothing else in the app can reach the network.

## Architecture

Solution `DogAI.sln`, seven projects:

| Project | Role |
|---|---|
| `src/DogAI.App` | WPF host window, `BlazorWebView`, DI startup, tray/hotkeys, WebView2 lockdown |
| `src/DogAI.UI` | Razor class library: pages, components, CSS, bundled JS (highlight.js, KaTeX), no CDN |
| `src/DogAI.Core` | Domain models and logic with no UI or I/O dependencies: prompt builder, context budgeter, card parsing, lorebook matching, memory, story engine |
| `src/DogAI.Engine` | `llama-server` process manager, streaming client, GGUF header parser, hardware probe, fit estimator, voice engines |
| `src/DogAI.Data` | EF Core + SQLite, migrations, repositories, FTS5 search, vector storage |
| `src/DogAI.Hub` | **The only assembly allowed to open outbound connections**: Hugging Face client, download manager, engine updater |
| `tests/DogAI.Tests` | xUnit tests |

**Engine.** One `llama-server` child process per loaded model, bound to `127.0.0.1` on a random port, plus a small second instance for embeddings. The app talks to it over its HTTP API: streaming chat completions, `/tokenize` for exact token counts, `/apply-template` + `/completion` for continue/prefill, and JSON-schema-constrained output for structured results. Chat templates come from the GGUF itself. Idle auto-unload frees VRAM. On first run the app can adopt the `llama-server` you already have from winget until its own copy is downloaded.

**Offline guarantee (enforced, not just promised).**
- All `HttpClient` use sits behind one gateway in `DogAI.Hub`, gated by an "Online" switch that is off by default and only turns on inside the Models section.
- WebView2 is restricted to the app's own origin; any other request is blocked and logged. Links open in your browser only after a confirmation.
- Imported card and document text is treated as untrusted: markdown renders with raw HTML disabled.
- No telemetry, no update checks outside the Models section.

**Data.** SQLite. Messages are stored as a tree (each has a parent), which gives regenerate, swipes, edit-and-branch, and CYOA branching from one mechanism. Embeddings are stored as blobs with in-memory cosine search, which is plenty for single-user scale.

## Features

### Shared across all modes
- Streaming replies with stop, regenerate, continue, edit any message, branch from any point
- Sampler presets (temperature, min-p, top-p/k, DRY, XTC, repetition penalty), per mode and per character
- **Context budgeter**: exact token counting with priority trimming (system > card > lore > memory > recent turns) and a live context meter
- **Prompt inspector**: view the exact text sent to the model for any reply
- Collapsed display of reasoning ("thinking") output for reasoning models
- Tokens/sec and VRAM readout; quick model switcher in the title bar
- Full-text search across all chats and stories; backup/restore to a single archive; dark and light themes

### 1. General mode
- Assistants: reusable system-prompt profiles (coder, editor, tutor, etc.) with their own model and preset
- Markdown, syntax-highlighted code with copy button, LaTeX, tables
- Folders, pinning, auto-titling, export to Markdown
- Prompt library with fill-in variables
- **Chat with documents:** drop PDF, DOCX, text, markdown, or code files into a chat or a reusable "collection". Local embedding model finds relevant passages; answers cite the source passage, which opens on click.

### 2. Character mode
- **Import** Character Card V2 and V3 from PNG (embedded metadata) and JSON, including embedded lorebooks and alternate greetings. **Export** back to PNG/JSON.
- **Card editor** with all standard fields, avatar cropping, tags, and a live token count
- **AI card builder**: describe a character in a sentence and the loaded model drafts the full card for you to edit
- User personas (who *you* are in the chat), switchable per chat
- Lorebooks / world info: keyword-triggered entries with a token budget, attachable to characters or chats
- Swipes on every reply, alternate greetings, author's note, impersonate (model drafts your turn)
- **Long-term memory**: rolling summary of older turns plus vector recall of relevant past messages, so long chats stay coherent past the context window
- Group chats with multiple characters and turn-order control
- Per-character voice for read-aloud

### 3. Story mode
- **Story Bible**: premise, genre, tone, POV, tense, cast, world facts, style guide. Always in context and editable mid-story. Can be AI-drafted from a one-line idea.
- **Authored stories**: outline → chapters → scene beats; "write next" with optional steering; select text to rewrite, expand, shorten, or change tone
- **Interactive (CYOA)**:
  - Narrator turns end in 2–4 choices produced by schema-constrained output, so choices are always well-formed, plus a free-text "do something else"
  - Optional tracked state (inventory, stats, location, flags) the model updates each turn and you can edit
  - Optional dice checks for outcomes
  - **Branch map**: a visual tree of every path taken; click any node to rewind or explore an alternative
- Hierarchical summaries (scene → chapter → story so far) to keep long stories consistent
- Lorebooks and document collections usable as world reference
- Narrator read-aloud
- Export to Markdown, HTML, and EPUB

### 4. Models section (the online part)
- **Hardware profile**: GPU, VRAM (total and currently free), RAM, CPU, disk space per drive
- **Browse and search** Hugging Face GGUF repos with sort by trending/downloads/recent, model card view, and per-file listing
- **Fit badge on every file**, computed from file size, layer count, and the context length you want:
  - Fits fully on GPU (fast)
  - Partial offload (slower, with an estimate)
  - Too large
- Per repo, the best quantization for your hardware is pre-selected
- **Curated picks** per use case (General, Roleplay, Story, Embedding, Voice) for your VRAM tier. Shipped as a JSON file, refreshable when online. I'll research the current model landscape when building this list rather than rely on memory.
- **Download manager**: queue, pause/resume, SHA-256 verification, free-space check, optional Hugging Face token for gated models (stored encrypted with Windows DPAPI)
- **Library**: installed models with metadata read from the GGUF, add existing GGUF folders, delete, set defaults per mode
- **Benchmark** after download: measures real tokens/sec and auto-tunes GPU layers and context size
- **Engine updater**: installs/updates the CUDA build of llama.cpp

### Voice
- **Speech-to-text**: Whisper via `Whisper.net`, push-to-talk and hands-free
- **Text-to-speech**: neural voices (Piper/Kokoro) via `sherpa-onnx`, spoken sentence by sentence as the reply streams; Windows built-in voices as a zero-download fallback
- Audio via `NAudio`. Voice models download through the Models section.

## Design gate (completed; outcome is in Status above)

No styling, navigation, or page layout gets built until you have picked a look. This happens in the middle of M0, right after the bare shell proves the WPF + Blazor pipeline runs.

- I produce **four distinct design directions** as clickable HTML mockups. Because the app's UI is HTML/CSS, the chosen mockup carries straight over into the real app.
- Each direction differs in both **aesthetic** (palette, typography, density, how much "DOG" personality the branding has) and **layout** (for example: left icon rail with mode workspaces, top tabs, a three-pane layout with a persistent context panel, a home-screen launcher that opens each mode full-window).
- Each mockup shows the same four screens so they compare fairly: General chat, Character chat, Story (CYOA turn with choices and branch map), and the Models browser with fit badges. Dark and light variants of each.
- You pick one, or mix parts ("layout of B, colours of D"). I do one revision round on the pick.
- The result is frozen as a set of design tokens (CSS variables) and a shell layout in `DogAI.UI`, which every later milestone builds on. Later screens that the mockups did not cover get a quick mockup for your approval before they are built.

## M22: Joined clips, and the helper's animals (planned with the owner 2026-11-18)

**What it delivers.**

1. **Join with the clip before.** A button on a clip that continues another (made with Continue the clip): it puts the two into one file that plays through, 10 seconds long. The joined clip is a new clip beside the two; nothing is replaced. Continued and joined once more it is 15 seconds, and that is the most.
2. **A joined clip is kept, played, saved and continued like any clip.** Kept videos shows its length; its large view says how many clips it holds and that its sentence is the last part's.
3. **The video helper writes a plain animal as that animal.** Today it copies the form's own example ("an anthropomorphic grey wolf") onto foxes, horses, dogs and eagles, and gives a horse a rider. One rule is reworded and the new wording is chosen by clips, as the forms were.

**What is there today (read 2026-11-18).** Continue the clip makes a second clip beside the first; `VideoContinue`'s own comment says "the two are not joined". The last frame held (`VideoFrame`, `Continues` true) does not know which clip it ended, and a try (`VideoTry`) does not know what it continues. `WebmInfo` walks a file by structure and gives back five facts (size, frames, length, writer); it hands out no positions and nothing writes a WebM. `VideoClips.Problem(webm, width, height, frames)` demands exactly the frames asked for, `VideoRequest.MaxFrames` is 121, and `Clip.Problems()` checks the kept video against the row's own `Frames`. `Clips` has one request per row and ends `Faster` … `Still`, `StartFrame`, `Video`; lists read the still and never the video, so a list cannot tell a clip's length from its file. `VideoClips.MaxBytes` is 96 MB (a Better clip is 5 to 14 MB). `video.js` `lastFrame` seeks to the file's own length; its fallback is (frames − 1) ÷ fps from the row. The strip holds four tries. The helper's rule is the last line of `VideoWriter.SentenceRules`, shared by both forms: `An animal-like character (…) is written as what they are, "an anthropomorphic grey wolf", never as a person with animal ears.` No projector for Huihui is on this PC (M21 deleted it after its checks).

**Settled already** (follows from earlier milestones and from M21's trial; not up for choice):

- Offline as ever. **Joining sends nothing to any engine**: it is done in DOG.ai's own memory, by structure, with nothing decoded and nothing encoded. No request ever asks for more than 121 frames; `VideoRequest`, the request body and both programs' start switches are not touched (the pinned tests stay as they are, and a plain clip's request stays byte for byte M20's).
- No end frame, no loop, never `end_image`. Images still sends no input picture. Faster stays off by default.
- A joined clip that is not kept is in memory only, as every clip. The clip before, when it is held for joining, is held in memory only.
- **Every joined file is checked before it is shown or kept**, by the reader that checks the engine's files (`WebmInfo`: one VP8 track, no tags, no words but the two program names, no part it does not know), and besides: the right number of frames, and every frame the parts' own bytes in order. A file that fails is refused in words and never shown.
- A new column on `Clips` is a table rebuild by hand, as `ClipsFaster` did it (drop the three triggers, copy, carry `sqlite_sequence`, rename, index, triggers again), the three large values last. Lock, backup, restore, portable move and an upgrade from M21's build (`b9ad2ee`) are checked.
- **The helper's forms change only with a clip trial**, criteria written before the first look, the helper's first answer taken each time.
- Nothing is downloaded without asking, each file by name and size. All three looks, dark and light; an id for every new control; `CLAUDE.md` updated in the last step.

**Decided by the owner (2026-11-18):**

1. **A joined clip is a new clip beside the others.** On Make it is a new clip in the strip; Keep writes a new kept row. The clips it was made from stay as they are, kept or not (the rule Upscale has in Images). Known cost: keeping both the original and the joined clip stores the first five seconds twice.
2. **At most three clips in one file, 15 seconds** (a clip and two continuations: as far as M21's trial called the picture usable).
3. **Make one like this on a joined clip puts back the last part** (its sentence, settings, seed and first frame) and says that Draw makes the last five seconds again, not the whole clip. The earlier parts' sentences, seeds and first frames are not kept with a joined clip.
4. **Save as a file writes the joined file only.** While the parts are still in the strip on Make, each has its own Save as a file there.
5. **The helper's "anthropomorphic" fix is in M22, as a step of its own** (recorded in `f5c3a11`).

**Defaults chosen here (say so to change one):**

1. **Joining is a button, never automatic, and only on the Make tab:** "Join with the clip before" on a clip that was made from the last frame of another clip in this run of DOG.ai (a clip in the strip, or a kept clip that Continue the clip was pressed on). That is the only pair DOG.ai knows to belong together. **A continuation that was kept without being joined cannot be joined later**: the kept row does not remember what it continued.
2. **The repeated frame is left out.** A continuation's first frame is the clip before's last frame over again, so the joined file has 241 frames for two clips and 361 for three, and is 10.0 and 15.0 seconds long.
3. **Only clips of the same size join.** A Quick clip continued at Better is not offered, and the page says why. Faster and plain parts join. A joined clip's facts (quality, Faster, seed, sentence) are its last part's.
4. **The join is left as it is and said in a line:** at each join the picture softens in one step and jumps a little. Nothing smooths it (that needs an encoder).
5. **One new column on kept clips, "how many clips the file holds"** (1 on every clip kept before), so Kept videos can say "10 s" without reading every video and the check on a kept clip stays exact. It is a rebuild of the `Clips` table, as M21's was.
6. **A clip of three parts can still be continued** (a new clip beside it, as today); joining that one is refused in words.
7. **The saved file is named `video-<seed>-joined.webm`**, so it does not collide with the last part saved by itself.
8. **Join is off while a clip is being made** (it takes well under a second otherwise).
9. **The helper's trial uses first frames drawn in Images** (an ordinary wolf, an anthro wolf), no photograph. For the frame ideas the chat model must see: **Huihui's projector is asked for at the start of that step, by name and size** (`Huihui-Qwen3.5-9B-abliterated.mmproj-f16.gguf`, 918,166,016 bytes; deleted afterwards, as in M21). Without the owner's yes the step does the ideas from words only and says the frame form was not checked.
10. **The helper's bar**, written here before any look. *Counted in text first* (ten answers an idea): a plain animal called "anthropomorphic" in none of 40 from words and none of 10 from a frame; nothing added that the words do not name (rider, saddle, bridle, collar, clothes) in none; the animal's colour not taken from the form's example; the wolf in a jacket still anthropomorphic in at least 8 of 10; "a cat girl with white hair" a girl with cat ears and white hair in at least 8 of 10. *Then clips* (Quick with Faster, the old form's sentence against the new form's, the same seed, read as sheets): the new form's clip no worse in any pair, and in every plain-animal clip the animal is an animal; M21 step 4's six ideas made again both ways, no worse in any; then the new form alone on a second seed set. Under the bar the wording changes and the round is repeated; after three rounds under the bar the owner is asked.

**Steps, and which model to use for each.** One step per session, each ending in a build, passing tests, a look at the app (where there is a screen), this plan's Status updated and a commit, then the hand-over.

| # | Step | Delivers | Model / effort |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Core and storage** (no screen) | **The writer:** `WebmInfo` hands out a file's parts in order from its one walk (header, facts, track, each frame with its time and bytes; not a second reader). `WebmJoin` (Core/Video) makes one file of two: the first file's header and track as they are, the facts with the new length, the first file's frames, then the second's from its second frame on with each time raised by the first's last frame's time, a new cue list (one point a frame), the seek index and every size written anew. It refuses in words files of different size, time unit or track number, and anything `WebmInfo` refuses. It takes a joined file as the first (two, then three). **The check:** `VideoClips` takes a number of parts (`MaxParts` 3; frames = parts × (frames − 1) + 1); a joined file must pass `WebmInfo.Read`, have that many frames, hold every frame of its parts byte for byte in order, and stay under `MaxBytes`. The engine's own clips are still held to exactly the frames asked. **The session:** the last frame held knows the clip it ended (a try's own clip, or a kept clip by id); a try knows what it continues and how many parts it holds; joining adds a new try beside (never changes one), refused in words when the clip before is gone, another size, would make four, or a clip is being made; a joined try remembers what its first part continued, so three clips made one after another join in two presses. **Storage:** migration `ClipsParts` rebuilds `Clips` by hand (`Parts`, 1 by default, after `Faster`; `Still`, `StartFrame`, `Video` last), `Down` with SQLite's own `DROP COLUMN`; `Clip.Parts`, `Clip.Of`, `Problems()`, the store's lists; `ToRequest()` unchanged (the last part's); `VideoLike` and `VideoContinue` on a joined row, the words for both in `VideoPanels`; the last frame's time for a joined clip. **Tests:** the writer on made-up files with a mark in every frame (order, times, cue positions, seek index, length, two and three parts, every refusal); the kept check; the session; the store on M21's schema (upgrade and back, the old table's pages wiped, locked, backup and restore, portable). **Real files, once, by hand** (a scratch program over `VideoServer`, the last frames read by a page as `video.js` reads them, as in M21 step 1): a Quick clip and two continuations, one of them Faster, and a Better pair; each joined file played in a page to its end, its length read, sought to twelve places, and its frames compared with its parts' frames. Throwaway folders in `D:\dogai-b-data`. | Fable 5.2, high |
| 2 | **Make and Kept videos** | **Make:** `#video-join` on a clip that continues another, `#video-join-why` when it cannot (the words from step 1); the joined clip appears in the strip and is shown; its facts say "2 clips joined · 10 s"; the line about the join; Keep, Save as a file (`video-<seed>-joined.webm`), Continue the clip on it. **Kept videos:** a joined clip's tile says its length; its large view says how many clips it holds and that the sentence and first frame are the last part's; Make one like this puts the last part back with its line; Continue the clip reads the last frame at the file's end; joining a continuation of a kept clip reads that clip's video by id, and says so when it has been removed meanwhile. **In the app, on the real engine:** a clip, continued, joined (10 s), kept; the joined clip continued and joined (15 s), kept; a fourth refused in words; a Better pair joined once; a kept clip continued and joined; each joined clip plays through and can be sought in; Make one like this on a joined clip makes the last part's clip. All three looks, dark and light, at 700, 886 and 1,400 px, a joined clip shown at each. | Opus 5.5, high |
| 3 | **The helper's animals** | The projector asked for first (default 9). **Counts in text**, then **clips**, against the bar of default 10: ideas from words (a fox, a horse, a dog, an eagle; a cat girl with white hair; a wolf in a leather jacket; a lighthouse; a woman in a red coat) and from first frames drawn in Images (an ordinary wolf, an anthro wolf), plus M21 step 4's six ideas again; every clip Quick with Faster through DOG.ai's own `VideoServer`, read as sheets of frames. The rule reworded in `VideoWriter.SentenceRules` (and in the two forms only where the counts show the need), the test that pins the forms updated, `VideoWriterSmokeTests` given a plain animal. The counts and the rounds go into this plan. Also counted in the same answers and reported, changed only on the owner's yes: "to the left" or "to the right" in a sentence. The projector and every scratch file deleted afterwards. About 36 clips a round, 45 minutes of the card. | Opus 5.5, high |
| 4 | **Finish** | **Leak check with joined clips** (plain and locked): a joined clip not kept, one kept, one kept and removed, a 15-second one, a kept clip read for joining and the join then not kept; pieces of every part and of the joined files searched as bytes and base64. **Every request recorded once** (both engines' stand-ins): nothing is sent when clips are joined; a plain clip's body is M20's 681 bytes; Make one like this on a joined clip sends the continuation's own request byte for byte; never `end_image`; the helper's request with the new rule. **Upgrade from M21's build** (`b9ad2ee`) in the app, plain and locked: its kept clips play and say 5 s, a joined clip is kept beside them, backed up, removed, restored. The published portable build. **DOG.ai's own memory** (`DOGAI_TEST_MEMORY_LOG`) while three clips are made, joined twice and a 15-second Better clip is played. `/code-review high` over M22; every changed screen in three looks, dark and light, at 700 px too; `CLAUDE.md`; this plan's Status. | Fable 5.2, xhigh |

Why this split: steps 1 and 4 are where a wrong judgement costs most (a writer of private files and a migration over kept clips; the last look). Step 2 repeats the Make page's and Kept videos' patterns; step 3 repeats M21 step 4's method on a narrow change.

**Checks that decide whether it is done:** a clip and its continuation joined play as one file of 10.0 seconds, three as 15.0, and every frame in the joined file is a frame of its parts; a fourth clip is refused in words; the clips it was made from are unchanged, kept or not; a joined clip kept, DOG.ai restarted, plays, is continued, is saved as one file that another player opens; Make one like this on it sends the last part's request and says so; a clip kept in M21's build plays after the upgrade and still makes the same clip; nothing is sent to an engine when clips are joined, and nothing of a joined clip is on disk unless it was kept; a plain clip is asked for exactly as in M20; the helper calls no plain animal "anthropomorphic" and adds nothing to it, the wolf in a jacket and the cat girl come out as meant, and no clip from the new form is worse than its pair from the old.

**Not in M22** (say so to take one up): joining two kept clips after the fact (the row would have to remember what it continued); cutting a joined clip back into its parts, and Save the parts (about half a session); a joined clip that remakes its whole chain (every part's request kept in the row and several clips made in a row: 1 to 2 sessions, and more of what was typed stays on disk); more than three clips in one file; smoothing the join (needs an encoder); joining clips of different sizes, or clips that do not continue one another; clips longer than five seconds made in one go; the helper with chat models other than Huihui; and M21's list as it stood (a render queue, sound, video made larger, LoRAs for video, clips in story exports, the local API, the 14B models and an end frame, the three large video files fetched over the network, Windows Hello at the lock screen). **Still not checked from M21, and not checkable here:** a real card under 24 GB, a PC with less than 32 GB of RAM, Better on cards of 17 to 23 GB.

## M21: Video, second round (planned with the owner 2026-11-17)

**What it delivers.**

1. **Continue the clip.** A button on a clip (a try on the Make tab, a kept clip's large view): it opens Make with that clip's last frame as the first frame of the next clip, the sentence still in the box.
2. **An end frame, and with it a loop**, *only if the trial shows the installed model takes one*: a second picture the clip ends on, and a tick "Loop" (the clip ends on its own first frame, so it plays round without a jump).
3. **Write it for me for video.** The chat model writes the sentence: from a few words of the owner's, or by looking at the first frame held and saying what could move.
4. **Faster clips**, *only if the trial shows one of the engine's speed-up switches pays*: a choice "Faster", off unless chosen.
5. **The loose ends of M20 step 6.** DOG.ai's own guests on the card (the document search model, the speech model) can no longer come onto the card while a clip is made; a clip is offered on smaller cards than 16 GB, by the amount the engine really needs once it is told what is free; DOG.ai's own memory after many clips is followed up; the status bar says "set aside for a video" after a video; a story picture's Animate… is looked at; the fast decoder's real download is fetched once through the app.

**What is there today (looked at 2026-11-17).** The installed picture engine's help lists `--end-img`, `--cache-mode` and `--sage-attn`; none was tried (M20 "Not checked"). **Looked up on the web, to be settled by the trial, not relied on:** other tools give Wan 2.2 an end frame only with the large I2V 14B model, not with TI2V-5B, the one installed; the engine's own notes describe "first and last frame" with `--init-img` and `--end-img` without naming a model; a caching change to the engine reported a Wan 2.1 clip's drawing going from 44 to 33 s, with its maker's remark that results on Wan were "not very good"; speech-to-video for this engine exists only in someone's fork, not in the engine DOG.ai installs. In the code: `VideoRequest` has `StartFrame` and nothing for an end; `Clips` ends with `Still`, `StartFrame`, `Video`; the video program lives for one clip, so a start switch can differ from clip to clip (`VideoServer.Arguments`, pinned by a test); a picture reaches Make only through `VideoSession.Hold`; `video.js` already draws a frame of the player onto a canvas (`still`); the Images helper is `PromptHelperBox` over `PictureWriter.PromptAsync` and `PromptFromPictureAsync`; the guests are `EmbeddingServer` and `WhisperRecognizer` (`ICardGuest`), which load without a card turn; `VideoMemory.Need` is 15 and 16 GB, `SmallestCard` 16 GB, and `EnginePlan` already tells the engine what it may plan with; the status bar's words are in `EngineStatus.razor`.

**Settled already** (follows from earlier milestones, not up for choice):

- Offline as ever. **No new model family and no model download in M21.** If the installed model takes no end frame, the end frame and loops are not built, and the owner is asked again before anything larger (the 14B models are 20 GB and more, and their two models swap through the 32 GB of RAM).
- Images still sends no input picture. A last frame, an end frame and the helper's words are in memory only (`VideoSession`) until a clip is kept; then they are in the kept row and nowhere else. Frames go to the engine inside the request as base64, never a path or an address.
- The helper is a DOG.ai model job: no thinking, a `MaxTokens` limit, a schema, its answer checked before it reaches the box, and it reaches the box only after the owner pressed something. The first frame goes to the chat model the one way every picture does (the 1,024-pixel JPEG made in the page, `AttachedPictures.Plain`, `Problem`). Never logged.
- **Kept clips from M20 still make the same clip:** a clip made without the speed-up or an end frame is started and asked for byte for byte as in M20 (the start-switch test and the recorded request body say so). Anything that changes a clip's frames is recorded with the kept clip.
- A new column on `Clips` is a table rebuild (drop the three triggers, copy, rename, index, triggers again), one rebuild for everything M21 adds, the large values last (`Still`, `StartFrame`, `EndFrame`, `Video`); lists still read the still only. Lock, backup, restore, portable move and an upgrade from M20's build (`88ff82c`) are checked.
- The video program's start still never has `--offload-to-cpu`, `--mmap`, `-m`, a LoRA or upscaler folder, and always has `--disable-image-metadata`. Every clip still passes `VideoClips.Problem` before it is shown.
- All three looks, dark and light; an id for every new control; `CLAUDE.md` updated in the last step.

**Decided by the owner (2026-11-17):**

1. **M21 is more from video**, not the local API, not sound, not a tidy-up alone.
2. **Its parts:** Continue the clip and the end frame; Write it for me for video; the speed-up switches. The trial decides whether the end frame and the speed-up are built. No render queue.
3. **All four loose ends are in:** guests take a card turn, the memory rise followed up, the small ones (status bar wording, a story picture's Animate…, the fast decoder's 23 MB download fetched once, asked by name and size at the time), and smaller cards.

**Defaults chosen here (say so to change one):**

1. **Continue the clip makes a new clip beside the first.** The two are not joined into one file: DOG.ai has no video encoder. The trial looks at whether two of the engine's files could be joined by their structure alone and reports; it is built only on the owner's yes.
2. **The last frame is read in the page from the clip itself** (the player paused at its end, drawn and written as a JPEG at the clip's own size), so each continuation starts a little softer than the clip before ended. The trial makes three in a row and says how far it holds. The next clip takes the clip's Quick or Better and shape, the same sentence (the owner changes it) and a new seed.
3. **The bar for the speed-up:** at least a quarter faster for a whole Quick clip and a whole Better clip, and on three clips with the same seed side by side (a place from a sentence, a person and an anthro character from a first frame) no worse to the eye. Below the bar nothing is built. Above it: a choice "Faster" in the side panel, **off by default**, said in the estimate and in a kept clip's facts.
4. **The bar for the end frame:** on three seeds the clip ends on the picture (its last frame compared with it), what lies between is motion and not one picture fading into the other, and a loop moves instead of standing still. Above it: a second picture slot under the first ("End on a picture"; a file, cut to the clip's shape as the first frame is) and the tick "Loop"; Animate… stays as it is.
5. **The helper** is one dialog on the Make tab ("Write it for me…"), from words or from the first frame held. The model is the one in memory, else the General default, never a swap; a model that cannot see says so before anything is sent. It writes one to three English sentences: what is seen, what moves, how the camera moves, the light. Its wording is chosen by clips made from its sentences (step 4), as every form's wording was chosen by runs. It replaces the sentence in the box (there is one box).
6. **A guest never comes onto the card while a clip is made.** Whether it waits for the clip or runs on the processor meanwhile (the speech model can) is decided in step 5 by measuring; either way the owner is told in words, never left with a silent wait.
7. **Smaller cards get Quick only**, from the smallest card size at which a Quick clip was made at normal speed with another program holding the rest of this card, **in the app as well as with the engine alone**; it also needs about 9.5 GB of free RAM, checked before the clip and refused in words. Better stays at 16 GB. The page says such a card was not tried for real.

**Defaults: confirmed or changed by the trial (2026-11-17).** Step 2 builds these unless the owner says otherwise.

1. **Continue the clip makes a new clip beside the first: confirmed.** New: joining by structure works (see "Trial results › Joining"); it stays out of M21 unless the owner asks for it.
2. **The last frame read in the page as a JPEG at the clip's own size: confirmed** (quality 0.9, as chat pictures). A PNG of the same frame gave a continuation 2 to 3% sharper, a fifth of what a continuation loses anyway, so the first-frame check keeps taking WebP and JPEG only. The page reaches the last frame by setting the player's time to the clip's length (every frame of the engine's files is a key frame, so the seek is exact). **New: the Make page says in a line that each continuation is a little softer and further from the first picture, and that after two it shows.** The same sentence makes the same motion again (the wolf went on turning his head until it hung): the sentence stays in the box, and the line says to change it.
3. **The speed-up: over the bar at Quick (32 to 39%), at the bar at Better (26% and 23%), no worse to the eye. Built as "Faster", off by default**, said in the estimate and in a kept clip's facts. It is the engine's `--sage-attn` at start together with `cache_mode` "dbcache" in the request, the cache at the engine's own settings (8 blocks always computed, threshold 0.08, 8 warm-up steps), written out in the request so a later engine cannot change a kept clip. Not offered: sage attention alone (14 to 17%, under the bar), a stronger cache setting (threshold 0.12: 2 to 3 points faster, a little hazier), easycache and spectrum (faster, visibly softer). **A clip made without Faster is started and asked for exactly as in M20.**
4. **The end frame: under the bar on all three counts. Not built**: no second picture slot, no "Loop". The request's field would be `end_image`; nothing is sent in it.
5. **The helper: unchanged** (the trial did not touch it). *Built in step 4:* no worse than the owner's words in any pair, better in half (the bar's "most" missed by one; the owner chose one more round, then accepted the result as recorded, 2026-11-17).
6. **Guests: unchanged.** *Built in step 5:* both run on the processor meanwhile (measured: 5 s for a 7-second sentence, 4 to 9 passages a second), told on the Video page; and a guest in use when the clip starts steps off the card (it stayed in M20, and the clip then took half as long again).
7. **Smaller cards: unchanged, with one correction for step 5.** *Built in step 5:* Quick from 8 GB (7 GB of the card free, measured at 6.8 with the engine alone and 7.0 in the app), **12 GB of free RAM, not 9.5** (the engine's own RAM is 11.0 GB with a plan, 16.6 GB when 17 GB or more is free; with 9.8 GB free a clip took 401 s), Better still 16 GB. With a plan of 10 GB and nothing else on the card the engine's own memory in RAM was **16.7 GB and free RAM fell to 2.3 GB** (of 31.3), not the 9.5 GB step 6 of M20 worked from: the free-RAM check is measured again in step 5, with the engine alone and in the app. Faster works with a plan (93 s against 140 s).

**Steps, and which model to use for each.** One step per session, each ending in a build, passing tests, a look at the app, this plan's Status updated and a commit, then the hand-over.

| # | Step | Delivers | Model / effort |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 ✅ | **Trial** (no app code; done 2026-11-17, results below) | By hand, outside DOG.ai, in `D:\dogai-b`, the installed engine run from hard links of its own files and started with `VideoServer.Arguments`' switches, as M20 step 1 did (`CLAUDE.md`, "Video: what the trial found, and running the picture engine by hand"). **No downloads.** First frames drawn in DOG.ai's Images section first (a place, a person, an anthro character). **Speed-up:** the exact switches and values from the engine's own help; each alone and together, at Quick and Better, whole clips of 20 steps: seconds per step, the whole clip, the card's peak and RAM; the same seed with and without, looked at side by side as sheets of frames; whether it is a start switch or can be asked per clip; once with a plan of 10 GB (a crowded card). **End frame:** the route's field for it (the engine's help and its answers say); a first and an end picture of the same scene, and the same picture twice (a loop), three seeds each; is the last frame the picture, is the middle motion or a fade, what an end picture of another shape does, what it costs in time and memory. **Continue the clip:** a clip's last frame read in a page (the way `video.js` draws a frame), written as a JPEG and given back as the next first frame, three in a row for a place and for the anthro character; sheets; how soft and how far from the character the third is. **Joining:** two of the engine's files read by structure (`WebmInfo`): what would have to be rewritten to play them as one; reported only. **What the engine writes and prints** with the new switches and an end frame (the leak question, as in M20 step 1, with made-up words). Ends with: built or not for the end frame and for the speed-up against the bars above, the numbers for the estimate, the defaults above confirmed or changed, and steps 2 to 6 corrected in this plan. | Fable 5.2, high |
| 2 ✅ | **Core, storage and engine** (done 2026-11-17; see Status) | *From step 1:* no end frame (nothing named `EndFrame`, nothing sent as `end_image`). **Continue the clip's Core:** a first frame "from the end of a clip" (`VideoFrame.Of` on the JPEG the page made, wording in `VideoPanels`: where it came from, and the line that a continuation is softer and drifts, after two it shows), and what Make is given for the next clip (the clip's quality and shape, its sentence, **whether it was Faster**, a new seed), tested as `VideoLike` is. **Faster:** a choice on `VideoRequest` (off by default); with it `VideoServer.Arguments` adds `--sage-attn` and `SdApi.VideoBody` adds `cache_mode` "dbcache" and `cache_option` "Fn=8,Bn=0,threshold=0.08,warmup=8" (the engine's own settings written out: a real-engine test checks they give the frames that leaving them out gives); the pinned tests hold a plain clip's start list and request body byte for byte as in M20, and the faster ones'. `VideoEstimate`: drawing takes **0.62 of the plain time at Quick and 0.53 at Better** (seconds per step 3.3 at 0.61 megapixels, 5.35 at 1.01; the smaller sizes, untried, 0.65 of plain); start, decoding and encoding as they are (the trial's plain clips: Quick 118 s, Better 401 to 404 s, as the estimate says). The cache skips 5 to 7 of the 20 steps but the engine still reports all 20, so the bar counts steps as before. The page reads the last frame by setting the player's time to the clip's length (a hidden `<video>` as `video.js` `still` uses, drawn at the clip's own size, JPEG at 0.9). **"Faster"** in the side panel (a fold of its own, off by default, one line: about a third faster at Quick, a quarter at Better, fine lines a little softer), in the estimate, the status line and a kept clip's facts, put back by Make one like this and taken over by Continue; remembered in `VideoChoices` (never a frame or the sentence). **No end picture and no "Loop"** (step 1).
