An upgrade from `88ff82c` in the app; the published portable build; `/code-review high` over M21; every changed screen in three looks, dark and light, and narrow; `CLAUDE.md`; this plan's Status. | Fable 5.2, xhigh |

Why this split: as in M20. Steps 1, 2, 5 and 6 are where a wrong judgement costs most (what the engine really does, a migration over private clips, the card rule, the last look); steps 3 and 4 repeat patterns the code has (the Make page's controls, the Images helper).

**Checks that decide whether it is done:** a clip continued from another starts on that clip's last frame and is made in the time the estimate said, give or take a third; Faster does in the app what it did by hand (about a third faster at Quick, a quarter at Better, the same clip to the eye), and there is no end frame and no loop in the app; a sentence the helper wrote makes a clip no worse than the owner's own words did, and with a model that cannot see nothing is sent; a clip kept in M20's build plays after the upgrade and Make one like this sends its request byte for byte; no guest comes onto the card while a clip is made; a Quick clip is made at normal speed at the smallest card size offered (another program holding the rest), and refused in words below it or with too little RAM; DOG.ai's memory after ten clips is explained and, if it can be, lower; nothing of a last frame, an end picture or the helper's words is on disk unless its clip was kept; with no video model installed every other part of DOG.ai behaves as before M20; Images still sends no input picture.

**Not in M21** (put to the owner on 2026-11-17 with their cost, and passed over; say so to take one up): a render queue (about 2 sessions; needs a decision on where finished clips go beyond the four held); sound and talking characters (the largest: the installed engine has no speech-to-video, DOG.ai's clip check allows one silent track, a new model family and large downloads; a trial and 4 or more sessions); video made larger (the engine enlarges pictures only: 121 frames at about 9 s each, and the frames need encoding again; a trial and 2 to 3 sessions); LoRAs for video (few exist for the 5B model; 2 to 3 sessions and a download to try); clips in story exports (a passage must own a clip first; about 3 sessions); a local OpenAI-compatible API (3 to 4 sessions, design first); the 14B models, and with them a sure end frame; **added by the trial:** an end frame and loops at all (the installed model does not take one: see "Trial results"; a model that does is Wan 2.1 FLF2V 14B or one of the Wan 2.2 14B pair, 20 GB and more, and the owner is asked before anything of the kind), and continued clips joined into one file (possible without an encoder: a writer in Core beside `WebmInfo`, a clip check that takes 241 and 361 frames, a kept row whose request no longer makes the whole clip; about 2 sessions); the three large video files downloaded over the network for real (17.5 GB); Windows Hello at the lock screen (needs the owner at the PC); clips longer than 5 seconds.

### Trial results (step 1, 2026-11-17)

Run by hand, outside DOG.ai, in `D:\dogai-b`, as M20's trial was: the installed picture engine (`master-1104-5dcfa79`, CUDA 12) from hard links of its own files, started with `VideoServer.Arguments`' switches, **a fresh program for every clip** (as DOG.ai does), the request `SdApi.VideoBody` writes (121 frames, 24 a second, 20 steps, Euler, guidance 5, shift 5, file-size setting 70, a full-size preview every fourth step). The card was otherwise empty (0.6 GB held by Windows). "Whole clip" is from the request to the finished file, the model's files still in Windows' cache (the program's own start, 1 second, is not in it). 59 clips; about 45 looked at as sheets of frames. Seven pictures drawn in DOG.ai's Images section first (novaFurryXL, Small, 960 × 640: a mountain lake with waterfalls, a girl with red hair in a wheat field, an anthro wolf in a leather jacket at a campfire; of each a second picture with the same seed and other words, as an end picture of the same scene; the wolf once more at 640 × 960). The three sentences: the lake ("…a tall waterfall falls into the lake, mist rises, clouds drift… The camera moves slowly forward."), the girl ("…the wind blows her hair, the wheat sways, she smiles gently and tilts her head."), the wolf ("…He slowly turns his head toward the fire, the flames flicker and sparks rise.").

**The switches, from the engine's own help.** `--sage-attn` ("use native CUDA SageAttention in the diffusion model, with flash/default attention fallback") is a **start switch** and has no request field. `--cache-mode` takes `easycache` (DiT), `ucache` (UNET, not for this model), `dbcache`, `taylorseer`, `cache-dit` (DiT block-level) and `spectrum`; `--cache-option` takes named values (easycache: `threshold=,start=,end=,decay=,relative=,reset=`; the three block caches: `Fn=,Bn=,threshold=,warmup=`; spectrum: `w=,m=,lam=,window=,flex=,warmup=,stop=`). Both are generation defaults and **can be asked per clip in the request** as `cache_mode` and `cache_option` (also `scm_mask`, `scm_policy_dynamic`, for cache-dit only). The names the web search had found differ; these are the ones this build takes. The engine prints what it did ("CacheDIT enabled - mode: DBCache, Fn: 8, Bn: 0, threshold: 0.080, warmup: 8", "CacheDIT skipped 7/20 steps"), never the prompt.

**Speed: whole Quick clips** (960 × 640, the fast decoder; seconds for the whole clip, the drawing alone in brackets, and against plain):

| | Place, from a sentence | Person, from a first frame | Anthro, from a first frame |
|---|---|---|---|
| Plain | 118.1 (107.3) | 118.3 (107.9) | 117.9 (107.9) |
| Sage attention | 98.6 (87.5), 17% faster | 98.7 (88.3), 17% | 98.6 (88.3), 16% |
| dbcache | 95.0 (83.7), 20% | 85.0 (75.0), 28% | 85.1 (75.1), 28% |
| **Sage attention + dbcache** | **80.2 (69.0), 32%** | **72.6 (62.2), 39%** | **72.9 (62.2), 38%** |
| easycache | 71.0 (60.0), 40% | 75.0 (65.6), 37% | 71.4 (60.8), 39% |
| Sage attention + easycache | 61.8 (50.3), 48% | not run | not run |
| spectrum | 75.4 (64.6), 36% | not run | not run |

- A plain step took 4.75 s by the engine's own count (the first 7.6 s, with reading the model), with sage attention 3.77 s. dbcache computed 13 to 15 of the 20 steps (5 skipped from a sentence, 7 from a first frame), easycache 10 or 11, spectrum 11. **`taylorseer` and `cache-dit` gave the dbcache clip frame for frame** at their own settings: for this model the three are one. A stronger dbcache (threshold 0.12 in place of the engine's 0.08): 72.6 s for the place and 68.1 s for the anthro (38 and 42%); 0.16: 68.4 s. easycache at threshold 0.1 in place of 0.2: 76.4 s.
- **The card and RAM do not change with the switches.** Every Quick clip peaked at 22.8 GB on the card (the engine's own 21.6 GB) with free RAM never under 16 GB and the engine's own RAM at 1.6 to 2.1 GB. (easycache and spectrum by themselves peaked at 18.8 GB.)

**Speed: whole Better clips** (1216 × 832, the model's own decoder, which took 182 to 184 s in every one):

| | Anthro, from a first frame | Place, from a sentence |
|---|---|---|
| Plain | 403.6 (205.6) | 400.8 (203.9) |
| Sage attention | 347.1 (150.9), 14% faster | not run |
| dbcache | 335.4 (139.5), 17% | not run |
| **Sage attention + dbcache** | **300.4 (104.1), 26%** | **306.9 (109.6), 23%** |
| Sage attention + dbcache, threshold 0.12 | 290.2 (94.5), 28% | 301.7 (102.8), 25% |
| easycache | 290.1 (92.5), 28% | not run |
| Sage attention + easycache | 277.1 (77.5), 31% | not run |

- The drawing itself is halved (49 and 46% faster), but decoding is 45% of a plain Better clip and none of the switches touches it. The card peaked at 20.4 to 20.7 GB plain and 20.6 to 20.9 GB with sage attention (0.3 GB more); free RAM never under 16 GB.
- **With a plan of 10 GB** (`--max-vram 10`, no previews, as DOG.ai asks on a crowded card; nothing else was on the card): plain 139.9 s, sage attention + dbcache 92.7 s (34% faster); the engine held 12.9 GB of the card and **16.7 GB of RAM, and free RAM fell to 2.3 and 2.7 GB**. A clip made with a plan is the same scene and motion but not frame for frame the clip the free card makes from the same seed.

**How they look, same seed side by side** (sheets of six frames, then the face or the middle of the picture at full size; "sharpness" is a number from the page, the mean strength of fine edges, only to say which way a difference goes):

- **Sage attention:** the same clip to the eye in all three (sharpness 6.82 against 6.88, 6.28 against 6.32, 30.2 against 30.6).
- **Sage attention + dbcache:** the same clip in layout, motion and colour in all five pairs. Place: the same (30.3 against 30.6; Better 27.5 against 27.8). Anthro: the same at Quick and at Better, also enlarged (Better 5.00 against 5.06). Person (Quick): the same at the clip's size; enlarged twofold the finest hair lines are a little softer and one eye a little less clean in the last second (6.26 against 6.88). **No worse to the eye at the size a clip is watched.**
- **easycache, spectrum:** visibly softer: the lake's trees and rock lose their grain (27.5 and 25.8 against 30.6), the girl's hair is smoothed, and the Better clip is blurred outright (4.21 against 5.06). Not used.
- **Threshold 0.12:** a little hazier in the Better place's mountains and the girl's hair than 0.08, for 2 to 3 points of speed. Not used.
- Seen on the way: the place from this sentence came out pale with burnt-out whites at Quick and at Better, plain and faster alike (the model's weak point from a sentence, as in M20). At Quick the wolf's face changed in the first second (his fur lit orange, his muzzle whiter) in every variant; at Better he stayed himself.

**Against the bar:** a whole Quick clip a quarter faster: **yes, 32 to 39%.** A whole Better clip a quarter faster: **26% from a first frame, 23% from a sentence.** Three clips side by side no worse: **yes at the clip's size.** Built, off by default; the estimate and the kept clip say "Faster".

**For the estimate** (`VideoEstimate`): the plain numbers stand (Quick whole 118 s by hand, the estimate's warm figure 116 s; Better 401 to 404 s, the estimate 410 s). With Faster the drawing takes 0.58 to 0.64 of the plain time at Quick (**0.62**) and 0.51 to 0.54 at Better (**0.53**); start, decoding and encoding are unchanged. Quick about 1¼ minutes in place of 2; Better about 5 minutes in place of 6¾.

**The end frame.** The request's field is `end_image`, base64 as `init_image` is (the engine's own page sends both; the start switch for files is `--end-img`, "required by flf2v"). The engine's list of what it can do (`GET /sdcpp/v1/capabilities`) says `end_image: true` for whatever model is loaded, so it tells nothing; its own text names an end-frame model only for Wan 2.1 FLF2V 14B. **With the installed model the request is taken without an error or a warning and costs nothing** (117.8 to 118.6 s against 117.9 s, the same card and RAM). What comes out, in twelve whole Quick clips (the wolf's two pictures on three seeds, the wolf's one picture as both on three seeds, the lake's two, the girl's two, the lake's one as both, the tall picture as the end, and one of each kind again with the model's own decoder):

- **The clip does not end on the picture.** The last three frames (118 to 120) of every clip are a pale, grey, soft version of it: their sharpness is 3.0 to 4.4 where the pictures and the rest of the clip have 6, and the last frame is 18 to 27 from the end picture on a scale where a first frame is 2 to 3 from its picture. Both decoders the same, so it is the model, not the fast decoder: its last piece stands for four frames and the picture is put in as one.
- **The end picture itself shows a few frames earlier, and arrives as a cut.** The wolf turns toward the end pose over four seconds (motion, and good), then the picture jumps in around frame 114 to 116 (the background changes at once; nearest frame 9 to 20 from the picture). The lake stands as the day picture for almost four seconds and **cuts to the sunset picture between two frames**, which then stands until the grey frames. The girl moves, then jumps to the end pose in the last frames.
- **A loop does not move like a clip and does not close.** With the same picture at both ends the clip stays within 4 to 6 of the picture for 117 frames (a plain clip from that picture is 18 away by frame 30): the flames flicker, the wolf barely breathes. Then the three grey frames, which a looping player shows as a flash every five seconds.
- **An end picture of another shape is cut from its middle**, as a first frame is: the tall wolf became a torso without a head.
- **Against the bar** (ends on the picture, the middle is motion, a loop moves): **no on the first for every clip, no on the second for two scenes of three, no on the third.** Not built. Dropping the last four frames would not save it (the frame before them is still 9 to 20 from the picture, after a cut).

**Continue the clip.** The page read each clip's last frame as `video.js` reads the first (the bytes as a `blob:` address, a `<video>`, the time set to the clip's length, drawn on a canvas at the clip's own size, written as a JPEG at 0.9: 100 to 170 KB) and the JPEG went back as `init_image` with the same sentence and a new seed; three in a row for the lake clip (itself from a sentence) and for the wolf clip (itself from the picture).

- **It starts on the last frame:** the continuation's first frame is 2.0 to 4.1 from the frame it was given, as any first frame is from its picture. It takes the time of any clip (117.8 to 119.7 s) and the same memory.
- **Softness, the wolf:** sharpness of each clip's last frame 6.4, then 5.75, 4.9, 4.5: a tenth lost by the first continuation, a quarter by the second, 30% by the third. **The lake** (pale to begin with): 29.5, then 18.3, 10.1, 5.0, its colours pushed to a harsh turquoise and the sky burnt out by the third.
- **The character:** the first clip had already turned the wolf's head to the fire; each continuation, told the same sentence, turned and bowed it further. After one continuation he is the wolf of the first clip's end; after two his ears lie back and his eyes are shut; **after three he is another animal in the same jacket** (from the picture: 19, 26, 33, 36 on the scale above).
- **JPEG or PNG:** the same continuation from a PNG of the same frame, same seed, was 2 to 3% sharper throughout (5.87 against 5.75 at its end): about a fifth of what one continuation loses. The JPEG stays.
- **So:** good for one more clip, usable for two, and the page says so. A new sentence for each continuation is what keeps it from repeating its motion.

**Joining** (report only). Two of the engine's files read by structure: the same parts in the same order (header "webm", seek index, 45 empty bytes, the facts with the length as a 4-byte number and "stable-diffusion.cpp" twice, one `V_VP8` track numbered 1, then **one cluster per frame, each holding one frame that is a key frame**, then the cue list with one point per frame). The two files differ only in the track's random id, the frames and the positions. To play two as one, nothing is decoded: keep the first file's header, facts and track; write the length again (in place); append the second file's clusters with each one's time raised by the first clip's length (for its first six clusters the time grows from one byte to two, so their sizes are written again); write a new cue list (a time and a position per frame), the seek index's position of it, and the whole size. A 60-line script did exactly that with a clip and its continuation (leaving out the continuation's first frame, which repeats the last): **241 frames, 10.0 s, 9.4 MB; the page played it, sought in it and read frames from both halves.** At the seam the picture softens in one step and jumps a little (the first frame after a given frame always moves about twice as much as the others). In DOG.ai it would need a writer beside `WebmInfo`, a clip check that takes 241 frames, and a kept row whose request makes only the last part.

**What it writes and prints** (the leak question; sage attention and the cache on, a first and an end picture as WebP and as the JPEG read from a clip, made-up words in the prompt and the negative prompt, one clip cancelled while drawing; then `tools/leak-search.ps1` for the three words and for pieces of both clips and all five pictures, bytes and base64): **its own folder: 29 files, none new or changed. Its TEMP folder: empty. The models folder: nothing written. What it printed: no hit. The user's TEMP: no hit. The two clips hold none of the words, no model file name, no "dbcache".** A cancelled job with an end picture leaves what any cancelled job leaves (no result). The answer's `generation_info` gains a `cache` part (the mode and its numbers) and still repeats the prompt; it has nothing of the end picture. The WebM files made with the new switches have exactly the parts of M20's.

## M20: Video (planned with the owner 2026-11-16; built after M19)

**What it delivers.**

1. **A Video section.** A short clip (about five seconds) made from a sentence, or from a sentence and a picture that becomes its first frame. Progress, Cancel, and an estimate of the time before it starts.
2. **Kept clips.** A clip that is tried and not kept stays in memory, as pictures do; a kept one is in the database with what made it, plays in the page, and can be saved as a file.
3. **Animate this picture.** A button wherever a picture lives (a try in Images, a kept picture, a portrait, a story or chat picture): it opens Video with that picture as the first frame.
4. **Models knows video models.** Get models › Video and On this PC › Video; a video model is several files (the model, its text encoder, its VAE) downloaded together.

**What is there today (looked at 2026-11-16).** The picture engine DOG.ai installs (`sd-server`, build `master-1104-5dcfa79`, CUDA 12) has video generation built in: its own help lists `--video-frames`, `--fps`, `--init-img` and `--end-img`, `--diffusion-model` with `--t5xxl`, `--vae` and `--clip_vision`, `--high-noise-diffusion-model`, `--temporal-tiling`, `--offload-to-cpu` and `--mmap`. Its documentation describes a video route beside the picture one (`/sdcpp/v1/vid_gen`: a job with progress, preview and cancel, as `SdApi` uses for pictures; the start frame sent inside the request; WebM, animated WebP or AVI as the result, no MP4). (All of it was tried in step 1: see "Trial results" below.) DOG.ai starts the engine with one checkpoint (`-m`, `ImageServer`), knows only SDXL-family files (`ImageFileRecognizer`), keeps only WebP pictures (`Pictures`), and its card rule (`CardCoordinator`, `PictureMemory`) estimates a picture. The owner's checkpoints and LoRAs do not work with a video model, and video models read sentences, not tags.

**Settled already** (follows from earlier milestones, not up for choice):

- Offline as ever: a clip is made by the engine on `127.0.0.1`; only the model downloads go online, through the Models section.
- A clip that is not kept is never on disk: not a file, not a cache, not TEMP. If the engine cannot make a clip without writing a file of its own, the trial says so and the owner decides before anything is built.
- The start frame goes to the engine inside the request, made from bytes DOG.ai already holds or from a file the page opened and wrote again as a plain picture (M17's path); never a path, never an address.
- A prompt is never logged; whatever is sent to the engine is told to `PictureLogFilter` first.
- A clip takes the whole card: the chat model is set aside, the picture model and every guest let go, one thing at a time through the card turn. Nothing else is drawn or answered meanwhile.
- A kept clip's data is the last column of its table, deleted rows leave the disk the way removed data does, and the table goes through lock, backup, restore and a portable move.
- All three looks, dark and light; ids for every new control; `CLAUDE.md` gets "How video works", and its line "Never draw from an input picture" is changed to say video's start frame is the one exception.

**Decided by the owner (2026-11-16):**

1. **M20 comes after M19.**
2. **The first version is text to video and a start frame**, with one model family. End frames, loops, continuing a clip, a queue, sound, talking characters and video upscaling are not in it.
3. **A start frame is allowed, for video only.** The earlier decision stands for Images: nothing there is drawn from an input picture.
4. **The trial downloads Wan 2.2 TI2V-5B only** (the model, its text encoder and its VAE; roughly 15 to 20 GB), each file asked for by its exact name and size first. Another family only if this one disappoints, and only after asking again.

**Defaults to confirm** (the trial gives the numbers; step 2 builds the recommended one unless told otherwise):

1. **Kept clips are in the database**, in a table of their own (recommended: the lock and backups cover them). The price is size: a clip is roughly 2 to 20 MB, so a hundred kept clips could add a gigabyte or two to the database and to every backup. The other way is files in a folder, which a lock would not cover.
2. **A clip is kept as WebM** (the browser control plays it; the engine writes no MP4). Save as a file gives the same bytes.
3. **Sizes and length offered:** what the trial finds comfortable on this card, one "quick" and one "better" size, a fixed length near five seconds.
4. **Where models go:** `<models>\video`, with the engine's own folder names beneath it, created only when needed, as `<models>\image` is.
5. **The prompt is a sentence the owner types**; "Write it for me" for motion (the chat model, also from the start frame through M17's picture input) is a later addition, not in the first version.
6. **Animate this picture leaves the picture as it is**: the clip is a new thing beside it, never in its place.

**Defaults: confirmed or changed by the trial (2026-11-16).** Step 2 builds these unless the owner says otherwise.

1. **Kept clips in the database: confirmed.** A 5-second clip is about 3 to 9 MB at 960 × 640 and about twice that at 1216 × 832 (worked out from the trial's clips, which were made at a higher file-size setting than the one chosen; see "The result" below), so a hundred kept clips add roughly 0.5 to 1.5 GB to the database and to every backup.
2. **WebM: confirmed.** The engine writes VP8 video in WebM with no sound, no date and no tags; DOG.ai's page plays it. Save as a file gives the same bytes.
3. **Sizes: the clip sizes are DOG.ai's own picture sizes**, so a picture drawn in DOG.ai becomes a first frame without being cut: **Quick** = Small (640 × 960, 960 × 640, 768 × 768), **about 2 minutes**; **Better** = Normal (832 × 1216, 1216 × 832, 1024 × 1024), **about 7 minutes with the model's own decoder, 3.6 with the fast one**. Length fixed at **5 seconds** (121 frames at 24 a second: what the model was made for; 8 seconds worked and looked worse). 20 steps.
4. **Models folder: confirmed**, `<models>\video` with `diffusion_models`, `text_encoders`, `vae`.
5. **The prompt is a sentence: confirmed.** New: DOG.ai sends a fixed negative prompt in English with every clip (it did as well as the model's Chinese one); the owner does not have to type one.
6. **Animate this picture: confirmed.** New: the clip takes the picture's shape (tall, square or wide); a picture of another shape is cut to the nearest of the three in the page, never left to the engine (which cuts the middle out: a tall portrait put into a wide clip lost its head).
7. **New: a fourth file, the fast decoder** (`taew2_2.safetensors`, 23 MB, MIT licence). It turns the finished clip into frames in 1 to 2 seconds where the model's own decoder takes 40 seconds (Small) to over 3 minutes (Normal), a little softer in fine detail, and it gives full-size previews while the clip is made. **Recommended: Quick uses the fast decoder, Better the model's own; the request carries size and decoder apart, so step 4 can offer "Better, fast decoder" (3.6 minutes) if the owner wants it.**
8. **New: the start frame's face can drift.** Nothing is built for it; the Make page says in one line that a clip follows its first frame only loosely.

**Steps, and which model to use for each.** One step per session, each ending in a build, passing tests, a look at the app, this plan's Status updated and a commit, then the hand-over.

| # | Step | Delivers | Model / effort |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 ✅ | **Trial** (no app code; done 2026-11-16, results below) | By hand, outside DOG.ai, in a scratch folder on D:, the installed engine started the way `ImageServer` starts it plus the video switches. **Downloads only with the owner's yes, each file by name and size** (Wan 2.2 TI2V-5B, its text encoder, its VAE; which quantisation fits). Measured: seconds, graphics memory and RAM for a clip from a sentence and for one from a start frame, at two or three sizes and step counts; how long a clip may be; the server's video route (its request, progress, preview, cancel, and what a cancelled job leaves); **what the engine writes to disk and prints while making and encoding a clip** (its own folder, TEMP given a folder of its own, the output: the leak question); the result's format and size in MB, and whether the page plays it; whether a start frame may be given as bytes in the request; whether the loaded video model can be let go and a picture drawn straight after; what happens with 32 GB of RAM (`--offload-to-cpu`, `--mmap`). How good: three sentences and three start frames (a picture drawn in DOG.ai of a person, of a place, of an anthro character), looked at. The model's licence read. Ends with the family and files to recommend, the sizes and times for the estimate, the numbers for the card rule, the defaults above confirmed or changed, and steps 2 to 6 corrected in this plan. | Fable 5.2, high |
| 2 ✅ | **Core, storage and engine** (done 2026-11-16; what was built and checked is in the Status block "M20 step 2") | **Core:** a video request beside `ImageRequest` (nothing shared that would change a picture's bytes): sentence, the fixed negative prompt, size (Quick or Better) and shape through `PictureLevels`' own sizes, 121 frames at 24 a second, 20 steps, Euler, guidance 5, flow shift 5, seed, decoder (fast or the model's own), an optional start frame as bytes; its result (WebM, frame count, seed); the time estimate from the trial's table; `VideoMemory.Need`. **A WebM check** in the way of `WebpInfo` (read by structure, nothing decoded: EBML header, `webm`, one `V_VP8` track, **no Tags element, no attachment**; anything else is refused), run on every result before it is shown or kept. **Engine:** `SdApi` gets `vid_gen` (body, job, result; `generation_info` is dropped unread and never logged); a video model is three paths and the fast decoder (`--diffusion-model`, `--vae`, `--t5xxl`, `--taesd`, with `--taesd-preview-only` when the model's own decoder is asked for), started with `--max-vram -3` and `ImageServer`'s own switches (`--disable-image-metadata` above all), **never `--offload-to-cpu` or `--mmap`**, never `-m`, no LoRA or upscaler folder; a test pins both switch lists (a picture's is byte for byte as before M20). Only `.safetensors` and `.gguf` are opened. A cancel that has not taken within a few seconds (the model's own decoder cannot be interrupted) ends the program. **Card:** `CardCoordinator` clears the card for a clip under one turn (chat model set aside, picture model and guests let go), refuses in words when the cleared card has less than the need, ends the video program when the clip is collected or anything else wants the card, and brings the chat model back as after a picture. **The start frame** is a WebP DOG.ai drew or the JPEG the page made, checked before it is sent, base64 inside the request; `PictureLogFilter` is told the sentence first. **Storage:** a table for kept clips (the video the last column, before it a WebP still and the start frame; owner kind and id; the request that made it), migration and triggers, removed-data rules, nothing for `PortableMove` (no full path is stored: a test), lock, backup, restore and portable checks, an upgrade from the previous build. Tests with a fake engine and the real database; a real-engine test behind environment variables (the files are in `D:\dogai-b\models\video`). No screen yet. | Fable 5.2, high |

Video files recognised by their headers (`ImageFileRecognizer`'s way for the three `.safetensors`; the text encoder's GGUF header through the existing reader; read only, refused formats never opened). **The chat-model list and the search-model pick must skip `<models>\video`**: today `ModelLibrary.ScanAll` walks the whole models folder for `.gguf`, so the text encoder would be offered as a chat model (a test). Get models › Video: the recommended set of four files downloaded together (17.5 GB; three from Hugging Face `Comfy-Org/Wan_2.2_ComfyUI_Repackaged` and `city96/umt5-xxl-encoder-gguf`, the fast decoder from GitHub `madebyollin/taehv`: check `OriginPolicy` allows it, no token sent there), each checked against its published checksum, with whether it fits (a card of 16 GB or more; smaller cards are not offered in the first version: untried). On this PC › Video (what is installed, what is missing of the four, delete), in `ModelsPlaces` with addresses and names; the side panel's card memory; a "missing" line with a link where Video needs a model. All three looks, dark and light. | Opus 5.5, high |

One line says a clip follows its first frame only loosely. Controls remembered without the prompt, as `ImagesMemory` does. A picture is handed to Make through `VideoSession.Hold(VideoFrame)`: a DOG.ai WebP of one of the three shapes goes as it is (`VideoFrame.Of(bytes, from)` checks it for both qualities); any other picture goes through `attach.js` `openFrameBytes` → `VideoFrames.Cut` → `cutFrame` as a file does. A kept clip is a `Clip` (`IClipStore.PageAsync(ClipOwners.Video, 0, …)` gives stills without video); play it with `video.js` `play` from its bytes; Make one like this puts back its sentence, quality, shape, seed and first frame (`Clip.ToRequest()`). The Video wording is `VideoPanels`. The Kept videos tab: stills in a grid (stills in a grid, a page at a time, the large view that plays, "Make one like this", Remove, Save as a file); the **Animate…** button on an Images try, a kept picture, a portrait in the character and persona editors, and a story or chat picture's large view, each handing its picture to Make as the start frame (the engine takes a DOG.ai WebP as it is; a picture that is not one of the three shapes is cut in the page). All three looks, dark and light. | Opus 5.5, high |

For the leak check: a first frame handed over is held in `VideoSession.Frame` (memory only) and, once its clip is kept, in the row's `StartFrame`; the cut is made in the page (`attach.js`), and a kept clip plays from a blob: address (`video.js`). Not yet seen: a story picture's Animate…, a clip from a cut picture on the real engine. Leak check with clips in place of pictures (a clip made and not kept, kept, kept and removed, a start frame from a file; pieces searched for in the data folder, models folder, engine folders and TEMP, plain and locked); every request body recorded once; every clip made in the app checked to hold no tags and no prompt word; the engine started without its metadata switch refused by a test; graphics memory and RAM before and after ten clips; **a clip while another program holds several gigabytes of the card** (step 2 saw the card at 23 of 24 GB while a clip is decoded: see its "Seen, not changed"); the square sizes and 832 × 1216, which the trial did not make; a chat reply and a picture straight after a clip; the published portable build; `/code-review high`; every new screen in three looks, dark and light, and narrow; `CLAUDE.md` ("How video works"); this plan's Status. | Fable 5.2, xhigh |

Why this split: as in M16 and M17. Steps 1, 2 and 6 are where a wrong judgement costs most (what the engine really does, private clips on disk and a migration, the last look); steps 3 to 5 repeat patterns the code has (the picture downloads, the Images section's two tabs, the buttons that hand a picture on).

**Checks that decide whether it is done:** a clip from a sentence and a clip from a picture are made in the app in the time the estimate said, give or take a third; a kept clip plays after a restart, survives lock, backup and restore and a portable move; a clip that was not kept, and a kept one that was removed, are nowhere on disk; the chat model answers and a picture is drawn straight after a clip, with no engine left behind; with no video model installed every other part of DOG.ai behaves exactly as before M20; Images still sends no input picture.

**Not in M20 (could follow, each needs another model family or a look of its own):** an end frame, and with it living portraits (a seamless loop), passage-to-passage clips and "Continue the clip"; a render queue; "Suggest a motion" and Write it for me for video; sound and talking characters (a portrait and the read-aloud voice's audio); video made larger; the larger two-model Wan 2.2 (its two 14B models swap through RAM: the 32 GB is the tight spot); LTX-2.5, MiniMax-H3 and HunyuanVideo; LoRAs for video models; clips in story exports. Added by the trial: cards under 16 GB (the engine can keep the model in RAM, which takes 15 GB of it; smaller model files exist; untried for real); the engine's speed-up switches (`--cache-mode`, `--sage-attn`: untried); clips longer than 5 seconds.

### Trial results (step 1, 2026-11-16)

Run by hand, outside DOG.ai, in `D:\dogai-b`: the installed picture engine (`master-1104-5dcfa79`, CUDA 12) run from hard links of its own files, so nothing could be written into `D:\DOG.ai` and anything written beside the engine would show; started with `ImageServer`'s switches (loopback, a free port, the blank page, `--completed-ttl-seconds 60`, `--disable-image-metadata`, the working folder its own, `LLAMA_*` settings removed, TEMP pointed at a folder of its own) plus the video ones. 66 clips made or cancelled; about 40 looked at as sheets of frames in a browser. Three start frames drawn in DOG.ai's Images section first (novaFurryXL, Small: a girl in a wheat field, a mountain lake, an anthro wolf at a campfire). The scripts are not in the repo.

**The files** (all checked against their published checksums; 17.5 GB together):

| File | Bytes | From | Licence |
|---|---|---|---|
| `wan2.2_ti2v_5B_fp16.safetensors` (the model) | 9,999,658,848 | Hugging Face `Comfy-Org/Wan_2.2_ComfyUI_Repackaged`, `split_files/diffusion_models` | Apache 2.0 |
| `wan2.2_vae.safetensors` (its decoder) | 1,409,400,960 | the same, `split_files/vae` | Apache 2.0 |
| `umt5-xxl-encoder-Q8_0.gguf` (the text encoder) | 6,043,068,256 | Hugging Face `city96/umt5-xxl-encoder-gguf` | Apache 2.0 |
| `taew2_2.safetensors` (the fast decoder) | 22,848,048 | GitHub `madebyollin/taehv`, `safetensors/` | MIT |

- **Licence read** (Wan 2.2's model card and `LICENSE.txt`): plain Apache 2.0; Wan claims no rights over what is made; the user answers for their use, and must not share what breaks the law, harms people or spreads false information.
- The text encoder is a `.gguf`: the engine's own documentation uses it (the full one is 11.4 GB), and DOG.ai already reads GGUF headers. **It must not be listed as a chat model** (see step 3).

**Times** (one clip of 121 frames, 24 a second; sentence or start frame took the same; the card cleared, a 3 GB reserve):

| Size | Megapixels | Seconds per step | Model's own decoder | Fast decoder | Encoding to WebM | **20 steps, own decoder** | **20 steps, fast decoder** |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 832 × 480 | 0.40 | 2.98 | 26 s | not run | 4 s | 90 s | not run |
| **960 × 640 / 640 × 960 (Quick)** | 0.61 | 4.95 | 38 to 41 s | 1 to 2 s | 8 to 9 s | **150 s** | **114 s** |
| 1280 × 704 (the model's own size) | 0.90 | 8.4 | 178 s (in pieces) | not run | 11 s | about 360 s (not run whole) | not run |
| **1216 × 832 (Better)** | 1.01 | 9.95 | 192 s (in pieces) | 2 s | 11 to 14 s | **408 s** | **217 s** |

- The estimate: start + steps × seconds per step + decoder + encoding. **Start** is 3 s for a new sentence when the model is still in memory, 6 s when the program was just started and Windows still has the files in its cache, and **about 35 s after anything else large was read** (a picture checkpoint, a chat model: the text encoder then took 12 s and the model 20 s to read). A start frame adds half a second.
- 12 steps took 71 s (fast decoder, Quick) and looked hazy, unfinished; 30 steps took 160 s and were crisper. **A different step count is a different clip** (other layout), so a quick draft does not preview the slow one. 20 is the default.
- 33 frames (1.4 s): 0.64 s per step at 832 × 480, 1.75 at 1280 × 704. 193 frames (8 s) at 960 × 640: 9.3 s per step, 208 s, and visibly worse (grainy, harsh). **Frames must be 4n + 1** (120 asked gave 117).
- **A size that is no multiple of 32 is rounded up** (1000 × 600 came out 1024 × 608). DOG.ai's six picture sizes are all multiples of 32.
- Guidance, shift and sampler (short clips of one sentence, same seed): the documentation's guidance 6 with shift 3 gave the harshest picture; **guidance 5 with shift 5, Euler** was the best of four; 3.5 was softer and lost the action; two other samplers looked the same as Euler; 50 steps no better than 30. No negative prompt, an English one and the model's own Chinese one gave near enough the same clip.

**Graphics memory and RAM** (24 GB card, Windows itself holding 0.9 to 1.4 GB; 31.3 GB of RAM, 19 to 20 GB free at the start; figures are the engine's own, in GiB as the app writes them):

- **The engine places the model itself** (it prints its plan at start): with the card free everything goes on the card (text encoder 5.6, model 9.3, decoder 1.3) and **its RAM stays under 2.7 GB**; free RAM never fell below 17 GB. Nothing is loaded until the first clip (the program answers after 1.1 s holding 0.3 GB).
- **It uses what is there and gives way by itself:** peaks were 20.2 GB (Quick, own decoder: decoding all 121 frames at once), 13.8 GB (Quick, fast decoder, from the second clip on), 14.7 to 15.0 GB (Better, either decoder: the model's own then decodes in pieces after two refused attempts, which is why it takes 3 minutes). Between clips it keeps 2.3 to 10 GB.
- **`--max-vram -3`** (keep 3 GB free): the same speed, peak 20.2 GB. **Without it the first clip of a freshly started program overflowed the card once** (fast decoder: 22.4 GB plus Windows' shared memory, and the 1-second decoding took 175 s). With it, never (ten fresh starts, both decoders).
- **`--offload-to-cpu` is wrong for 32 GB:** the model sits in RAM (16.9 GB working set; **free RAM fell to 2.7 GB**), the clip was 30 s slower, and the RAM is kept afterwards. With `--mmap` as well: the same low point. The text encoder on the processor: 32 s slower, 7 GB of RAM. None of them is needed when the card is cleared.
- **Smaller cards** (asked of the engine with `--max-vram 11` and `7`, not tried on one): it kept the whole model in RAM (free RAM 4.0 GB), drew at the same speed and still peaked at 13.9 and 9.9 GB on the card. So under 16 GB is not offered.
- **For the card rule:** a clip takes the whole card (as planned). `VideoMemory.Need`: **15 GB for Quick, 16 GB for Better** (measured 13.8 and 15.0, plus the margin); the cleared card has about 22.5 GB. Start the program with `--max-vram -3`; end it when the clip has been collected or when anything else wants the card.
- **A picture straight after:** unload and end the program took 0.1 s and the card was back at 0.9 GB; the picture program answered 0.7 s later and an 832 × 1216 picture was done 21 s after the clip's program was told to go (most of it reading the checkpoint). A clip straight after that picture: 52 s for a short one, 30 s of it reading the video files again.

**The route** (`POST /sdcpp/v1/vid_gen`, then the same job routes as pictures):

- **Body used:** `prompt`, `negative_prompt`, `width`, `height`, `seed`, `video_frames`, `fps`, `init_image`, `sample_params` (`sample_method`, `sample_steps`, `guidance.txt_cfg`, `flow_shift`, `scheduler`), `preview`, `preview_interval`, `output_format` (`webm`, `webp`, `avi`), `output_compression`, `vae_tiling_params`. There is **no `batch_count` and no `embed_image_metadata`** for video.
- **Reply:** 202 with `id`, `kind` "vid_gen", `status` "queued". A second job while one runs is accepted and waits (DOG.ai never sends two).
- **The start frame goes as base64 bytes in `init_image`: PNG, JPEG and WebP were all taken** (a WebP DOG.ai drew, as it is). The first frame of the clip is the picture. **A start frame of another shape is cut from the middle** to the clip's shape.
- **Progress:** `progress.stage` goes `preparing`, `vae_encode` (only with a start frame), `conditioning` (detail `positive_prompt`, `negative_prompt`; skipped for a sentence it has just read), `sampling` (`unit` "steps", `completed`, `total`), `vae_decode`, `encoding` (detail `video`). **Decoding and encoding report no count**, also when decoding in pieces.
- **Preview:** `"proj"` is the newest frame at one sixteenth of the size (60 × 40 pixels for 960 × 640), a PNG of 5 to 7 KB after every step. With the fast decoder loaded, `"tae"` gives the frame at full size (a PNG of 1.2 to 1.4 MB after every step). Either kind cost about half a second a step against `"none"` (8 steps: 39.3 and 39.5 s against 35.6).
- **Result:** `result.b64_json` (the whole file), `mime_type`, `output_format`, `fps`, `frame_count`, and **`generation_info`, which repeats the prompt, the negative prompt, the model file names and the seed**.
- **Cancel** (`POST jobs/{id}/cancel` → 200, `cancel_requested: true`): while drawing it stops after the step in progress (5 s at Quick); a waiting job at once; **while the model's own decoder runs it takes effect only when the decoding has ended** (38 s later at Quick; it would be 3 minutes at Better). The cancelled job has no result (`error.code` "cancelled") and no preview, the engine's memory is back at its idle figure, and a clip straight after worked. A finished job is "410 job expired" a minute later; the cancelled one was still listed after 70 s (it holds nothing). An unknown job: 404.
- **The engine decides the decoder at start, not per clip:** with `--taesd` it uses the fast one for the clip (and for reading the start frame); with `--taesd-preview-only` as well, the model's own for the clip and the fast one for previews. Changing decoder is a restart (1 s, plus reading the model again).

**What it writes and prints** (the leak question; one run with made-up words in the prompt and the negative prompt, a JPEG and a PNG start frame, one clip cancelled; then `tools/leak-search.ps1` for the words and for pieces of both clips and both start frames, bytes and base64):

- **Its own folder: nothing new, nothing changed. Its TEMP folder: empty. The models folder: nothing written. What it printed: no word, no piece.** Windows' own TEMP, NVIDIA's folders: no hit. (Files that appeared in the user's TEMP during clips appear with no engine running too: another program's.)
- At its normal level it prints the model files' full paths at start, its memory plan, progress bars and timings. Never the prompt.
- **Without `--disable-image-metadata` the prompt, the negative prompt and the model file names are inside the file, in all three formats.** With it the WebM holds only the program's name ("stable-diffusion.cpp") as the writer, no date and no tags; the animated WebP has no EXIF or XMP part. The request cannot ask for this, only the start switch can: hence the WebM check in step 2.
- A watch on every file created or changed on C: and D: during clips showed nothing of the engine's (other programs' files only).

**The result:**

- **WebM, VP8, no sound; DOG.ai's own page plays it** (tried in the real app's page: a `<video>` fed from a `blob:` address played and looped; **a `data:` address is refused** by the page's content rules, `media-src 'self' blob:`). The animated WebP shows as a picture there (no controls); the AVI is MJPG.
- **File size** follows the scene and `output_compression`: at 90, five seconds were 1.1 MB (a dark scene) to 18 MB (a lake full of detail) at Quick and 11 to 27 MB at Better. **At 70 a clip was half the size of 90 and looked the same** (a face, side by side); 50 was a fifth smaller again and a little softer. **Use 70.**

**How good** (5 seconds, 20 steps, guidance 5, shift 5):

| | From the picture as first frame | From a sentence |
|---|---|---|
| A place (mountain lake, waterfall) | Good: the water falls, mist and clouds drift, the picture's own look is kept. | Good: a believable filmed lake, the camera moves forward. |
| A person (girl with red hair, wheat field) | Good motion (hair, dress, wheat); the drawing style is kept; **her closed smile becomes a grin with odd teeth** by the middle of the clip. | Fair: a photographed woman walking and turning, the field far too yellow. |
| An anthro character (grey wolf, leather jacket, campfire) | **Good: he turns his head to the fire and narrows his eyes, the flames move, he stays himself.** At 1216 × 832 the same. | **Poor at night (an almost black clip).** In daylight a man with a wolf's head, photographic, steady, not the drawn style. |

- The model reads sentences and films what they say; DOG.ai's tags and checkpoints mean nothing to it. A snow scene came out with burnt-out whites at every setting; exposure is the model's weak point from a sentence. **The start frame is what makes it useful for the owner's characters.**
- The fast decoder against the model's own on the same clip (Quick): a little softer in fine branches, faces the same.

**Left for later steps to check:** see "Not checked" in the Status block. For step 2's real-engine test: `DOGAI_SD_SERVER` at the installed `sd-server.exe` and the four files in `D:\dogai-b\models\video`; a short clip (33 frames, 832 × 480, 8 steps) takes 17 s once the model is in memory.

## M19: Write mine's opening line, Looks from a portrait (planned with the owner 2026-11-16)

**What it delivers.**

1. **Write mine writes a message, not a stand-in.** Today about one try in three comes back as "[Start a new chat]" or another line in brackets (M17 step 6: 2 of 6 by hand, 1 of 3 in the app). The model copies the line DOG.ai puts at the start of every character chat's prompt. That line gets a plain wording, chosen by a trial, **only if the characters' replies are no worse with it**. ***Built otherwise (step 1, 2026-11-16):*** the trial showed the line is not the cause (it is far worse than one in three right after a greeting and in groups, with any line); Write mine's own request was changed and the line stays, by the owner's decision. See "Trial results" below.
2. **Looks from the portrait.** The Looks box of a character or persona gets a second button beside "Draft from the description": the chat model looks at the portrait in the editor and drafts the Looks words from what it sees.

**What is there today (looked at 2026-11-16).** The line is `CharacterPrompt.ChatStart` ("[Start a new chat]"), put in by `ChatService.BuildPromptAsync` as a user turn whenever the first message that goes into the prompt is a reply: before a greeting, **and in a long chat once its oldest messages are left out and the first one kept is a reply**. Character chats and group chats get it; stories have their own ("[Begin the story.]", `StoryPrompt.OpeningTurn`, not touched: stories have no Write mine). Write mine's own instruction is also in brackets (`ImpersonateDirective`, `ImpersonateHint`). Three tests name the line (`ChatServiceTests`, `GroupChatTests`, `EngineSmokeTests`). The Looks box is `Components/Images/LooksBox.razor` over `PictureWriter.DraftLooksAsync` (a form: kind, then six to twelve tags; no thinking; tags checked); a character keeps its full picture as a PNG (`Character.Image`) and a 256-pixel square (`Avatar`); Write it for me from a picture (`PromptFromPictureAsync`, `attach.js` `bytesToJpeg`, `AnthroRule`) already turns a picture into checked tags.

**Settled already** (follows from earlier milestones, not up for choice):

- Nothing is stored for either part: no table, no migration, no new setting. A chat's saved messages do not hold the opening line, so old chats simply get the new one at their next reply.
- The portrait goes to the model the one way every picture does: turned into the 1,024-pixel JPEG in the page, made plain and checked by Core (`AttachedPictures.Plain`, `Problem`), sent as a `PromptPicture`. That JPEG is held in memory only for the one request; never a file, never the log.
- The draft never thinks, has a `MaxTokens` limit and a schema, and its tags go through the tag check (`TagList.Current` after `ExtraTags.FollowAsync`); left-out tags are dropped as everywhere.
- Looks are still never sent to the chat model in chats.
- All three looks, dark and light; an id for every new control; `CLAUDE.md` updated in the last step.

**Defaults chosen here (say so to change one):**

1. **The new line goes everywhere the old one does** (character chats and group chats, at a greeting and in a long chat). Stories keep theirs.
2. **If the trial finds replies worse with every plain wording, nothing is changed** and the owner is asked again (the other way, a plain line only in Write mine's own request, costs a re-read of the chat before and after each Write mine; the owner did not choose it).
3. **The bar for Write mine:** no stand-in, bracketed line or repeated instruction in at least 19 of 20 tries on each test chat (today about 13 of 20).
4. **Which picture is looked at:** the full portrait as it is in the editor now, saved or not; where only the small square exists, that one. No portrait: the button is off and says so.
5. **Which model:** the one in memory, else the Characters default, as "Draft from the description" does; never a swap. A model that cannot see: one line saying so, with the link to Chat models (the words of `ComposerPictures.CannotSee`).
6. **The draft replaces the words in the box, unsaved**, as the other draft does; a chosen LoRA and its strength are kept. The note under the box lists corrected and left-out tags as today.
7. **What is written:** lasting features only (what they are, hair, eyes, skin or fur, build, ears, tail, horns, marks, things never taken off). No clothes, pose, expression, background or light, although the picture shows them. An animal-like character by `AnthroRule`.
8. **Personas too** (the box is shared).

**Steps, and which model to use for each.** One step per session, each ending in a build, passing tests, a look at the app, this plan's Status updated and a commit, then the hand-over.

| # | Step | Delivers | Model / effort |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **The opening line: trial and change** (**done 2026-11-16**, as "Write mine: trial and change": the line stays, Write mine's request changed; see Status and "Trial results") | **Trial first, by hand against the engine** (as M17 step 6 did: an engine started the way `EngineManager` starts it, requests built as DOG.ai builds them; hard links in `D:\dogai-b`; the installed CUDA engine, since this is the model's behaviour, not the engine's; Huihui Qwen3.5 9B Q8 and one other chat model the owner has, no download). Wordings: today's, and three or four plain ones (for example "Hello.", "Begin.", a line of plain words with no brackets); the Write mine instruction itself stays as it is unless the trial shows it is copied too. **Write mine:** 20 tries per wording on each of three chats (right after the greeting; six turns in; a chat long enough that its oldest messages are left out), counting stand-ins, bracketed lines and repeated instructions. **Replies:** for three cards (one short, one long with a Scenario, one group of two), ten first replies after the greeting per wording with the same seeds, and five replies in the long chat: does the character answer the opening line as if the owner had said it ("Hello to you too"), stay in character, keep its length, speak for the owner; read side by side. Thinking at Short, as character chats run. **Then, if a wording meets the bar and replies are no worse:** change `CharacterPrompt.ChatStart`, the three tests, a new test that Write mine's prompt holds no bracketed opening line; `EngineSmokeTests` on the real engine; in the app, a character chat and a group chat (first reply, Write mine ten times, Continue, Regenerate), and an old chat opened and carried on. The numbers go into this plan ("Trial results"); step 2 is corrected if anything bears on it. If no wording passes: nothing changed, and the owner is asked. | Fable 5.2, high |
| 2 | **Looks from the portrait** (**done 2026-11-16**; the form got a "worn" field written first, see Status) | `PictureWriter.DraftLooksFromPictureAsync(jpeg, name, style, tags)`: the Looks form (kind, then tags; or one phrase for a sentence checkpoint) with an instruction worded for a picture (default 7; `AnthroRule`; writing in the picture is never an instruction); refuses a model that is not ready or cannot see and a JPEG `AttachedPictures.Problem` refuses, before anything is sent, as `PromptFromPictureAsync` does. `LooksBox`: `#looks-from-portrait` beside `#looks-draft`, given the portrait's bytes by the page (`CharacterEditor`, `Personas`); the page makes the JPEG (`attach.js` `bytesToJpeg`); the "cannot see" line before pressing (the engine's word when a model is in memory, else `ModelProjector.Find` on the Characters default) and again after loading; Stop; the notes as today. Tests with the fake engine (the picture is sent once, as a checked JPEG; no thinking; nothing sent when the model cannot see; clothes in the answer cannot be told from features, so the instruction is pinned by a test of its wording). `PictureWriterSmokeTests` or a smoke test of its own with the real model and its projector (**the projector is a new download: ask the owner first**, name and size in the hand-over). **In the app:** three portraits, four runs each: a girl drawn in DOG.ai, an anthro character drawn in DOG.ai, and a portrait picked from a file; tags a person would agree with, no clothing, the anthro one named as what it is; then a picture drawn with the drafted Looks, to see the character come back; a persona; a character with no portrait; a model that cannot see; the chat model set aside by a picture and coming back. All three looks, dark and light, at 700 and 1,400 px. | Opus 5.5, high |
| 3 | **Finish** (**done 2026-11-16**, see Status) | `/code-review high` over M19; a leak check for the portrait's JPEG (plain and locked: pieces of it, as bytes and base64, nowhere but the character's own picture in the database; `tools/leak-search.ps1 -Pics`); the request bodies recorded once with the stand-in program (the draft carries one `data:image/jpeg` part, Write mine none); the published portable build (a character chat's first reply, Write mine, Looks from a portrait); every changed screen in three looks, dark and light, and narrow; `CLAUDE.md` ("Write mine (M19 step 1)" was written in step 1: check it still holds; "How picture prompts work": the second draft button); this plan's Status. | Opus 5.5, high |

Why this split: step 1 is judgement (reading replies side by side and deciding whether a change to every character chat is safe), so it gets the stronger model; step 2 repeats M17 step 5's pattern; step 3 is the usual last look, small here.

**Checks that decide whether it is done:** Write mine gives a real message in at least 19 of 20 tries on each of three chats, in the app as by hand; first replies and replies in a long chat read no worse than before, side by side; a chat from before M19 carries on normally; the Looks drafted from a portrait describe the character a person sees, with no clothing, and a picture drawn from them shows the same character; with a model that cannot see, the button says so and nothing is sent; nothing of the portrait is anywhere on disk that it was not before.

**Not in M19** (put to the owner on 2026-11-16 with their cost, and passed over; say so to take one up): the picture input's projector on the processor, as a switch per model (frees 1.1 GB of graphics memory, so Huihui Q8 would stay loaded beside a Normal picture; 3 to 5 s to read a picture instead of 0.3 s; 1 to 2 sessions); the model seeing the pictures DOG.ai drew for its replies (2 to 3 sessions; up to 990 tokens per illustrated reply); pictures in stories (about 2 sessions); scanned pages for Documents (4 to 5 sessions, a trial first: small print was invented at 1,024 pixels in M17's trial); a local OpenAI-compatible API (3 to 4 sessions, design first); checks of other projectors and model families (one session, downloads of 0.6 to 0.9 GB per projector and 7 to 8 GB for another family's model). Decided and closed: no note about the winget engine's memory step, no pixel-size limit on chosen pictures.

### Trial results (step 1, 2026-11-16)

**How it was run.** A scratch program around DOG.ai's own `ChatService`, chat store and `EngineManager` (a detached worktree of the commit before the change, with switches for the trial only: the opening line, a seed per request, and the ways of asking below), so every request was built exactly as the app builds it. The installed CUDA engine (b12359), 8,192 tokens, the Balanced style, thinking at Short. Two models: **Huihui Qwen3.5 9B Q8** (ChatML, thinks) and the owner's **Gemma 2 9B writer, Q6_K** (strict template: no system message, user and reply strictly by turns; does not think). Hard links in `D:\dogai-b`, removed afterwards.

**The chats** (made once per model with today's line, then frozen; persona Alex, a mapmaker): **A** a short card (Pip, two sentences) right after its greeting; **B** a long card with Scenario and examples (Ilse) right after its greeting; **C** a group of two (Bram and Wren) at the greeting; **D** Ilse six turns in; **E** the group six turns in; **F** Ilse, 21 exchanges, the oldest 11 messages left out; **G** the group after a long run of replies with no message of the owner's (only the newest reply is left in the prompt, see Status).

**Counted as a real message:** not empty, no line wholly in brackets, not the opening line given back, not the instruction or the card given back. 20 tries each, the same 20 seeds for every row.

**1. Write mine as it was** (an instruction in the system message, the model writes into an open user turn), by opening line. Real messages of 20:

| Opening line | A | B | C | D | E | F | G |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Huihui** "[Start a new chat]" (today) | 0 | 2 | 0 | 18 | 2 | 19 | 0 |
| "Start a new chat." | 1 | 14 | 1 | 19 | 2 | 19 | 0 |
| "Hello." | 5 | 18 | 3 | 18 | 2 | 19 | 1 |
| "Begin." | 2 | 15 | 1 | 19 | 1 | 19 | 0 of 10 |
| "Let's begin." | 0 | 19 | 0 | 19 | 1 | 19 | not run |
| "The chat begins." | 1 | 19 | 1 | 19 | 5 | 19 | not run |
| **Gemma 2** "[Start a new chat]" (today) | 3 | 19 | 3 | 20 | 19 | 20 | 2 |
| "Start a new chat." | 5 | 19 | 3 | 20 | 20 | 20 | 2 |
| "Hello." | 2 | 17 | 3 | 20 | 20 | 20 | 1 |
| "Begin." | 2 | 16 | 2 | 20 | 20 | 20 | 1 |
| "Let's begin." | 2 | 20 | 2 | 20 | 20 | 20 | 1 |
| "The chat begins." | 2 | 19 | 3 | 20 | 20 | 20 | 1 |

No line reaches 19 of 20 on A, C or G on either model. In F the line is not in the prompt (the first message kept is the owner's), so every row gave the same 20 drafts. What came back on A with today's line (Huihui): the instruction 14 times (word for word in 13), the card once, "[Start a new chat]" twice, other bracketed lines 3. With a plain line on B, 14 to 17 of the "real" drafts were written as a script ("Alex: …", some going on as "Ilse: …"); only "Hello." gave plain messages there.

**2. Other ways of asking** (Huihui, real messages of 20; where two figures are given, the second is with the owner's own label, "Alex:" or "[Alex]", taken off the start, which the change does):

| Way | A | B | C | D | E | F | G |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Open turn, no instruction in the system message, today's line | 0 | 16 | 0 | 18 | 1 | 20 | 0 |
| The same, "Hello." | 20 | 18 | 3 | 19 | 7 | 20 | 0 |
| The same, "Let's begin." | 7 | 20 | 0 | 20 | 8 | 20 | 0 |
| The same, "The chat begins." | 10 | 20 | 1 | 20 | 6 | 20 | 0 |
| Open turn, the instruction without its brackets, "Hello." | 16 | 19 | 18 | 18 | 2 | 19 | 1 |
| **An ordinary request** (the chat, then a turn that asks), today's line | 20 | 20 | 18 → 20 | 20 | 19 → 20 | 20 | 5 → 20 |
| The same, "Hello." | 20 | 20 | 18 → 20 | 20 | 19 → 20 | 20 | 4 → 20 |
| The same, asking for the owner's length ("about the same length") | 20 | 20 | 19 → 20 | 20 | 19 → 20 | 20 | 14 → 20 |

Gemma 2: the ordinary request 20 of 20 on all seven chats with each of the three wordings (it hardly ever labels a draft); the roles changed over 20 of 20 on all seven; the open turn without the instruction and with "Hello." 1, 10, 2, 19, 20, 20, 0.

**Length of a draft** (median characters; the owner's messages in these chats are about 80): Huihui, ordinary request as worded before 406 to 1,014, "about the same length" 345 to 512, "no longer than they are" 246 to 412; roles changed over 516 to 1,233; an open-turn draft, when it was a message, 39 to 183. Gemma 2: 83 to 255, 72 to 140, 55 to 149.

**Thinking** (Huihui, the ordinary request at Short): 7 s a draft against 1 s, and no better (20 and 20 of 20 on A and B, more labels); so a draft does not think.

**3. The code as built** (20 tries per chat, a new seed each): Huihui 20 of 20 on all seven (median 243 to 420 characters); Gemma 2 20 of 20 on all seven (62 to 139). About one second a draft.

**4. Replies, today's line against "Hello."** (Gemma 2 only, 42 replies each, same seeds: ten first replies each for the short card, the long card and the group, five replies six turns in, five in the group's long run, two in the long chat). Nobody spoke for the owner or left character with either line. With "Hello." the first replies were a little shorter (median 293 → 248, 421 → 348, 283 → 253 characters), and **when the owner's first message was "Hi Pip.", four of five replies remarked on the "Hi" itself** ("Pip heard visitor say 'Hi'! Pip does like 'Hi'!"), none with today's line; the long card's and the group's replies read alike. On Huihui the comparison was stopped after 30 replies with today's line, once the owner had decided the line stays; in those, the model's thinking named "[Start a new chat]" in 3 of 30, each time as set-up and not as something the owner said ("[Start a new chat] (System prompt setup)"), and no reply mentioned it.

**What follows from it.** The line was not what Write mine copied most, and no wording of it reaches the bar; the ordinary request reaches it with any line; a plain "Hello." is not harmless for replies. So only Write mine's request changed.

## M17: Image understanding (planned and approved by the owner 2026-11-15)

**What it delivers.** The chat model can look at a picture.

1. **A picture in a chat message.** In General, character chats and group chats the chat box takes pictures (a button, paste, or a file dropped on it); the model answers about what it sees, and the picture stays in the chat with its message.
2. **A prompt from a picture.** In Images, **Write it for me…** can start from a picture in place of a sentence: the chat model looks at it and writes a prompt, checked against the tag list as today. The picture is only looked at; nothing is drawn from it (image-to-image stays out).
3. **Models knows which chat models can see.** A "Sees pictures" mark on Recommended, Browse and On this PC › Chat models; the extra file such a model needs is downloaded with it.
4. **Three small things that ride along:** an Illustrate button on replies in General chats (character and group chats have one); the owner's decision on LoRA file words (Status, 2026-11-15); and two checks left open by M15 and M16.

**How picture input works, and what is on this PC (looked at 2026-11-15).** The chat engine can load a second file beside a chat model, its *projector* (a GGUF with "mmproj" in its name, made for one model family). With it, a message can carry pictures. The chat engine on this PC has the switch (`--mmproj`). **None of the chat models in `D:\DOG.ai\Models` has a projector file**, so nothing here can see a picture today. *The trial (2026-11-16) found:* the four Qwen models here can all see once their projector is beside them, and each one's own repository has it (about 0.9 GB); the two Gemma 2 writers cannot. DOG.ai hides projector files from its lists on purpose (`Quantization.IsAddOn`) and never passes one to the engine; `PromptMessage` is text only; messages have nowhere to keep a picture.

**Settled already** (follows from earlier milestones, not up for choice):

- A picture goes only to the chat engine on `127.0.0.1`. Nothing new goes online; the downloads are ordinary model downloads through the Models section.
- An attached picture is kept only in the database (so under the lock, in backups, moved with a portable folder), never as a file, a cache or in TEMP. It is deleted with its message and leaves the disk the way removed data does.
- A file from outside is untrusted. It is opened only by the page (the browser control), reduced and written again there as a plain picture, so what DOG.ai keeps has no camera, place or date information, and no code of DOG.ai's own reads a foreign picture file. Anything that is not a plain picture (SVG included) is refused. The owner's file is never changed, moved or copied.
- Pictures and what the model says about them are never written to the log.
- All three looks, dark and light; ids for every new control; `CLAUDE.md` gets "How image understanding works".

**Defaults chosen here (say so to change one):**

1. **Where:** General, character chats and group chats (they share the chat box). Not stories.
2. **How many and how large:** up to four pictures a message, each reduced to **1,024 pixels on the long side** and kept as a JPEG at quality 90 (*from the trial; for the owner to confirm*: at most 1,026 tokens a picture, usually 580 to 680).
3. **The button shows only when it can work**, as the voice buttons do: when the chat's model can see pictures. Pasting a picture with a model that cannot shows one line saying so and where such models are marked.
4. **Older pictures:** a picture stays in what the model is given for as long as its message does (the oldest messages are left out first when room runs out, as today). The context meter counts it. With a model that cannot see, the picture is replaced by a short note for the model, and the message says "The model could not see this picture".
5. **Text-only jobs stay text-only:** summaries, recalled messages, search, the markdown export and a story made from a chat get "[picture]" where a picture was, never the picture.
6. **A prompt from a picture uses the default model for General**, as Write it for me does. If that model cannot see, the dialog says so.

**For the owner to confirm** (from the trial; step 2 builds the recommended one unless told otherwise). *All three were built as recommended in step 2 (2026-11-16); the owner did not say otherwise.*

1. **Pictures are reduced to 1,024 pixels on the long side** (recommended), not 1,536. At 1,024 a picture costs at most 1,026 tokens and four cost at most 4,104 of the 8,192; at 1,536 one square picture costs 2,306 and four would not fit. The price: very small text is lost (a file name's odd letters were misread at every size; the sentences on a full-HD screenshot were right at 1,024 and invented at 768).
2. **The projector is loaded whenever the model has one** (recommended), with **Remove picture input** in Models as the way to switch it off. It costs 1.1 GB of graphics memory all the time: Huihui Q8 then keeps its place beside a Small picture (about 1.1 GB to spare by DOG.ai's rule, 2.2 GB today) but is set aside for a Normal one (0.65 GB to spare today, short by about 0.45 GB then), and loses the "Fits beside pictures" mark, which judges a Normal picture. The other way would be a switch per model.
3. **The projector offered is the F16 file** (0.92 GB for the 9B models, 0.93 GB for the 27B and 40B), the only one tried. Repositories also hold a Q8 (0.62 GB, mradermacher's) or BF16 and F32 files; F32 is twice the size for nothing and is never offered.

**Trial results (step 1, 2026-11-16).** Run by hand, outside DOG.ai, with PowerShell scripts against the engine's own address (none is in the repo). Hard links in `D:\dogai-b` to Huihui Qwen3.5 9B abliterated Q8, Defiant Fable 9B Q8, the Qwen3.8 27B Q4_K_M and a Gemma 2 writer; the projector downloaded there with the owner's yes; context 8,192; each engine started the way DOG.ai starts it (same switches, a key in the environment, no `LLAMA_ARG_*`), plus `--mmproj`. Test pictures: a photo-like rendered landscape that ships with Windows (no real photograph is on this PC outside the owner's own files), screenshots of DOG.ai at 1400 × 860 and 1920 × 1080, and three pictures drawn in DOG.ai's Images section on a throwaway data folder (640 × 960, 960 × 640, 832 × 1216, as the WebP files DOG.ai keeps).

*The two engines.* The winget one (build 12314) is the **Vulkan** build; the one DOG.ai installed (build 12359) is **CUDA**. Everything below was the same on both unless it says otherwise.

| | Installed (CUDA) | winget (Vulkan) |
|---|---|---|
| Huihui Q8 alone, graphics memory | 8,860 MiB | 8,520 MiB |
| With the projector | 9,986 MiB (+1,126) | 9,644 MiB (+1,124) |
| With the projector and `--image-max-tokens 1024` | 9,858 at load, 9,930 after pictures (+1,070) | 9,516 at load, 9,791 after pictures |
| Load time, without → with | 4.5 → 5.3 s | 5.5 → 7.1 s |
| Reading a 1024 × 640 picture (642 tokens) | 0.18 s | 0.20 s |
| Reading 1024 × 1024 (1,026 tokens) | 0.24 to 0.39 s | 0.28 to 0.53 s |
| Reading four pictures in one message (2,854 tokens) | 0.65 s | 0.72 s |
| Reading 2048 × 2048 (4,098 tokens) | 1.1 s | 1.3 to 1.6 s |
| Extra memory kept after pictures up to 1024 × 1024 | 0.07 GB | 0.07 to 0.1 GB |
| Extra memory kept after pictures up to 4,098 tokens | 0.42 GB | **2.7 to 3.2 GB** (see below) |
| A 250-token text reply, without → with the projector | 84.9 → 86.7 tokens a second, the same opening words | 88.9 → 86.1, the same opening words |

- **Tokens.** A picture costs round(width ÷ 32) × round(height ÷ 32) + 2, halves rounded to the even number (1008 → 32, 1040 → 32, 944 → 30). Seen: 256 × 160 = 42, 512 × 320 = 162, 640 × 960 = 602, 768 × 768 = 578, 832 × 1216 = 990, 1024 × 640 = 642, 1024 × 1024 = 1,026, 1536 × 960 = 1,442, 1536 × 1536 = 2,306, 2048 × 2048 = 4,098. Anything larger is reduced by the engine to at most 4,096 + 2 (the 6400 × 4000 original cost 4,002), anything tiny is raised to 9 + 2. **`--image-max-tokens 1024` caps it**: 1536 × 1536, 2048 × 2048 and the original then cost 1,026, 1,026 and 1,002. The rule belongs to this model family (its projector's header says patch 16, merge 2); another family counts differently.
- **The engine counts for us.** `POST /v1/messages/count_tokens` (the message with the picture as `{"type":"image","source":{"type":"base64","media_type":…,"data":…}}`) answered at once and gave exactly the picture's tokens on both builds, the cap included (173 against 175 for the whole request: the two tokens of difference are the same with no picture). `apply-template` shows the picture as a marker (`<__media_…__>`, a new random one at every start, also in `GET /props` as `media_marker`); tokenising that text gives a wrong count (40 against 178), and **a message that contains the marker's own text is refused** (400 "Failed to tokenize prompt").
- **Does the model see?** `GET /props` → `modalities.vision` is true with a projector, false without.
- **Formats.** Read: PNG, JPEG, BMP, GIF (and a PNG labelled as JPEG: the label does not matter). **Refused: WebP** (every kept picture; "Failed to load image or audio file", and the log says "failed to decode webp buffer" after **"failed to launch ffprobe"**: on bytes it cannot read the engine tries to start a program of that name), TIFF, SVG, words labelled as a picture. JPEG at quality 90 and 75 read the same as PNG at half and a third of the bytes.
- **Addresses.** Given `http://…` in place of the picture's bytes, build 12314 **tried to fetch it** ("cannot make GET request" after 2 s against a closed port on this PC); build 12359 refused only because the host "is not a public address". `file://` is refused unless `--media-path` is set; a bare path is "Invalid base64 value". Base64 with no `data:` in front is accepted.
- **Turns and the cache.** Picture in turn 1, a text question in turn 2: 52 new tokens, 663 from the cache. A second picture in turn 3: only it is read. The same request again (regenerate): 4 new tokens. Turn 2 edited: the picture is not read again. Another chat in between, then back: the first picture came from the engine's own cache, the second was read again (0.15 s). **The same picture as other bytes** (saved again as PNG) **is a different picture**: everything is read again. So the stored bytes are what is sent, every time.
- **Where a picture may be.** In a user turn, between pieces of text, in the system message and in an assistant turn: all answered correctly. A group chat turn ("Ann: … [picture] Nell: … [Write Bo's reply.]") and a character's system message with the picture in the user's turn gave replies in character about the picture.
- **Thinking and the form.** No thinking with the helper's kind of form (people, then tags): the form filled, 0.7 s. Thinking with a budget of 512 and `reasoning_control`: about 2,000 characters of thinking, then the answer; the same with the form. 
- **Too much for the room.** The engine cuts nothing: 400 `exceed_context_size_error` with `n_prompt_tokens` and `n_ctx` ("request (3291 tokens) exceeds the available context size (2048 tokens)"), for one large picture, for four, and for a long chat with a picture at its start. It goes on answering afterwards.
- **Without a projector.** A picture sent to a model loaded without one: 500 "image input is not supported - hint: if this is unexpected, you may need to provide the mmproj". **The engine never takes a projector by itself**: with the file beside the model under three names (`<model>.mmproj-f16.gguf`, `mmproj-F16.gguf`, `mmproj-<model>.gguf`) and no `--mmproj`, it loaded as before, the same memory to the megabyte.
- **A projector that does not belong.** Huihui's with **Defiant Fable** (another fine-tune of Qwen3.5 9B): loads, and sees as well (it called the wolf knight "a stoic wolf knight", and wrote "furry, humanoid"). With the **27B** and with a **Gemma 2** model: the engine loads the whole model first, then stops with exit code 1 after 23 to 40 s: "mismatch between text model (n_embd = 5120) and mmproj (n_embd = 4096)". The two numbers are in the files' headers (`clip.vision.projection_dim` in the projector; the model's embedding length, which `GgufInfo` reads already), so DOG.ai can tell before it starts anything. The projector's header: `general.architecture = clip`, `general.type = mmproj`, `clip.has_vision_encoder = true`, `clip.projector_type = qwen3vl_merger`.
- **The original model's projector.** Not downloaded. What is known: Defiant Fable's own repository ships `mmproj-F16.gguf` with the same checksum as the one in `unsloth/Qwen3.5-9B-GGUF` (the original's), and Huihui's differs from it by 64 bytes in size (its header carries Huihui's name). Together with the Defiant Fable run above: within one base model the projectors are interchangeable, and the makers of uncensored versions ship the original's.
- **The projector on the processor** (`--no-mmproj-offload`): no graphics memory at all, but 2.8 s for a 1024 × 640 picture, 5.0 s for 1024 × 1024 and 14.7 s for four. Not proposed; it is there if the card is ever too full.
- **The Vulkan build's memory.** After a picture of more than about 2,000 tokens the winget engine's own graphics memory rose by 2.2 to 2.8 GB within the next request or two and stayed (three runs; the per-process counter, not the whole card). With pictures up to 1024 × 1024, or with the cap, it did not (+0.1 GB). The CUDA build never did it. One more reason for the size and the cap. *Step 6 (in the app, 26 pictures and more in one chat):* with the size and the cap twenty pictures leave both builds flat, but the Vulkan build still steps up once later, when the chat has outgrown the model's room and the engine reads the prompt again from a changed start: by 2.2 GB with pictures in the prompt and by 0.7 GB in a chat with none, then no further. It is that build's behaviour in any long chat, not the pictures' size.
- **What the engine writes.** Its output: load lines, per request the token counts and timings, a warning or two per picture (`find_slot: non-consecutive token position …`, harmless), and the errors above. No prompt, no reply, no picture data (searched for the questions' and answers' words and for base64). Nothing in its TEMP folder (given a folder of its own for the run), nothing new in either engine's folder.
- **How well it sees** (Huihui Q8, no thinking):
  - *Landscape* (1024 × 640 and 512 × 320, the same answers): sunset; snowy hills with trees on the left shore; grassy marshland on the right; no person, animal or building; "computer rendering".
  - *Screenshot, 1400 × 860* (Settings › Chats and stories): at 1400, 1024 and 768 pixels it named the three thinking settings with their values, the selected entry, quoted the grey line under the first list exactly, and at 1400 and 1024 the status bar ("No model loaded VRAM 8.8 GB / 24.0 GB"). **At 512 it made things up** ("Short (up to 32 tokens)", a status bar that is not there).
  - *Screenshot, 1920 × 1080* (Settings › Pictures): the settings' values and a quoted sentence right at 1920, 1536, 1280 and 1024 (PNG and JPEG alike); at 768 the sentence was invented. The checkpoint's file name (`illustrious_novaFurryXL_ilV180A`) was wrong in its last part at every size, 1920 included ("…_111V180A", "…_jjV180A"), and mostly wrong at 1024 and below.
  - *Three drawn pictures into tags* (the helper's instruction reworded for a picture, its form, temperature 0.6; four runs each at 640 and 384 pixels, about 2.5 s a run). Girl in a sunflower field: 1girl, red hair, green eyes, straw hat, white dress, smile, sunflowers, blue sky, clouds, day every time; standing and outdoors in two of four (once "sitting", which is wrong); "long hair" and "solo" never. Forest waterfall: no humans, forest, waterfall, moss, sunlight, trees, rocks every time, people 0 every time. **Anthro wolf knight: armor, red cape, holding sword, castle, moon, night every time, but "wolf ears, silver hair" on a "1boy" (twice "1girl")**, never the kind of character. The tags at 384 pixels were as good as at 640.

**Steps, and which model to use for each.** One step per session, each ending in a build, passing tests, a look at the app, this plan's Status updated and a commit, then the hand-over.

| # | Step | Delivers | Model / effort |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Trial** (no app code). **Done 2026-11-16** (see "Trial results" above and Status) | By hand, outside DOG.ai, in a scratch folder. Which model: candidates looked up on Hugging Face then; **the owner is asked before each download, with its name and size**.
