For each: does it load with its projector on the chat engine this PC has and on the one DOG.ai installs; does a projector made for the original model work with the owner's uncensored versions; graphics memory the projector adds; seconds to read a picture; how many tokens a picture takes at several sizes and whether that can be capped; which file formats the engine reads (kept pictures are WebP); several pictures in one message and over several turns, and whether a later turn reads them again; with thinking off and with a fixed answer form (the Images helper needs both); counting tokens and showing the prompt when a picture is in it; what happens when a picture is sent to a model without a projector; what the engine prints and writes to disk when given a picture. How well it sees: a photo, a screenshot with text, and three kept pictures turned into tags and compared with their own prompts. Ends with the model to recommend, the numbers for the budget and for fit, the open points settled, and steps 2 to 6 corrected in this plan. | Fable 5.2, high |
| 2 | **Core, storage and engine**. **Done 2026-11-16** (see Status) | Pictures on a message: a table of their own (not `Pictures`, whose rows are drawn pictures with their settings), the picture as its last column, deleted with the message, with its migration; edit, regenerate and swipes keep the question's pictures. **What is kept** *(trial)*: a JPEG at quality 90, at most 1,024 pixels on the long side, checked in Core before it is stored and before it is sent (a JPEG by its bytes, its size, nothing in it but the picture), the way `PicturePreview.Problem` checks previews; the stored bytes are what is sent, every time (the same picture as other bytes is read again by the engine). **The prompt:** `PromptMessage` carries pictures; `LlamaApi` sends each as an `image_url` part holding `data:image/jpeg;base64,…`, built from the stored bytes and from nothing else. **Never an address** (the engine fetches web addresses) and never anything but a checked JPEG (on bytes it cannot read the engine tries to start `ffprobe`); a test fails on any other kind of part. Text is counted without the picture parts (the engine's marker tokenises wrongly, and its text must never reach a prompt); each picture is counted by the engine itself (`POST /v1/messages/count_tokens`, remembered per picture and model in memory, asked only while `IsLoaded` so counting never wakes a model), else by the rule round(w ÷ 32) × round(h ÷ 32) + 2, at most 1,026. The budget leaves out the oldest messages first as today, and a picture goes only with its message; the engine's `exceed_context_size_error` is put into words; the prompt inspector shows DOG.ai's own marker with the picture's size and tokens; default 5 above. **The engine:** a model's projector found by rule in the model's own folder: a `.gguf` with "mmproj" in its name whose header says `general.type = mmproj` and whose `clip.vision.projection_dim` equals the model's embedding length (of several: F16, then BF16, then Q8_0, never F32); no saved full path, so `PortableMove` needs nothing. Given on load as `--mmproj <file> --image-max-tokens 1024`; never `--media-path`, `--mmproj-url` or `--mmproj-auto`; `EngineEnvironment.Clean` also drops `MTMD_*`. `IChatEngine` says whether the loaded model sees (`GET /props` → `modalities.vision`). A model without a projector is started exactly as today (the engine takes none by itself). Fit, "Fits beside pictures" and the measured memory count the projector (its file size + 0.25 GB; a measurement taken with the projector is never used for a load without it, nor the other way round). A projector that does not belong is not passed (the engine would load the whole model and then stop); if the engine still stops with one, the model is loaded again without it and says so. **LoRA file words sent cleaned** (the picture maker and the test picture; `CLAUDE.md`'s "Not cleaned on purpose" changed). Tests with the fake engine and the real database; a real-engine test behind an environment variable; lock, backup, restore and portable checks for the new table; a deleted message's picture gone from the data folder. **In the app: a locked data folder made by the previous build, opened by the new one** (the check left open since M16 step 1; this step brings a migration of its own). No screen yet. | Fable 5.2, high |
| 3 | **Models**. **Done 2026-11-16** (see Status) | "Sees pictures" on Recommended, Browse and On this PC › Chat models. *(Trial)* On Recommended and Browse the mark comes from the repository's file list (a `.gguf` with "mmproj" in its name), not from Hugging Face's `image-text-to-text` tag, which the owner's own Huihui repository lacks; on this PC from step 2's rule. A model with a projector in its repository is offered with it ("with picture input, + 0.9 GB"; the F16 file, else BF16, else Q8_0, never F32; names seen: `mmproj-F16.gguf` for every size of model in a repository, and `<model>.mmproj-f16.gguf`) and both land in one folder; a model here without its projector offers **Get picture input** (from the repository it came from), one with it offers **Remove picture input**; Delete removes the projector too only when no other model in the folder uses it. **No new entry in the Recommended list is needed**: `mradermacher/Huihui-Qwen3.5-9B-abliterated-GGUF` (General and Characters) and `unsloth/Qwen3.5-9B-GGUF` are in it and both have projectors; they get the mark. Fit badges count the projector (file size + 0.25 GB), and "Fits beside pictures" is judged with it. A note in the defaults block when the General default cannot see. All three looks, dark and light. | Opus 5.5, high |
| 4 | **Chats**. **Done 2026-11-16** (see Status) | The chat box: attach button, paste, drop; small previews above the box with a remove button each; reducing and rewriting in the page *(trial: to 1,024 pixels on the long side, JPEG at quality 90, white behind anything see-through; the page may open PNG, JPEG, WebP, BMP and a GIF's first frame, never SVG; what reaches Core is always that JPEG)*. Pictures shown with their message, a large view on a click. The notes of defaults 3 and 4; the context meter (a picture's tokens as the engine counts them; a line when the pictures do not fit the context: four can take 4,104 of 8,192). One line of help says small print may be misread (the trial: sentences on a full-HD screenshot were read right, a file name's odd letters were not). Editing a message keeps or removes its pictures. General, a character chat and a group chat, each with a real model answering about real pictures. All three looks, dark and light, at 700, 1000 and 1400 px. | Opus 5.5, high |
| 5 | **Images and General**. **Done 2026-11-16** (see Status) | **Write it for me… › From a picture**: the picture shown on the Make tab, a kept picture, or a file; the model fills the same form as today (how many people, then tags; no thinking; tags looked up; the rating from Settings › Pictures; no quality or trigger words), and the result goes to Add after the prompt / Replace the prompt as today. The picture is held in memory only, like the helper's sentence. *(Trial)* **The chat engine refuses WebP, so the try and the kept picture are turned into the same 1,024-pixel JPEG in the page first** (the path step 4 built; `pictures.js` already makes JPEGs for EPUB); tags from a 384-pixel copy were as good as from a larger one, so nothing bigger is needed. The form's wording is for a picture ("how many people the picture shows"), and **it must say what an anthro or animal-like character is**: in the trial a wolf knight came back as "1boy, wolf ears, silver hair", twice as "1girl". Checked with at least three pictures, three runs each, against their own prompts (a person, a place without people, an anthro character); about 2.5 s a run. The card: the chat model comes back through `EnsureReadyAsync` while the picture model may be loaded. **Illustrate on replies in General chats** (the planner with nobody's Looks). All three looks, dark and light. | Opus 5.5, high |
| 6 | **Finish**. **Done 2026-11-16** (see Status) | Leak check as in M16 step 6, with pictures in place of words: pictures attached and sent, attached and removed before sending, a message with a picture deleted, a chat deleted, a file used for From a picture; pieces of each searched for as bytes and as base64 in the data folder, models folder, engine folders and TEMP, plain and locked (*trial:* the engine wrote nothing to TEMP or its folder and printed counts and timings only; the check confirms it in the app, the log included). Every request body with a picture holds only `data:image/jpeg` parts. **Graphics memory after twenty pictures in one chat on both engines** (the Vulkan build grew by 2.2 to 2.8 GB after pictures over about 2,000 tokens; with the 1,024 size and the cap it must not). The published portable build. `/code-review high`. Every new screen in three looks, dark and light, and narrow. **Unload everything while a chat model is loading and during a benchmark** (left open since M15). `CLAUDE.md` ("How image understanding works"), this plan's Status. | Fable 5.2, xhigh |

Why this split: steps 1, 2 and 6 are where a wrong judgement costs most (what the engine can really do, private pictures on disk and a migration, the last look). Steps 3 to 5 repeat patterns the code has (the "Fits beside pictures" chip and paired downloads, the picture maker's canvas code and `MessagePictures`, the prompt helper).

**Checks that decide whether it is done:** a real model answers correctly about a photo, a screenshot with text and a kept picture, in each of the three kinds of chat; a chat with pictures survives lock, backup and restore, and a portable move; a deleted picture is nowhere on disk; a chat model without a projector behaves exactly as before M17 (same prompt, same memory, same speed); a picture in a chat makes the context meter move by what the engine then reports; From a picture on a kept picture gives tags a person would agree with.

**Not checked by the trial** (for the steps that touch them; *checked since:* a real photograph, an animated GIF, Answer now and a streamed reply with a picture, the Vulkan build's memory in long chats; the rest is still open, see the step 6 entry in Status): a real photograph; the Q8 projector, the original Qwen3.5 9B's projector file, and the 27B and 40B with their own (a large model with a projector on this card: its file is 18 GB, the projector adds about 1.1); any model family but Qwen3.5 (its token rule and whether `--image-max-tokens` caps it); Answer now and a streamed reply with a picture in the prompt (the trial asked without streaming); an animated GIF; an engine older than builds 12314 and 12359 (whether it knows `--image-max-tokens` and `count_tokens`; an unknown switch stops the engine, which step 2's "load again without it" covers); a PC without a graphics card; why the Vulkan build's memory grows.

**Not in M17 (could follow;** *drafting Looks from a portrait became M19, the rest was passed over by the owner on 2026-11-16 and stays open***):** the model seeing the pictures DOG.ai drew for its own replies (the trial: a picture in an assistant turn is read like any other); the projector on the processor when the card is full; pictures in stories; drafting a character's Looks from their portrait; reading scanned pages for Documents with the model; video and sound as input; anything that draws from a picture. The other points under "Not checked" in Status (an upscaler that enlarges ×2, a sentence checkpoint, more than 420 kept pictures and the rest) stay open, as does unsaved typing in the lore editor, personas and Assistants and prompts.

## M18: Smaller pictures, settings in pictures, thinking budget (planned 2026-11-15)

The owner's three requests (2026-11-15), in the owner's words: (1) "Images should be defaulted to a lower resolution. It may be expanded on manually." (2) "When in character or story chat, the tags generated should try and pull all the detail about the background as well as the character. For example, if the story is in a forest, those tags should be used so the image doesn't show an indoor scene." (3) "I believe thinking is important to the character and story mode, but we have to have options to dial it back if it takes too long. Can there be a throttle on the thinking?"

M18 does not depend on M17 and M17 does not depend on it: the owner may build either first. Building M18 first gives M17's "Illustrate in General" the better place words for free.

### 1. Pictures start smaller, made larger by hand

**Today.** Every picture starts at about one megapixel: Tall 832 × 1216, Square 1024 × 1024, Wide 1216 × 832 (`PictureLevels`), in the Images section (`ImagesControls.StartFor`), the picture maker (portraits, story and chat pictures) and the test picture. Draft makes fewer steps, not a smaller picture. Larger already exists: Fine (hires fix), and in Images Upscale › Redraw larger (same seed, same layout).

**What changes.**

- **A size choice: Small or Normal**, beside shape and quality. Small is the new default everywhere a picture is started; Normal is today's size. **Small is 640 × 960 tall, 960 × 640 wide, 768 × 768 square** (about 0.6 megapixels; chosen by looking in the trial: one step smaller breaks faces and hands). Every side is a multiple of 64, because the picture engine rounds up to one.
- **Making it larger by hand:** every try has **Make larger**, which redraws it from the same seed with a hires fix, so the layout stays and only the detail grows: **Small ×1.5 gives 960 × 1472** (wide 1472 × 960, square 1152 × 1152) with Fine's own second pass (half the steps, strength 0.3, Lanczos); Normal → Fine's size as today. In Images it is the existing Upscale › Redraw larger offered from a Small try; in the picture maker it is a button beside Try again. Typing a width and height in the Images side panel stays as it is. The same seed at another size is another picture (seen in the trial), so Make larger never simply draws at the larger size.
- **Fits beside the chat model** (`PictureMemory.FitsBeside`; simpler than first planned, since Small is the start everywhere): (a) when Settings says Normal and a Normal picture would set the loaded chat model aside where a Small one would not, the picture starts Small and the note says so; (b) Make larger uses ×1.5, or ×1.25 (exactly Normal's size, a little softer) when only that fits beside the chat model: with Huihui Q8 loaded ×1.5 needs the chat model set aside and ×1.25 does not. If even Small does not fit, it is drawn as today (the chat model set aside) and the note says so.
- **Settings › Pictures** gets "Start pictures at: Small / Normal" (`PicturePreferences`); the Images section's remembered size wins there once the owner has chosen one.

**Must not change.** A kept picture redraws byte for byte (its size is in its row). `PictureTries.Adopt` / `BaseOf` tell a kept picture's size from its row as they tell quality from steps and hires fix; Try again on a kept Normal picture stays Normal. `ImageRequest.TiledAbovePixels` stays where it is (a Small picture made larger is 1.41 megapixels, well under it). A Small picture made larger is a row with a hires fix at 640 × 960: `BaseOf` must not take it for Fine (Fine is a hires fix at Normal's size), so size is read from the row's width and height first.

### 2. Chat and story pictures take their place from the setting

**Today (the cause).** The scene planner (`PictureWriter.SceneAsync`) gets the story's premise and World box under the heading **"Background, for understanding only (do not draw it)"** (`Illustrations.Background`). A passage that never says "forest" leaves the planner guessing the place, and it often guesses indoors. Character and group chats pass only up to six earlier messages (`ChatPictures.Background`); the character's Scenario reaches the planner only inside "Who these people are… use it for what they wear" (`PictureWriter.About`). The model gives at most four place words and `PicturePrompt` keeps three.

**The cause, corrected by the trial.** The heading is not it: when the World box names the place, today's planner draws it (forest, ship's deck, rainy city at night, tavern: every run), even for a passage that is only talk. Two other things are: (1) **the place was set only in earlier passages**, which the planner never sees for a story (it gets the premise and World box and nothing of what came before); (2) **in chats the Scenario is lost when the card's description is long**: it rides at the end of "who these people are", which is cut at 500 characters (`PictureWriter.MaxCastAboutLength`), after the description and personality. And with no place words in the prompt, the checkpoint draws a room.

**What changes** (in this order of worth).

- **What happened just before, for stories:** the planner is given the end of the passage before (as chats already pass up to six earlier messages), under "What happened just before (it tells where they are)". In the trial this alone turned a tavern and a bedroom into the forest campfire, 3 of 3.
- **Where the last picture was** is remembered with that picture (its place and light words, in the message's story details beside `picture`, through `StoryTurn`; no new table). When the newest passage does not say where it is, the planner is given "The last picture of this story took place in: forest, night, campfire. Stay there unless the passage or the setting says otherwise." (3 of 3 in the trial.) It covers a run of talk-only passages that the passage before does not.
- **The Scenario gets its own block, in full:** character chats pass the card's Scenario (and the chat's own summary) as the setting, no longer inside the 500-character "about"; group chats each member's Scenario. Stories pass the World box and premise under the same heading: "Setting: where the story takes place. It is to be drawn: take the place, the light and the weather from it, unless the passage clearly says this moment is somewhere else." (Trial: beach and dawn 3 of 3 where today gave a shore at sunset or under cloud.) The planner cuts its background at 1,500 characters today; the setting, what came before and the last place each need room of their own.
- **More place words: up to five kept** (the model may write six), three for light, checked against the tag list as today. The place line of the form says: the place itself first, then indoors or outdoors, then what is seen there; and take it from the setting when the passage does not say. Six in the trial gave "outdoors" or "indoors" almost every time and real detail (moss, fern, pebble), and in a drawn comparison did not crowd out the two people; it also gave things that are not places (mug, chart, potted plant) and repeated light words as place words, so code drops a place word that is also a light word. To watch in step 3: with six words the planner twice put a second person into a passage that had one.
- **The place shown before drawing:** when "Show me the prompt before drawing" is on, the review shows the place and light words on a line of their own, easy to correct.

**Must not change.** The owner's Looks are put in as typed; at most two people; the rating rules; nothing the planner reads is logged.

### 3. A thinking budget

**Today.** Thinking is on or off per kind of chat: General on, character and group chats off, stories off (`ChatOptions`, `StoryOptions`; owner's decision 2026-11-15). With it on, Huihui Qwen3.5 9B thinks for 2,500 to 4,000 tokens before answering, and a long think can use up the room and leave no answer (`ThinkingOnly`).

**What the engine offers (looked at 2026-11-15).** The chat engine on this PC (llama.cpp build 12314) has `--reasoning-budget N` (a limit on thinking tokens; -1 for none, 0 for none at all) and `--reasoning-budget-message` (words put in just before thinking is ended, e.g. "Time to answer."), plus `--reasoning-effort`. **Settled by the trial: the budget is given per request, on both engines** (the winget one, build 12314, and the one DOG.ai installed, build 12359). The request body takes `thinking_budget_tokens` (a number of thinking tokens; 0 is no thinking; left out is no limit) and, if wanted, `reasoning_budget_message`; nothing is reloaded. Both engines also end the thinking of a reply in progress on request: the reply is asked for with `reasoning_control: true`, and `POST /v1/chat/completions/control` with `{ "id": <the reply's id from the stream>, "action": "reasoning_end" }` makes the model answer at once. DOG.ai's own route (stop the request, ask again with the thinking so far closed) was tried and works too (the answer began 0.2 s into the second request), but it is not needed and is not built.

**What changes.**

- **A thinking level** in place of each on/off switch, for General, character and group chats, and stories: **Off, Short, Normal, Long, No limit**. The trial confirmed the token counts: **Short 512** (a reply in about 8 s), **Normal 1,536** (about 20 s), **Long 4,096** (up to about 50 s); Off stays the template switch it is today. Starting levels (owner's decision, 2026-11-15): General No limit; character chats, group chats and stories **Short**. On the first start after M18 the old switches are read once: General on → No limit, General off → Off; character and group chats and stories → Short whatever their switch said (they were off by default, so "off" cannot be told apart from untouched). Backups made before M18 restore the same way. Summaries and DOG.ai's own jobs still never think.
- **Answer now:** while a reply is thinking, a button beside Stop ends the thinking at once and makes the model answer, through the engine's own control route (every chat reply is asked for with `reasoning_control: true`; `LlamaApi` keeps the reply's id from the stream). Stop still stops everything.
- **A count while it thinks:** "Thinking… 1,200 / 1,536" in the reply as it streams. DOG.ai counts the thinking pieces itself (one piece is one token): the engine reports no thinking count of its own.
- **Notes:** a reply whose thinking was cut by the budget or by Answer now shows that in small type under its thinking. The engine puts **no mark in the stream** at a cut (the thinking simply ends and the answer begins), so "cut by the budget" is told from the count: a 512 budget ended at 489 to 493 pieces, 1,536 at 1,487 to 1,504, 4,096 at 4,013 (within about 100 of the budget). `ThinkingOnly` keeps telling stopped, out of room and ended by itself apart, and learns "cut by the budget" (no answer after the cut would be a model that ignores the closing).
- **No budget message.** The engine can add words at the cut ("I will write the reply now."); the trial saw no gain in chats and stories, and on a sums question the reply with the message was wrong where the plain cut was right (one sample). The thinking stays the model's own words.
- **An engine that does not know the fields** ignores them (an unknown field is not refused), so the thinking runs past the budget and Answer now gets no "success". DOG.ai then says under the reply that the chat engine needs updating in Models › Engines for thinking limits; nothing else is built for it.

### Decided here (say so to change one)

1. Small becomes the default for every new picture: Images, portraits, story and chat pictures, and the test picture.
2. The place in a picture follows the story's world and the character's setting unless the passage says otherwise.
3. **Thinking levels start at:** General No limit; character chats, group chats and stories Short (owner's decision, 2026-11-15: "thinking is important to the character and story mode").
4. *From the trial (2026-11-15):* Small is 640 × 960 and Make larger is ×1.5, or ×1.25 when only that fits beside the loaded chat model (**both confirmed by the owner, 2026-11-15**); no words are added at a thinking cut; an engine too old for the thinking fields gets a note to update it and no route of DOG.ai's own (**both confirmed by the owner, 2026-11-15, before step 4**).
5. The Images section forgets a size remembered before M18 once (owner's decision, 2026-11-15): every save there wrote the size, chosen or not.

### Trial results (step 1, 2026-11-15)

Run by hand, outside DOG.ai, with hard links in a throwaway folder on D: (removed afterwards; the owner's models and engine folders are byte for byte as before). Chat model: Huihui Qwen3.5 9B abliterated Q8, about 85 tokens a second. Checkpoint: `illustrious_novaFurryXL_ilV180A` with the Illustrious defaults (Euler, 28 steps, guidance 5.5). Picture engine `master-1104-5dcfa79`. Three scratch programs (thinking in Python against the engine's own address; sizes and place in C# against `DogAI.Core` and `DogAI.Engine`); none is in the repo.

**Sizes** (104 pictures drawn; 14 contact sheets looked at, with crops of faces and hands).

| Size (tall) | Megapixels | Seconds | Card at the peak, above the idle picture model | Look |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 576 × 864 (drawn as 576 × 896) | 0.52 | 3.4 | about 1.8 GB | Layout, place and two people fine; on full-body pictures a smeared eye and a hand with extra fingers. Too small. |
| **640 × 960** | 0.61 | 4.0 | 2.2 GB | Good: faces a little simpler on a full-body picture, hands right, two people kept apart with the right hair and clothes, places as good as larger. |
| 704 × 1056 (drawn as 704 × 1088) | 0.77 | 4.8 | 2.7 GB | Good, hard to tell from Normal; saves little. |
| 832 × 1216 (Normal) | 1.01 | 6.6 | 3.6 GB | Today's picture. |

- Wide and square sizes of the same area took the same seconds and memory and looked the same (864 × 576, 960 × 640, 1056 × 704, 1216 × 832; 704, 768, 864, 1024 square).
- **The engine rounds each side up to a multiple of 64** (576 × 864 came out 576 × 896, 864 square came out 896). Small's three sizes are multiples of 64, so they come out as asked.
- The idle picture model holds 7.0 GB. With Windows' own 1.6 GB the card peaked at 11.0 GB for Small and 12.5 GB for Normal (GB here are gibibytes, as in the app). `PictureMemory.Need` says 10.5 and 12.1 GB, about 1 GB above what was measured (9.5 and 10.9): left as it is.
- **The same seed at another size is another picture** (other pose, other framing), so a Small picture cannot be "drawn again at Normal": Make larger has to be the hires fix.
- **Made larger** (Small with Fine's second pass: 14 steps, strength 0.3, Lanczos; the whole redraw from the seed):

| | Comes out | Seconds | Card at the peak (with Windows) | Look |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| ×1.25 | 832 × 1216 (exactly Normal) | 7.4 | 12.5 to 13.2 GB | Same layout; sharper than Small, a little softer than a picture drawn at Normal. |
| ×1.3 | 832 × 1280 | 7.6 | 12.8 to 13.4 GB | An odd shape (the engine's rounding); dropped. |
| **×1.5** | 960 × 1472 | 8.7 | 14.2 to 14.6 GB | Same layout; as good as or better than a picture drawn at Normal. Small things change as with Fine (a mouth opened). |
| Normal, drawn plain | 832 × 1216 | 6.6 | 12.5 to 13.2 GB | (another picture: other pose) |
| Fine | 1280 × 1856 | 15.4 | 18.1 GB | Today's Fine. |

- **Beside the chat model** (Huihui Q8 at 8,192 tokens holds 8.6 GB; drawn for real with both loaded): Small peaked at 19.5 GB of 24.0 and Normal at 21.1 GB, same seconds as alone, and the chat model answered afterwards at its usual speed. By DOG.ai's rule (need + 1 GB margin against 13.8 GB free) Small fits with 2.2 GB to spare and Normal with 0.65 GB: Normal is the one that tips over when the document search model or a speech model is also on the card, or with a chat model holding more than about 9.3 GB; Small fits up to about 10.9 GB. Small made larger ×1.5 (13.7 GB by the rule) does not fit beside Huihui Q8; ×1.25 does.

**Place** (the scene planner's form filled 132 times: 12 cases, three seeds, up to five wordings; 2 to 5 s each).

- **World box names the place, passage does not** (forest, ship, city at night, tavern; also a forest passage that is only talk): today's wording gave the right place every time (forest + fog/moss; ship deck; alley, street, night, rain, neon; tavern, candlelight). The new heading changed little.
- **Place only in the passage before, World box empty** (two people talk and read a letter; the passage before put them at a campfire in the forest at night): today → garden bench, tavern, bedroom. New wording alone → indoors 3 of 3. **With the passage before → forest, campfire, night 3 of 3. With the last picture's place → 3 of 3.**
- **Character chat, long card** (description of 650 characters, so the Scenario falls outside the 500 the planner reads): today → "rocky shore", "steps", light sunset or overcast (guessed from the word "tide"). **Scenario in its own block → beach, dawn 3 of 3.** With a short card today's wording already gave beach and dawn.
- **The passage names another place** (a woodcutter's cabin in the forest story, the captain's cabin on the ship, the lighthouse kitchen in the beach chat): the passage won every time, in every wording. With the new wording the light the passage does not give is taken from the setting (dawn in the kitchen).
- **Six place words against three:** six almost always include "outdoors" or "indoors" and add real detail (moss, fern, pebble, foam); they also bring things that are not places (mug, chart, potted plant, mushroom) and light words as place words (night, moonlight), and in one case two of three runs added a person the passage did not have. Drawn by hand at 960 × 640, two seeds: **no place words → a room, both times**; "forest, night, campfire" → the forest campfire; eight place and light words → the same with moss and ferns, the two people unharmed.

**Thinking** (both engines gave the same results; times are from the winget one at 16,384 tokens of context).

- **Body fields that work:** `thinking_budget_tokens` (and its other name `reasoning_budget_tokens`); `reasoning_budget_message`; `reasoning_control`. **Ignored without an error:** `reasoning_budget`, `thinking: { budget_tokens }`, `reasoning_effort` (this model's template has no effort levels).
- **A cut in the stream:** the `reasoning_content` pieces stop (after the message's words when one is set) and `content` begins; no field marks it, the finish reason is "stop" as always, and the usage figures do not count thinking apart.
- **The control route:** `{"success":true}` and the answer began 0.05 s later; without `reasoning_control` on the reply `{"success":false,"message":"reasoning control not enabled for this completion"}`; a finished or unknown id `{"success":false,"message":"no active completion for this id"}`; an unknown action is refused with 400.
- **DOG.ai's own route** (stop at 512 thinking pieces, ask again with an assistant turn that holds the thinking so far and `</think>`): the second request read about 550 tokens again and began its answer in 0.15 to 0.2 s; replies as good as the engine's own cut. Not needed.
- **Seconds** (whole reply; in brackets the thinking tokens):

| | No limit | 4,096 | 1,536 | 512 | Off |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Character chat, turn 1 | 33.6 (2,641) | 33.9 (2,641) | 20.2 (1,502) | 8.3 (492) | 2.7 |
| Character chat, turn 2 | 46.8 (3,779) | 46.8 (3,779) | 19.6 (1,504) | 7.8 (493) | 2.0 |
| Character chat, turn 3 | 35.1 (2,778) | 34.8 (2,778) | 19.6 (1,487) | 7.7 (492) | 2.4 |
| Story passage (about 200 words) | 82.7, **no passage** (6,846, stopped by the trial's limit of 7,000) | 51.6 (4,013) | 21.5 (1,497) | 9.6 (489) | 3.9 |

- The answer begins about 6 s after sending at 512, 18 s at 1,536, 48 s at 4,096. 4,096 is reached only by stories here.
- **After a cut the answers were good:** in character, in the asked form (two or three short paragraphs; a passage of about 200 words ending on a decision), and often the very draft the thinking had reached. Read side by side, 512 was not worse than no limit; with no limit the story model fell to counting the words of its draft and never wrote the passage.
- With the budget message the replies were no better; on a sums question the reply with the message gave a wrong number where the plain cut gave the steps and the right one (one sample).

### Steps, and which model to use for each

One step per session, each ending in a build, passing tests, a look at the app, this plan's Status updated and a commit, then the hand-over.

| # | Step | Delivers | Model / effort |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Trial** (no app code). **Done 2026-11-15**: see "Trial results" above | By hand, in a scratch folder, with hard links to the owner's models. **Sizes:** draw the same three prompts and seeds (one person tall, two people wide, a place with nobody) with the Illustrious checkpoint at 576 × 864, 640 × 960, 704 × 1056 and 832 × 1216, and the square and wide sizes of the same area; seconds, graphics memory, and a look at each (faces, hands, two people kept apart); then the chosen small size made larger (hires fix ×1.3 and ×1.5) beside a plain Normal picture: same layout, as good? Whether Small fits beside Huihui Q8 loaded where Normal does not. **Place:** `PictureWriterSmokeTests` (or a scratch program) with five passages that do not name their place (a forest story, a ship, a city at night, a tavern, a character chat whose Scenario sets the place), with today's wording and the new one: place words, whether the passage still wins when it does name a place, six place words against three. **Thinking:** on the winget llama-server and on the one DOG.ai installs, whether the budget and its message can be given per request (try the body fields the engine accepts; read its source for the names if needed), what a cut looks like in the stream, whether the answer after a cut is good; the fallback route (stop, ask again with the thinking closed) with Huihui Qwen3.5 9B; seconds saved at 512, 1,536 and 4,096 against no limit on three character-chat turns and one story passage. Ends with the numbers, the route for the budget, and steps 2 to 5 corrected here. | Fable 5.2, high |
| 2 | **Picture sizes**. **Done 2026-11-15** (see Status) | `PictureSize` (Small, Normal) in Core beside quality and shape: Small 640 × 960, 960 × 640, 768 × 768 (every side a multiple of 64; a test checks); `At(quality, shape, size)`; `Adopt`/`BaseOf` read the size from the row's width and height first, so a Small picture made larger (a hires fix at 640 × 960) is not taken for Fine; Make larger in the picture maker (Small: ×1.5 with Fine's second pass, or ×1.25 when only that fits beside the loaded chat model; Normal: Fine); the fits-beside rule (Normal in Settings but only Small fits beside the chat model: start Small and say so); Images: Small in `StartFor`, Make larger from a try (Redraw larger; `RedrawSizes` already offers ×1.5 of the size first drawn at); Settings › Pictures "Start pictures at"; the test picture. Tests: a kept Normal and a kept Fine picture still redraw byte for byte; Try again keeps the size; a Small picture made larger and kept comes back as Small made larger. In the app: a portrait, a story picture and Images, all three looks, dark and light; a Small picture and Make larger with Huihui Q8 loaded (the chat model stays for Small). | Opus 5.5, high |
| 3 | **Settings in pictures**. **Done 2026-11-15** (see Status) | In the order the trial found worth: (1) stories pass the end of the passage before ("What happened just before"); (2) the last picture's place and light kept with it (`StoryTurn`) and passed on; (3) the Scenario in its own block, in full, no longer inside the 500-character "about" (character chats, with the chat's summary; group chats each member's), stories' World and premise under the same "Setting… it is to be drawn" heading, each block with room of its own beside the 1,500-character background; (4) the place line of the form (place first, then indoors or outdoors, then what is seen; from the setting when the passage does not say), up to five place words kept and three for light, a place word that is also a light word dropped; (5) the place line in the review. Tests with the fake engine for what the planner is given; the smoke test with the trial's cases: place only in the passage before (forest campfire), long card with a beach Scenario (beach, dawn), a passage that moves indoors (indoors), and that nobody the passage does not show is added. In the app: a story whose World box is empty, set in a forest by its first passage, a later talk-only passage illustrated (forest); a character chat with a long card whose Scenario sets the place. All three looks for the review. | Opus 5.5, high |
| 4 | **Thinking budget**. **Done 2026-11-15** (see Status) | The levels in Settings › Chats and stories (`ChatOptions`, `StoryOptions`; old switches read once as described above, characters and stories to Short; backups); Short 512, Normal 1,536, Long 4,096 sent per request as `thinking_budget_tokens` (`SamplerPreset`, `[JsonIgnore]` like `MaxTokens`; `LlamaApi.ChatRequestBody`); no budget message; `reasoning_control: true` on chat replies, the reply's id kept from the stream, and Answer now through `POST /v1/chat/completions/control` (`reasoning_end`) on the loopback client, behind `IChatEngine`; the count while thinking (thinking pieces counted in DOG.ai); the notes and `ThinkingOnly` ("cut by the budget" told from the count, within about 100 of the budget); the note for an engine that ignores the fields. DOG.ai's own jobs and summaries still never think. Tests with a fake engine that thinks forever. In the app with Huihui Qwen3.5 9B, with the winget engine and the installed one: General, a character chat, a group chat and a story at Short and No limit, Answer now in each; a story at No limit that never answers, ended with Answer now; all three looks, dark and light. | Fable 5.2, high |
| 5 | **Finish**. **Done 2026-11-16** (see Status) | `/code-review high` over M18; every changed screen in three looks, dark and light, and narrow; the published portable build; `CLAUDE.md` (sizes, the planner's setting, the thinking budget); this plan's Status. | Opus 5.5, high |

**Checks that decide whether it is done:** a new picture anywhere starts Small and Make larger keeps its layout; every kept picture still redraws byte for byte; a forest story's passage that does not say "forest" gets a forest picture, also when only an earlier passage said so; a chat with a long card gets its Scenario's place; a passage that moves indoors gets an indoor one; a reply at Short thinks no more than its budget and then answers; Answer now gives an answer within seconds; General chats behave exactly as before M18; character chats, group chats and stories think, within Short's budget, and still answer.

## M14 to M16: The owner's six ideas (planned 2026-11-15)

The owner's list, numbered in the order given:

1. Edit in the picture maker should let the owner keep or randomise the seed.
2. Clicking Settings a second time returns to the screen the owner was on (a chat, a story…); the same for Search.
3. A new section, Images, devoted to making pictures, with the feel of automatic1111.
4. An Unload button under "Graphics memory" that unloads all models.
5. Every model can be downloaded, even one that will not fit; models small enough to sit in memory beside the picture model are marked.
6. The document search model is unloaded when it is not being used.

Order: the two small ones first (M14), then the memory work (M15), because the Images section (M16) leans on both: it reuses the seed control and makes the card busier than any screen so far.

### M14: Quick fixes (ideas 1, 2)

1. **Seed in Edit and redraw.** Today Edit always keeps the seed and Try again always takes a new one (`PictureMaker.razor`). Edit gets a seed row: the number (editable), **Keep** (the default, as now) and **New seed**. Build it as a small shared component, since the Images section needs the same control. A kept picture redrawn with a new seed is a new try; the kept one is not touched until Use.
2. **Settings and Search go back.** The three shells link straight to `settings` and `search`. A small Core service remembers the last address that was neither; clicking the button while already there (any `settings/<category>`, or Search) returns to it, and with nothing remembered goes to the look's start page. The button's tooltip says "Back to …" while it would go back. Escape is left alone (dialogs and the drawer use it). Ctrl+K keeps working as now.

Checks: both in all three looks, dark and light; back from Settings into a chat, a group chat, a story, a character editor with unsaved typing (it must still be there, or the owner is warned as today), and Models.

### M15: Graphics memory and downloads (ideas 4, 5, 6)

1. **Unload everything** (`#unload-all`) under "Graphics memory" in the Models side panel: the chat model (through `ModelLauncher.UnloadByUserAsync`, so it is the owner's unload and nothing brings it back until asked), the picture model (`ImageServer`), the document search model (`EmbeddingServer`) and any speech model that holds graphics memory. It takes a card turn like every other change to the card; while a reply or a picture is being made it is disabled and says why. The readout updates afterwards.
2. **The document search model is let go sooner.** It already stops by itself, but only after 10 minutes unused (`EmbeddingServer.IdleStop`). New: 2 minutes, like the picture model; at once when the card needs the room (a chat model load or a picture that would not fit otherwise); and never while indexing or a search is running. The Documents side panel says whether it is in memory.
3. **Download anything.** `DownloadButton` is disabled for a model judged "Too large for this PC" (Recommended, Browse). It becomes enabled, with the warning kept visible beside it and one confirmation naming the size and why it will not run here. Loading such a model stays refused as today.
4. **"Fits beside pictures" mark.** A chat model whose need at the chosen context size, plus the picture model's need (`PictureMemory`, for the checkpoint in use at Standard), fits the card gets a chip on Recommended, Browse and On this PC › Chat models, with one line of explanation behind an ⓘ. Logic in Core with tests; "How fit is judged" in the side panel gains a line. No chip when there is no picture engine or checkpoint.

Checks: real models on a throwaway folder of hard links; graphics memory read before and after Unload everything; a picture drawn in a chat with a marked model (the chat model must not be set aside) and with an unmarked one (it must be).

### M16: Images section (idea 3). Design first

**What is settled already** (follows from M9, not up for choice): it draws through `CardCoordinator`; pictures that are tried stay in memory and only kept ones reach the disk, in the `Pictures` table (so they are encrypted with the lock, in backups, and follow a portable move); checkpoints, LoRAs, VAE and their settings come from `ImageModelCatalog`; prompts are never logged; all three looks, dark and light; a place in the navigation, in Ctrl+K and a colour in Playhouse.

**Controls every design has:** prompt, negative prompt, checkpoint, LoRAs with strengths, sampler, steps, guidance, width and height (with the Tall / Square / Wide shortcuts), seed (the M14 control), hires fix, how many pictures, Draw and Cancel, and for each result: keep, redraw, reuse its settings, save as a file.

**Four layouts to choose from:**

- **A. Classic.** The automatic1111 page: both prompt boxes across the top with Draw beside them; controls bottom left; the picture and a strip of tries bottom right; LoRAs as a fold under the prompts. Most familiar; the busiest screen in the app and the weakest in a narrow window.
- **B. Canvas.** The picture fills the page. The prompt boxes sit at the bottom where the chat box is; every control is in the side panel as folds (Model, LoRAs, Size, Sampling, Seed), which becomes the drawer below 1050 px. Uses the regions every look already has, so it needs the least new styling. Kept pictures are a second tab.
- **C. Sessions.** Like chats: the list holds picture sessions; the page is a feed of what each prompt produced, newest last; the prompt box is at the bottom and controls are in the side panel. Good for working on one idea over many tries; needs a new table for sessions, and decides what a "session" keeps.
- **D. Gallery first.** The section opens on a grid of kept pictures (filter by checkpoint, search the prompt). "New picture" or "Make one like this" opens the workspace (layout B) over it. Best if the point is the collection; one more click before drawing.

Recommended: **B, with D's gallery as its second tab.** It is the automatic1111 controls in this app's own layout, and the only one that works at every window width without new rules.

**To decide before the steps are planned:**

1. The layout (A to D, or a mix).
2. Prompts: typed raw as in automatic1111, or with the tag check offered (a "check my tags" button; never automatic).
3. Whether the rating words from Settings › Pictures are added here, or the prompt is sent exactly as typed.
4. A helper where the chat model turns a sentence into a prompt (the M9 planner), or none.
5. Several pictures per Draw: one after another with the tries strip, or a grid.
6. Kept pictures: only inside DOG.ai (encrypted with the lock), with "Save as a file" per picture, or also a folder DOG.ai writes to.
7. Later extras that would change the layout if wanted: image-to-image, inpainting, upscaling a kept picture.

**Decided by the owner (2026-11-15):**

- Point 1, the layout: **B, Canvas, with a Kept pictures tab** (chosen from wireframes of the four). Mock-up in the three looks, dark and light, both tabs: `docs/mockups/images-section.html` (open it in a browser; it is a drawing, not app code).
- Points 2 and 3, prompts: sent exactly as typed, as in automatic1111. A "Check my tags" button offers corrections; nothing is changed or added automatically, rating words included.
- Point 4: yes, a prompt helper (the chat model turns a sentence into a prompt the owner can then edit).
- Point 5: yes, several pictures per Draw, one after another into the tries strip.
- Point 6: kept pictures live only inside DOG.ai (the `Pictures` table), with "Save as a file" per picture. No output folder.
- Point 7: upscaling a kept picture is in from the start. Image-to-image and inpainting are not.

**The screen (layout B).** Address `images` (Make) and `images/kept`; a fifth entry in the navigation between Story and Models, in Ctrl+K, on Playhouse's home screen, with its own Playhouse colour (`--c-images`, amber in the mock-up).

- *Make.* The picture fills the page with the strip of tries beside it; under it one line of facts (size, seed, sampler, steps, time) and **Keep**, **Use these settings**, **Upscale…**, **Save as a file…**. The prompt and negative prompt boxes sit where the chat box is, with **Write it for me…** (the prompt helper), **Check my tags**, **Cancel** and **Draw**. The header has the two tabs, the checkpoint button and the side panel button.
- *Side panel* (`AsideRegion`, folds with one-line summaries, a drawer below 1050 px): Model (checkpoint, VAE), LoRAs (tick and strength; a LoRA's trigger words shown as chips that insert into the prompt on a click, never by themselves), Size (Tall / Square / Wide and width, height), Sampling (sampler, scheduler, steps, guidance, clip skip), Seed (the M14 control), Hires fix, Pictures per Draw (1 to 8, `ImageRequest.MaxCount`).
- *Kept pictures.* A grid, newest first, with a box that finds words in the prompt and filters for checkpoint and shape. A picture opens large with **Make one like this** (its settings and seed go to Make), **Upscale…**, **Save as a file…** and **Remove**.

**Rules the steps follow** (defaults chosen here; say so to change one):

- A new control starts from the checkpoint's own settings card and is then remembered (in `InterfaceMemory`'s way: one JSON document of its own, not `AppSettings`). **The prompt boxes are not remembered across a restart**: a prompt reaches the disk only inside a kept picture's row, the M9 rule.
- **Saved prompts, so common ones are not typed again** (owner asked, 2026-11-15; like automatic1111's styles). A saved prompt is a name with a prompt and a negative prompt, kept only because the owner pressed Save: one JSON document in the settings store (inside the database, under the lock, in backups), edited like the chat prompt library. A **Saved prompts** button beside the boxes lists them; one click puts a saved prompt in (**Replace**) or adds it to what is typed (**Add**), and **Save these as…** stores what is in the boxes now. One of them can be marked **Start with this**, and the boxes then start filled with it after a restart. With none marked, the boxes start with the chosen checkpoint's own quality words and negative prompt from its settings card (`QualityBefore`, `QualityAfter`, `Negative`), as visible, editable text. Storage and logic in step 1; the button and list in step 2.
- Kept pictures get a new owner kind (`PictureOwners.Images`) with no one-row limit. The tab lists only these; portraits and story or chat pictures stay where they are.
- The grid needs small previews. They are made in memory and stored in the picture's own row (a new column, so inside the database and under the lock), never as files.
- Nothing is added to what is typed: no quality words, no rating words, no LoRA triggers. `PictureLogFilter.Sending` is still told before every request, and everything draws through `CardCoordinator`.
- Save as a file goes through `IDesktopShell.SaveFileAsync`, so the one-time plain-export note shows while locked.

### M16 steps, and which model to use for each

One step per session, each ending in a build, passing tests, a look at the app, this plan's Status updated and a commit, then the hand-over. M14 and M15 come first.

| # | Step | Delivers | Model / effort |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Core and storage** | The raw request (typed values to `ImageRequest`, nothing added), the remembered controls, the `Images` owner kind, the preview column and its migration, listing and searching kept pictures a page at a time. Tests; lock, backup, restore and portable checks for the new column; removed-data check. No screen yet. | Fable 5.2, high |
| 2 | **Make** | The section, navigation, Ctrl+K, Playhouse colour; the Make tab with every control, Draw and Cancel, progress, several pictures per Draw, the tries strip (`PictureTries`), Use these settings, Keep. Real pictures drawn. All three looks, dark and light; the drawer at 700 and 1000 px. | Opus 5.5, high |
| 3 | **Kept pictures** | The grid with previews, find and filters, the large view, Make one like this, Save as a file, Remove. All three looks, dark and light. | Opus 5.5, high |
| 4 | **Helpers** | Write it for me (a DOG.ai model job like the planner: schema, no thinking, answer checked, result put in the box for the owner to edit; the chat model comes back through `EnsureReadyAsync`), Check my tags (`TagList.Current`; offers each change, applies only what is accepted), LoRA trigger chips. | Opus 5.5, high |
| 5 | **Upscale** | A trial first: what the picture engine can do to an existing picture (an upscaler file such as ESRGAN, or only a hires redraw from the seed), and what each costs in graphics memory and time. Then Upscale… on a try and on a kept picture, kept as a new picture beside the original. `PictureMemory` taught the larger sizes. | Fable 5.2, high |
| 6 | **Finish** | Leak check as in M9 step 9 (marker words in prompts and negative prompts, tried and kept, plain and locked), the published portable build, `/code-review high`, every Images screen in three looks dark and light and narrow, `CLAUDE.md` ("How the Images section works"). | Fable 5.2, xhigh |

Why this split: steps 1, 5 and 6 are where a wrong judgement costs most (private pictures and prompts on disk, what the engine can really do, the last look). Steps 2 to 4 reuse what M9 built (the picture maker, `MessagePictures`, the planner, the tag list).

### Sessions, and which model to use (M14 to M16)

| # | Milestone | Model / effort |
|---|---|---|
| M14 | Quick fixes | Opus 5.5, high |
| M15 | Graphics memory and downloads | Fable 5.2, high |
| M16 | Images section | Six steps, each with its own; see "M16 steps" |

Why: M14 repeats patterns the code has. M15 changes who holds the graphics card and when, where a wrong judgement leaves a model stuck or a picture waiting.

## M10 to M13: A tidier interface (planned 2026-11-14; all four done 2026-11-14)

**Why.** Every milestone added controls and none took any away. A review of the running app (Workbench dark, an empty data folder, window 1400 × 860; the other looks and chats with content were read in code only) found:

| Screen | Height against the window | Controls on the page |
|---|---|---|
| Settings | 2.5 screens | 30 |
| Models, Images | 2.5 screens | 28 |
| New character | 2.3 screens | 30 |
| New story | 1.6 screens | 22 |

Also: the Models side panel shows the same five panels on all seven tabs; hardware is shown twice on Recommended; a character chat stacks about seven always-open side panels; nearly every setting carries a two- or three-line paragraph; the picture checkpoint is chosen in two places (Settings and Models, Images).

**The ten ideas**, numbered as they were put to the owner:

1. A default model per job: one block of dropdowns (General, Characters, Story, Document search, Pictures), each with "No default" ("Pick automatically" for document search), in place of the "Default for…" menu on every model.
2. Settings in categories (Appearance, Chats and stories, Voice, Pictures, Privacy, Windows, Backup, About) with a side list and a search box.
3. The Models side panel shows only what fits the tab; hardware once; one Connection spot for the online note and the Hugging Face token.
4. Shorter help text: one line under a control, the rest behind an info button.
5. Model cards with one main button (Load); Benchmark, Delete and the file path in a "⋯" menu.
6. Collapsible chat side panels with a one-line summary each ("Style · Balanced"), remembered, and a button to hide the whole panel.
7. Character and story editors in tabs (Basics, Personality and scenario, Messages, Looks and voice, Advanced) with a Save bar that stays visible.
8. Models, Images split up: Checkpoints, LoRAs, Tags, and the test picture folded away as a diagnostic.
9. Hide what cannot work yet: the composer's microphone, hands-free and read-aloud buttons appear once a voice model exists.
10. Models regrouped (Get models; On this PC; Engines) with downloads as a tray button in the header; and Ctrl+K to jump to any chat, character, story or setting.

### M10: Defaults and Settings (ideas 1, 2, 4 for Settings, 5)

1. **Shared pieces first.** A collapsible section that remembers open or closed, a "⋯" menu, an info button for long help text, and page tabs. Built once and checked in every look, so later steps only reuse them. What is remembered (which sections are closed, the last Settings category) is kept in the settings store, with its logic in Core so it can be tested.
2. **Default model per job.** The block at the top of Models, Library. It writes the same `AppSettings.DefaultModels` the menus write today; the Pictures row writes `ImageModelCatalog.Chosen`. Each model keeps a chip saying what it is the default for.
3. **Model cards.** Load stays; the rest moves into "⋯".
4. **Settings in categories**, with search and trimmed help text. The ids the walkthroughs use must keep working.

### M11: Models section (ideas 3, 8, and the regrouping and tray of 10)

1. Side panel per tab; hardware once; the Connection spot.
2. Three groups in place of seven tabs. Old addresses (`models/images` and the rest) keep working.
3. Downloads tray in the header.
4. Images split into sub-tabs.

The riskiest milestone: about 49 messages and links in 35 files name a place such as "Models › Images", some of them error messages in Core and Engine. Each is reworded when its target moves, and `CLAUDE.md` with them.

### M12: Chats and editors (ideas 6, 7, 9)

1. Collapsible side panels and the hide button, in General, Characters, group chats and Story.
2. Voice buttons only when a voice model exists.
3. Editors in tabs with a Save bar. All tabs stay in one form (hidden, not removed), so switching tabs never loses typing; a problem found on saving opens the tab it is on.

### M13: Quick-jump and final pass (Ctrl+K of idea 10, the rest of 4)

1. Ctrl+K, built on the existing Search: an index in Core with tests; the box sits inside `.shell`; the key is caught in the page and in `MainWindow`, as Ctrl+L is.
2. Help text trimmed on the screens not yet touched.
3. Every screen looked at in three looks, dark and light; the portable build; `CLAUDE.md` brought up to date.

### Sessions, and which model to use for each

One milestone per session. Each ends in a build, passing tests, a look at the app in three looks (dark and light), this plan's Status updated, and a commit. Then the session stops and hands over (owner's instruction, 2026-11-14; the rule is in `CLAUDE.md`): it tells the owner to start a new session, names the model and effort from this table, and gives the prompt to paste into it. "Effort" is the session's effort setting.

| # | Milestone | Model / effort |
|---|---|---|
| M10 | Defaults and Settings | Opus 5.5, high |
| M11 | Models section | Fable 5.2, high |
| M12 | Chats and editors | Opus 5.5, high |
| M13 | Quick-jump and final pass | Fable 5.2, high |

Why this split: M11 moves things that about 49 messages and links point at, next to the only code that goes online, and M13 is the last look at everything, so a wrong judgement costs most there. M10 and M12 mostly repeat patterns the code already has (the Models side list, `Modal`, the existing panels and editors), so Opus 5.5 is enough and cheaper.

### For the owner to decide (asked 2026-11-14)

- **Where the picture engine's install lives. Decided (owner, 2026-11-14): under Engines**, beside the chat engine. Done in M11; the picture tabs show a note with a link while it is missing.
- **Where the context size is set. Decided (owner, 2026-11-14): in the defaults block.** Done in M10: Models → Library → Default models; the side panel only says which size the fit badges use.
- **Pictures with nothing chosen. Decided (owner, 2026-11-14): "Pick automatically"**, like Document search: DOG.ai draws with the first checkpoint found, as before.

## M9: Pictures (planned 2026-11-13; built 2026-11-13 and 14, all nine steps done)

DOG.ai draws pictures on this PC with **stable-diffusion.cpp**, a sibling of llama.cpp, run the same way as the chat engine: a child process on `127.0.0.1` that DOG.ai starts and stops. Nothing goes online to draw.

**Decisions the owner made (2026-11-13):**
- stable-diffusion.cpp as a second engine (not ComfyUI, not ONNX, not an online service).
- First version supports the **SDXL family only** (Illustrious, NoobAI, Pony, base SDXL): one checkpoint file each, plus LoRAs.
- Pictures are made in two places: **character and persona portraits** and **story illustrations**. A picture button in chats and a standalone Images screen are later steps.
- **Civitai files are added by hand** (downloaded in a browser, dropped into the image models folder). **Hugging Face downloads happen in the app**, as for chat models. Civitai is not added to the allow-list.
- After the chat model writes a picture prompt, DOG.ai **draws at once** by default; a switch in Settings makes it **show the prompt first**.
- The owner supplies an Illustrious checkpoint and LoRAs for the trial.
- LoRAs can be **character LoRAs as well as style LoRAs**: a card can carry its own, and a checkpoint can have style LoRAs that apply to every picture.
- Default shapes: **Tall** for portraits, **Wide** for story illustrations.
- **No automatic illustrations**; pictures are made only when asked for.
- A story picture shows **at most two or three people**.
- Prompt wording follows the checkpoint, with no switch to flip per picture: **Danbooru-style tags** for Illustrious, NoobAI and Pony, sentences for base SDXL. The checkpoint's quality words ("masterpiece, best quality…", Pony's score tags) and negative prompt are added by DOG.ai, never left to the chat model.

**What was read about the engine (2026-11-13, from its documentation, not yet run here):**
- It ships a server program, `sd-server.exe`, with its own API: start a picture (returns a job), ask a job for its progress and a small preview, cancel a job, list what it can do, load and unload a model without exiting. Pictures come back in the reply, not as files; PNG, JPEG or WebP.
- LoRAs are given per picture as a list (file and strength). `<lora:...>` tags typed in a prompt are **not** read by the server, so DOG.ai picks LoRAs in its own controls.
- Per picture: clip skip, sampler, steps, "hires fix" (draw, enlarge, refine). Per loaded model: the VAE. A face-detail pass is mentioned but not documented. V-prediction is not in the server's documentation: **the trial must settle it.**
- Windows downloads are a CUDA 12 zip plus a separate zip of NVIDIA runtime files. MIT licence.
- The server has a built-in web page. It will listen only on `127.0.0.1` on a random port, like llama-server.

### Trial results (step 1, 2026-11-13)

Run by hand on this PC, outside DOG.ai: engine build `master-1094-3bad892` (CUDA 12 zip plus the NVIDIA runtime zip, both SHA-256 checked against the release page; about 1.2 GB unpacked, most of it NVIDIA libraries), the owner's checkpoint `illustrious_novaFurryXL_ilV180A` (6.6 GB, recognised as SDXL, eps-prediction) and three LoRAs (detail, style, character; all plain LoRAs of 218 MB). 25 pictures drawn; seven looked at.

- **It works, and the pictures are good.** The first try (tags: silver hair, green eyes, scar on cheek, leather armor, green cloak, forest, dappled sunlight) showed every tag, with no black-picture VAE problem and no extra VAE file needed.
- **Speed (832×1216):** Draft (20 steps) 5 s; Standard (28 steps) 6.5 s; Fine (28 steps, hires fix ×1.5 with 14 more steps) 14 to 15 s, giving 1248×1824. About 5 steps a second. The very first picture after a start takes about 4 s longer (the model is read into graphics memory then, not at start, so the server itself is up in under a second). Four pictures in one request: 25 s.
- **Graphics memory (above what Windows already uses):** about 8 GB held while the model is loaded and idle, about 11.5 GB at the peak of a Standard picture, about 17 GB at the peak of a Fine one. Unloading through the API gives it all back without stopping the server.
- **Beside the chat model: no.** The owner's 27B chat model is a 16.8 GB file, so the two cannot both be on the card; this is arithmetic, not a test (the test was not run). The swap in "Sharing the graphics card" is needed every time with this chat model, and Fine quality needs the card nearly to itself. *(Step 3 ran it: the 27B holds 17 GB and is set aside for every picture; a 7 GB chat model stays loaded beside the picture model, the card peaking at 20.1 GB.)*
- **LoRAs:** each worked alone and all three together. The character LoRA with its trigger word gave the same character in two shapes and seeds; without the LoRA the same prompt gave a generic one. Changing the LoRA set costs about 3 s on the next picture (about 10 s for three at once); going back to none also works. Trigger words and the most common training tags can be read from the files' own metadata.
- **Cancel** stopped a 60-step picture within about 0.3 s, and the next picture was normal. **Preview** ("proj" mode needs no extra file) gave a new small picture every few steps. Progress reports the stage (conditioning, sampling, decoding, encoding) and the step.
- **Hires fix** makes a visibly sharper picture but can change small things (it drew a second scar). So Fine is for a picture already chosen, not for trying ideas.
- **Picture size on disk:** PNG about 1.7 MB (3.6 MB for Fine), JPEG at 92 about 255 KB, WebP at 90 about 128 KB. Decision for storage: **WebP**, with the quality number fixed in step 5 after comparing by eye (not compared closely yet). *(Step 5 compared them: quality 90; see Status.)*
- **Privacy:** the server wrote **no file** to its working folder or its temp folder in the whole run, and at its normal log level the prompt does **not** appear in its output (model path, timings and step counters do). Its verbose level was not tried and must never be switched on by DOG.ai. `--disable-image-metadata` exists, so the prompt need not be embedded in a picture that gets exported.
- **V-prediction:** the engine has a load option for it (`--prediction v`). Not tried: the owner's checkpoint is the normal kind. Needs a v-prediction checkpoint to confirm.
- **More than planned is there:** a face-detail pass (needs one small extra detection model), reference-picture control for SDXL (IP-Adapter), image-to-image and inpainting. None are in M9, but they are no longer blocked by the engine.
- **Not tried:** drawing while a chat model is loaded; loading a different checkpoint through the API (only unloading was tried; the first load was from the command line); a separate VAE file; LyCORIS-type LoRAs (none in the samples); face detail; verbose logging; the engine under DOG.ai's portable temp folder.
- **Suggested starting numbers for this family** (tuned in step 6: Euler instead of Euler a, the rest stayed; see Status): Euler a, guidance 5.5, clip skip 2, quality words "masterpiece, best quality, amazing quality, very aesthetic, absurdres", negative "worst quality, low quality, bad quality, lowres, bad anatomy, bad hands, jpeg artifacts, signature, watermark, text".

### How it will work

**Engine (`DogAI.Engine/Images`).** `ImageServer` follows `EmbeddingServer`: started the first time a picture is asked for, random loopback port, `EngineEnvironment.Clean`, `ProcessJob` so it dies with DOG.ai, stopped when idle. It is found by its own locator in `data\engine` (override `DOGAI_SD_SERVER` for tests), and Models' engine updater gets a second entry that downloads it from the project's GitHub releases (one new allowed path in `OriginPolicy`, checksum checked). *Built in step 2: it lives in `data\engine\images`; no `OriginPolicy` change was needed (GitHub's hosts were already allowed); a checkpoint is loaded by starting the program with it, because the API's load works only inside the server's own folder layout (see Status).* Core sees only an `IImageEngine` interface, so tests use a fake.

**Sharing the graphics card.** A 27B chat model and an SDXL model do not fit in 24 GB together. A small coordinator decides per picture: if both fit (measured free memory), keep both; otherwise the prompt is written first, the chat model is unloaded, the picture is drawn, and the chat model is loaded again **only when it is next needed** (not straight away, so "Try again" and a run of pictures cost one swap, not one each). The image model is let go after a short idle time. A chat model the owner unloaded by hand stays unloaded. *Built in step 3: the coordinator is the picture engine the app hands out; the chat model is "set aside" rather than unloaded, so chat pages keep working and the next message loads it again; a chat request during a picture waits for it; the picture model's idle time is 2 minutes (see Status).*

**Image models folder.** `<models>\image\checkpoints`, `\loras`, `\vae`, `\upscalers`. Only `.safetensors` is accepted; the old `.ckpt` format can carry program code and is refused with a plain message. DOG.ai reads each file's header to tell what it is (SDXL checkpoint, SDXL LoRA, something else) and shows files it cannot use as such.

**Models → Images tab.** Installed checkpoints and LoRAs, **Open folder** (for Civitai files), recommended downloads from Hugging Face, the image engine's install and update. Each checkpoint has a small settings card so pictures come out right without fiddling: prompt style (tags or sentences), the "quality" words it wants before and after a prompt, the negative prompt, sampler, steps, guidance, clip skip, normal or v-prediction, and its VAE. Known families get these filled in when the file is recognised; the owner can change them. Each LoRA has its trigger words (read from the file when it has them) and a default strength.

**Looks on a card.** Characters and personas get a **Looks** box: appearance tags ("silver hair, green eyes, scar on left cheek…"), and optionally a LoRA with its strength. The chat model can draft the tags from the card's description. Stored in the card's extra data so export and import keep it. This is what keeps a character recognisable from one picture to the next. *Built in step 6: in the card's extensions for characters, a column for personas; a checkpoint's style LoRAs are tick boxes in its settings (see Status).*

**Writing the prompt (`DogAI.Core/Images`).** A DOG.ai model job (schema-constrained, checked before use, thinking off) turns a card or a story passage into the pieces of a prompt in the checkpoint's style. The final prompt is assembled by code, not by the model: checkpoint quality words + the Looks of the people in the scene + scene tags + the checkpoint's negative prompt. For a story, the people are the passage's cast (`StoryPrompt.People`), at most two or three (SDXL handles more badly, and the plan says so in the app).

*Tag prompts.* For tag-style checkpoints the model does not write a free prompt. It first picks **one moment** of the passage that can be shown as a still picture, then fills named slots: who is in it (as a count, "1girl, 1boy"), clothing, expression, pose or action, place, time and light, framing ("upper body", "from above"). Code puts the slots in the order these checkpoints expect and adds each person's Looks from their card (so the model cannot change a character's hair colour). Every tag is checked against a **bundled list of real Danbooru tags** (shipped with DOG.ai, no lookup online): near misses are corrected to the real tag, unknown ones are dropped, and the prompt is kept short. Where the list comes from and its licence are settled in the trial. What this does well: one or two people, their looks, clothes, mood, place and light. What it does poorly, said plainly in the app: exact actions between people, one person's features leaking onto another, unusual objects, anything that depends on what happened earlier in the story. *Built in step 6: the list is the general and species tags of Danbooru and e621 (the owner chose the merged file), 33,057 tags from the a1111-sd-webui-tagcomplete project (MIT); framing is one of six sizes and one of five views (see Status).*

**Quality choices.** Three named levels in place of raw numbers: **Draft** (fast, for trying ideas), **Standard**, **Fine** (hires fix, and face detail if the trial shows it works). Shapes: Tall, Square, Wide, using SDXL's native sizes. A live preview and step count while drawing; **Cancel** works mid-picture. Every picture keeps its seed and settings, so **Edit and redraw** changes one thing and keeps the rest. Raw settings stay available behind "More". *Tuned in step 6: Draft is 20 of 28 steps, Fine enlarges 1.5 times with Lanczos and refines at strength 0.3, and Illustrious draws with Euler so that a Draft shows what the Standard picture will be. Face detail was not tried (it needs an extra model). The live preview, Cancel and Edit and redraw are the picture maker's (step 7); the levels and shapes are ready for it.*

**Portraits.** **Generate picture** in the character and persona editors opens the picture maker (shared component in `Components/Images/`): Draw, Try again (variations side by side, held in memory), Edit and redraw, **Use this picture**, which hands the result to the existing cropper. Nothing is saved until Use. *Built in step 7: Try again keeps the plan and takes a new seed (no chat model job); Edit and redraw changes the prompt, quality, shape or LoRAs and keeps the seed; the picture is kept (a `Pictures` row) only when the character or persona is saved, one per owner (see Status).*

**Story illustrations.** **Illustrate** in a passage's actions. The picture sits above the passage, opens large on click, and can be redrawn or removed. Which picture belongs to a passage is recorded through `StoryTurn`. HTML and EPUB exports include the pictures; Markdown does not. *Built in step 8: kept at Use (the passage already exists), one per passage; the EPUB carries JPEG copies, the web page the WebP itself (see Status).*

**Keeping pictures (and keeping them private).** Kept pictures go **inside the database** (a new `Pictures` table), so the lock, backups, restore and portable mode cover them with no new machinery. They are stored compressed (the trial compares PNG and WebP for size and looks); tried-and-discarded variations never touch the disk. The image engine is started so that it writes no picture, prompt or preview to disk, and DOG.ai's log never records a prompt (the engine prints prompts in its own output, so those lines are filtered). Drawing counts as "busy" for the idle lock. *Built in step 5: WebP at quality 90, made by the engine itself; the engine's own lines reach the log through a filter (it prints no prompt at its normal level, and the filter holds if a later version does); see Status.*

**Settings → Pictures.** Default checkpoint, default quality, "Show me the prompt before drawing" (off by default), and the **rating** pictures aim for (owner's decision 2026-11-13: a setting, not the chat model's choice). The rating tag is added by code, the tags for anything above the chosen rating go into the negative prompt, and the prompt job is told the rating so it picks a moment that fits. Which rating words each family uses (Illustrious, NoobAI, Pony differ) sits on the checkpoint's settings card. *Built in step 7: four levels; the settings live in the `image-models` document (see Status).*

### Steps, and which model to use for each

Each step is one session ending in a build, passing tests, a look at the app, and a commit. "Effort" is the session's effort setting.

| # | Step | Delivers | Model / effort |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 ✅ | **Trial** (no app code; results above) | The engine downloaded to a scratch folder and run by hand with the owner's checkpoint and LoRAs. Measured: load time, seconds per picture at each quality level, memory used, whether it fits beside the chat model, v-prediction, VAE, hires fix, face detail, LoRA formats, cancel, unload, PNG against WebP, and what the engine writes to disk and to its output. Ends with the open points in this plan settled and the three quality levels given real numbers. | Fable 5.2, high |
| 2 ✅ | **Engine** | Locator, download and update in Models, `ImageServer`, the API client, `IImageEngine`, tests with a fake and one real-engine smoke test behind an environment variable. | Opus 5.5, high |
| 3 ✅ | **Sharing the graphics card** | The coordinator between chat model and image model, with lazy reload; busy state for the idle lock. | Fable 5.2, high |
| 4 ✅ | **Models → Images** | Folders, file recognition, the Images tab, checkpoint and LoRA settings cards, Hugging Face downloads, Open folder. All three looks, dark and light. | Opus 5.5, medium |
| 5 ✅ | **Keeping pictures** | `Pictures` table and migration, lock, backup, restore and portable checks, log filtering, a first leak check. | Fable 5.2, high |
| 6 ✅ | **Prompts and quality** | Looks on cards, the prompt job, prompt assembly, quality levels tuned by looking at real pictures from the owner's checkpoint. | Fable 5.2, high |
| 7 ✅ | **Portraits** | The picture maker and Generate picture in both editors, Settings → Pictures. All three looks, dark and light. | Opus 5.5, high |
| 8 ✅ | **Story illustrations** | Illustrate on passages, the large view, exports. All three looks, dark and light. | Opus 5.5, high |
